campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:8: > - The Dock Ward is Waterdeep's port ward, a district of piers, shipyards, warehouses and tenements that holds, with the Field Ward, most of the city's poor. Xanathar's Guild treats it as its main surface territory, and Manshoon's Zhentarim fight a low-level street war against it here.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:14: > - Location journals: **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Xanathar Sewer Hideout** and **Sea Maidens Faire** apply. No other mapped site sits in this ward.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:35: - **Organizations:** Xanathar's Guild, Manshoon's Zhentarim, the Doom Raiders (Istrid Horn's warehouse, a Black Network safe house), Bregan D'aerthe (the Seven Masks Theater and the Faire's pier), the Harpers (Corene Wyldath and a chandler contact), the Emerald Enclave (Pier 17), the Order of the Gauntlet (the Chapel), the Dungsweepers' Guild, the Watch and the guilds of the sea trades.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:41: > - The ward is the front line of a street war between Xanathar's Guild and Manshoon's Zhentarim. It began when Manshoon's envoys came to Skullport three months before the fireball and Xanathar killed them. Brawls between the two are common, and the Watch is called to them often.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:52: > - Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim run the tavern as an informal ward headquarters, and Dragon Heist calls it Zhentarim-owned. The regulars are Splinter men on home ground, and they report to Kolat Towers, not to Davil Starsong.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:90: **Zhentarim Warehouse** — Warehouse, Candle Lane
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:91: - A warehouse at the back of a walled yard on Candle Lane, with painted-over windows and a black winged snake painted above the door handle. The gate is unlocked. The warehouse is mapped in **Zhentarim Warehouse**.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:97: > - The Zhentarim cell that ran the warehouse died in a Xanathar's Guild raid. The kenku are the Guild's, left to kill any Zhentarim who return. A cellar trapdoor leads down to the sewer route to the Guild hideout.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:145: > - Istrid likes having people in her debt and treats every loan as leverage. Emmek Frewn owes her 150 gp, and Zhentarim thugs collect it.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:211: - Captain Staget is Dragon Heist's Dock Ward Watch captain. He is an uptight man who does not drink or believe rumors, and he lets the Guild and the Zhentarim wear each other down because he sees no reason to stop them. No campaign event uses him, though **The City Watch** describes him and his replacement.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:217: - The Guild and the Zhentarim fight for the streets, and the Watch arrives late and often. In **Finding Floon**, Krentz's crew killed a Doom Raiders operative in the ward two nights before the party arrives, and the Splinter's thugs are under arrest at a crime scene on the way to the Skewered Dragon.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/dock-ward.md:254: **Tone:** A rough working harbor, played gritty and a little weary. Danger comes from particular gangs and particular alleys, and the people who live here are tired and decent. Keep the Guild, Zhentarim and Bregan D'aerthe business in the Secret and GM Notes callouts.

campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/southern-ward.md:31: - **Architecture:** Homes and businesses stand above stables or around inn yards, near where wagon trains are housed (Dragon Heist). Most buildings are old multi-story tenements with shops on the ground floor, and dust and mud are common in the streets (wiki). The Jade Dancer has three stories with balconies, and Kolat Towers is a dilapidated pair of towers.

campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/southern-ward.md:148: **Manshoon (Lawful Evil, Human, he/him)**
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/southern-ward.md:149: - A clone of the Zhentarim's founder, a wizard who lost his left arm to Halaster Blackcloak and wears a crafted replacement. The Zhentarim operatives on the city's streets answer to him, and none of them will say so. He leads the Splinter cell, which is **Manshoon's Zhentarim** and not the Doom Raiders.

The published adventure says Southern Ward in the Kolat Towers chapter (`sources/adventure-wdh.json:26797`, "stands tall in the Southern Ward") and in the character appendix (`:32215` and `:32218`), and says Trades Ward in its Manshoon summary (`:560`). The campaign now says Southern Ward in `campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md` (lines 9, 33, 62, 233, 269, 316, 324-326), `campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:73` and `campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/08-notable-patrons.md:57`, and `campaign/setting/waterdeep-lore.md` is deleted. This page treats the towers as the Southern Ward's. The wiki bundles do not mention Kolat Towers, so the wiki neither agrees nor disagrees, and the Southern Ward bundle has no Kolat line.

campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:1: # The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:3: Game mechanics for running the Doom Raiders (Davil Starsong's Zhentarim cell) as a player faction. For who they are, see the **Doom Raiders** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:7: The Doom Raiders are the legitimate Black Network in Waterdeep, and the faction is at war with itself. Davil's cell arrived in Waterdeep first. Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter came later as a rival cell, peeled off part of that network, and now works to dismantle Davil's people and everything they built. This internal conflict is the Doom Raiders' defining preoccupation. The Grand Game is secondary.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:9: The half-million dragons are another matter. Efforts are underway to reclaim and rebuild Zhentil Keep, and the reforming Zhentarim Council is choosing its membership. That money could buy Davil's cell a seat at that table — genuine continental influence rather than the precarious position of a regional branch operation. Characters who recover the gold and deliver it to the Doom Raiders are buying their patrons into something much larger than one campaign.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:11: The Doom Raiders do not tell the party until Mission 4 forces the truth out. **Skeemo Weirdbottle has been a traitor from the start.** The Master of Magic has been feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell and to House Gralhund since before the party was recruited. Everything the party has done for the faction has been visible to the Splinter. Davil has suspected something for months. Tashlyn confirmed it during his arrest.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:19: - Anything the party learns about Manshoon's cell: safe house locations, lieutenant identities, communication methods, operational timing. One confirmed piece per tenday is worth more than a dramatic report filed once a month.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:23: Davil's ideal outcome: the vault gold funds the Zhentarim Council bid, Manshoon's cell is dismantled before it can contest the vault, Skeemo's betrayal is cleaned up internally without Watch involvement, and Ziraj is alive. He keeps the moral ledger as balanced as his business allows.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:30: | **Gralhund Villa** | Davil is arrested in the Watch's post-Gralhund crackdown on all Zhentarim operations. A flying snake delivers the first word: *"Davil is unavailable. Continue normal operations. Await further contact."* Tashlyn takes over. Her first direct message to the party: *"Keep a low profile. I'll be in touch."* She will not explain what that means. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:31: | **Faction Outposts** | The Doom Raiders have been watching Yellowspire, Manshoon's Castle Ward relay point, for two months. Tashlyn has schematics. She has been waiting for someone capable of going inside. Mission 5 is the operational payoff of that patience. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:32: | **Kolat Towers** | Davil has the pass-amulet distribution ledger (from Mission 5) and Ziraj's rooftop diagram (from Mission 6). Between them, the Doom Raiders can offer the party two independent paths through Kolat Towers' outer defenses, provided both missions are complete. This is the faction's contribution to **Kolat Towers**. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:33: | **Vault of Dragons** | At Dread Lord renown: Davil proposes a 10% split in exchange for a distraction crew, a locksmith backup, and a wagon extraction team. If Skeemo is still alive, he is present at the vault working for Manshoon's simulacrum and will recognize the party on sight. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:45: - **Feed intelligence on Manshoon's cell to Davil or Tashlyn (+1).** The internal war is the Doom Raiders' most pressing problem. Any confirmed information about Manshoon's agents, safe houses, or operational plans earns this reward. One award per distinct piece of intelligence.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:46: - **Neutralize a named Manshoon operative (+2).** Eliminate or capture one of Manshoon's lieutenants. Delivering a prisoner to Tashlyn earns the full renown; confirmed elimination earns +1.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:47: - **Protect a Doom Raider commercial front from disruption (+1).** If a Black Network legitimate business is targeted by Manshoon's agents, the Watch, or a rival faction, characters who intervene earn renown. Once per incident.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:59: | 25 | **Ardragon** | The Network deploys a three-person crew (two **spies** and one **assassin**) to assist in one heist or operation per quest. Davil's informants inside Manshoon's cell can be activated for specific intelligence once per quest (DC 14 Intelligence check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two tendays). Ziraj the Hunter owes Davil a favor: once per campaign, request that Ziraj neutralize one named target, captured alive or quietly eliminated. Ziraj won't take assignments he considers suicidal. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:60: | 50 | **Dread Lord** | Eight Black Network operatives are available for one major operation. Davil proposes the character for a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council, a position of genuine continental influence within the Black Network. Doom Raiders safe houses in every ward. Davil offers formal partnership for the vault operation: a distraction crew draws off one faction's forces, a locksmith backup stands by, and a wagon team handles extraction logistics, in exchange for 10% of whatever the party walks away with. |

campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:9: Rather than competing with Xanathar or Manshoon for the Stone, Jarlaxle already holds Eye #3, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* and mounted beneath the *Eyecatcher*'s keel, and is content to let the other factions exhaust themselves chasing each other. His asset inside Xanathar's organization — **Nar'l Xibrindas**, a drow mage who serves as the beholder's advisor and reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell — keeps him informed of the Guild's movements. The Sea Maidens Faire parades give him cover to move personnel and materials through any ward without scrutiny.

campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:1: # Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:3: Game mechanics for running Manshoon's Zhentarim as a villain faction. For who they are, see the **Manshoon's Zhentarim** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:7: All three Zhentarim response teams consist of a lieutenant plus 3–4 **thugs**. Every lieutenant carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers and will try to destroy it if capture seems certain, except Agorn, who trades his to save himself.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:9: Any Zhentarim team sent to attack the characters carries orders to take them as prisoners to the Interrogation House on Brindul Alley. Questioned team members can point the characters toward the house. The lighter roster is intentional. Manshoon wants intelligence, not body counts, and underestimated enemies are more useful to him than dead ones. These teams also keep pressure on the party without forcing a lethal confrontation.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:19: *Independent discovery path: parties that searched Urstul Floxin's effects at Gralhund Villa have documents pointing to Brindul Alley. Parties that did not can find the safe house through North Ward canvassing — the traffic is unusual enough to notice (DC 14 Investigation over a day, no faction membership required). This is the Manshoon chain's street-level entry point.*
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:21: Use the map for house L1 (DH p. 62). Avareen Windrivver and Zorbog Jyarkoth maintain this building as a combination safe house and covert interrogation center. Three black **flying snakes** nest near the ceiling — the Zhentarim's message couriers.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:23: *Front Room.* Avareen and Zorbog's quarters. Avareen carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers. While the party explores, a flying snake arrives with *A Directive to Zorbog* signed by "Manshoon," ordering Zorbog to kidnap Fenerus Stormcastle of Quill Alley, a "known associate of these new meddlers in the Grand Game."
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:25: *Backroom.* A rack and other instruments of torture. An arcane circle on the floor. Strapped into the rack is **Ott Steeltoes**, a Xanathar's Guild agent captured while the Zhentarim were trying to locate the Eye. He knows nothing of the Eye itself.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:31: **Escalation:** Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3). Flying snakes carry the alarm within hours if any escape; Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 3 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:37: Use the Winter version of the Old Tower (DH p. 79). Amath Seccent and four **acolytes** occupy this converted tower. The permanent teleportation circle here connects directly to Kolat Towers — one of the few physical routes into Manshoon's lair that bypasses the pass-amulet requirement.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:39: *Area O2 — Ground Level.* A crumpled note from Avareen to Seccent, full of barely contained contempt: Avareen complains about Manshoon's directive to hand over a broadsheet publisher named Shan Chien for Seccent's "foul rites," but closes with "Manshoon's word is my command."
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:45: **Escalation:** Manshoon moves to Suspicious (Tier 2) if the Interrogation House has not yet been hit; moves to Lockdown (Tier 4) if it has. Agorn carries word to surviving Zhentarim contacts if he escapes; response teams redeploy within 3 days. **Founders' Day Clock: 3–5 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:54: | Lockdown | Urstul's team if the others fail; Manshoon issues personal directives by flying snake. Manshoon's simulacrum engages; Sidra locks down Kolat Towers |
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:56: Manshoon underestimates threats that aren't magical. A party that stays clear of Kolat Towers may go tendays before he escalates beyond his response teams. His preference for capturing over killing creates tactical openings.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:62: **Zhentarim Interrogation House** (North Ward, Brindul Alley)
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:65: - Questioning Zhentarim response teams / being captured by them
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:73: **Lair: Kolat Towers** (Southern Ward)
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:82: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim** organization page
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:85: - **Manshoon** villain page
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:9: **All four villain factions run simultaneously.** No season villain. Xanathar's Guild, Manshoon's Zhentarim, the Cassalanters, and Bregan D'aerthe are active from session one, each pursuing a different angle on the same prize.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:11: **Act III is four distinct heist genres.** Xanathar's Lair is dungeon infiltration; Cassalanter Villa is social penetration; Sea Maidens Faire is a nautical caper; Kolat Towers is a fortress assault. Each target is scoutable, plannable, and responsive to how thoroughly the party prepares.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:13: **The three Eyes are distributed among the factions.** Recovering all three from their holders is the precondition for opening the vault. Kolat Towers, Manshoon's fortress, holds no Eye.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:32: **Feature All Villains** means keeping all four factions moving between sessions. Faction Response Teams deploy when the party hits assets; the party's choices shift which factions gain ground. Each faction responds to threat differently: Xanathar escalates on instinct, Manshoon prefers capture over kill, the Cassalanters exhaust legal and social leverage before calling up infernal muscle. That difference should be legible at the table. See [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) for the between-session tracking procedure.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:48: The MacGuffin chain runs in a straight line: the Stone of Golorr names the vault but can't open it without all three Eyes. Xanathar holds Eye #1 (in Sylgar's fish bowl, unknowingly). The Cassalanters hold Eye #2. Jarlaxle holds Eye #3 aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Kolat Towers holds Manshoon, not an Eye. It's the fourth heist target; clearing it before the Vault pushes the party to Level 7.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:11: | III | The Heists | 4–7 | Faction Outposts, Xanathar's Lair, Cassalanter Villa, Sea Maidens Faire, Kolat Towers | 16–28 | Can the PCs recover all three Eyes before any faction assembles them? |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:29: Five quests, four heist targets, no fixed order. The party holds the Stone and needs three Eyes to open the Vault. Faction Outposts provides the intelligence to plan. Three of the four lair heists hold an Eye; Kolat Towers holds Manshoon. Each target is built as a genuine operation: scoutable, plannable, and responsive to how thoroughly the party prepares. Xanathar's Lair is a dungeon infiltration into the criminal undercity. Cassalanter Villa is social penetration into a noble household with a cult temple beneath it. The Sea Maidens Faire is a nautical caper aboard three ships. Kolat Towers is a fortress assault against Manshoon's extradimensional sanctum. Two clocks run underneath all of it: the Faire departs Tarsakh 20 and Founders' Day falls Flamerule 1. Act III is the bulk of the campaign. Thorough planning pays off; the structure supports improvisation.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:52: > [!design]**Why Level 7 Needs Kolat Towers**
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:53: > Three lair heists bring the party to 22 cumulative points, which is Level 6. The Level 7 threshold is 23 points. Completing all four heists, including Kolat Towers, pushes the cumulative total to 26 and crosses that threshold before the Vault of Dragons begins. A party that skips Kolat Towers enters the Vault at Level 6 and reaches Level 7 inside the Vault sequence. A party that completes all four heists enters at Level 7 and exits at Level 8, the Undermountain handoff.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/bonds-and-flaws.md:56: > | 7 | The lair heists (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**): none of them can be done alone. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:53: ### The Holder: Urstul Floxin (Manshoon's Zhentarim)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:55: Urstul Floxin is Manshoon's chief field operative in Waterdeep: a large, heavyset Illuskan man in his forties who moves through rooms as though he owns them and reserves his anger for moments when it will accomplish something. He is methodical, personally dangerous, and one of the more capable people the character will encounter during the campaign.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:62: > Floxin works for Manshoon, not the Doom Raiders. A character whose Debt is held by Floxin is already entangled with the campaign's primary villain faction, whether they know it or not. Davil Starsong's cell is a separate organization and won't automatically come to this character's aid. Let the character discover the distinction on their own.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:133: He's a genuine professional who believes the Doom Raiders' moderate approach to crime is both more effective and more defensible than Manshoon's methods. His first formal ask of the character is small and framed as exactly that: he's calibrating them, not exploiting them, and he says so. Characters who engage honestly find the relationship evolving into something closer to a working partnership over the course of the campaign. Characters who attempt to use the asset-running as leverage against him will find that Davil has been anticipating that move since before the first note was dropped.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:15: The remix runs all four simultaneously for three reasons. First, a city where four powerful factions are genuinely competing gives players organic reasons to act without the plot needing to push them. If the Cassalanters are poisoning people, Xanathar is having informants killed, and Manshoon is blackmailing city officials, the party doesn't need a quest to care. Second, four-faction play creates genuine choice: which lair do you hit first? Whose Eye do you trade for an alliance? A single-villain campaign has only one answer to those questions. Third, no single villain defines the campaign's identity, which means it can accommodate different playstyles at once. The group that wants political intrigue gets it through the Cassalanters; the group that wants dungeon delving gets it through Xanathar's Lair.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:27: The four lairs run as four distinct genres. Xanathar's Lair is dungeon infiltration: break into an underground complex with a beholder at the center. Cassalanter Villa is social infiltration: work within a functioning household whose owners are watching you back. Sea Maidens Faire is a caper: the thing that's supposed to go wrong goes wrong in the first act, and you adapt in real time. Kolat Towers is a raid: Manshoon isn't a puzzle, he's an obstacle, and the question is whether you can take him. Different player instincts dominate different genres, so the heist phase plays differently from session to session rather than feeling like four iterations of the same map.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:125: - Vell (Manshoon Splinter spy; **The Talking Mare** antagonist hunting Maxeene) — single-mission NPC; no profile
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:43: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the party spends three sessions pursuing Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon haven't been idle. Keep a one-line answer ready for what each faction did during that time. When PCs canvass contacts, read Watch reports, or press informants, they may turn up traces of that off-screen activity. The world feels inhabited rather than scripted when the factions are visibly busy with their own agendas.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:51: **Manshoon** underestimates threats that aren't magical. A party that avoids Kolat Towers and doesn't advertise itself through arcane channels can operate at Suspicious tier for multiple sessions before he escalates. His preference for capturing over killing creates tactical openings, since a confrontation often produces a job offer before a fight.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:61: Manshoon's Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild are in active conflict at street level in the Dock Ward. Manshoon's operatives probe Guild territory regularly; Xanathar has issued a kill order on Manshoon that has failed twice. This is a grinding low-level engagement that produces casualties on both sides and keeps both factions consuming resources on a front entirely separate from the PCs.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:65: Jarlaxle and Manshoon maintain a fragile non-interference arrangement. Each considers the other the only genuine peer in the Grand Game, and neither wants direct conflict while other factions are more immediate problems. The arrangement is under strain: Manshoon has identified Fenerus Stormcastle as a Bregan D'aerthe cut-out and ordered his abduction. If that abduction proceeds, Jarlaxle will act unilaterally, and the non-interference collapses.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:72: > This is the Grand Game's self-reinforcing pressure. If the PCs dismantle Xanathar's Guild outposts, Manshoon's Zhentarim moves into the vacated Dock Ward territory. If the PCs weaken Manshoon, Xanathar's paranoia briefly lifts and it turns outward. The PCs' choices produce real chain reactions because thinking factions respond to changing conditions. The inter-faction operations matrix in **Running the Villains** shows where each faction's active operations overlap and which documents cross faction lines; consult it whenever the party clears territory a rival faction would want.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:78: **The Doom Raiders** are a player-joinable faction, not a villain faction. If the party has turned them hostile, Davil Starsong will negotiate the moment he sees a path to de-escalation. What he wants is information: something useful about the party's other activities or contacts. A working relationship can be bought back with the right currency. Turning the Doom Raiders hostile closes the Zhentarim renown track and the cell's contact network; restoring goodwill reopens them.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:80: **Manshoon** can be satisfied in the short term by a demonstration of usefulness or the delivery of an asset he values. His preference for capturing over killing means a direct confrontation often produces a job offer before violence, at least until the party has cost him too much. Manshoon holds debts. A party that delivers something useful will find him willing to deal again.

campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:14: | Doom Raiders (Zhentarim) | Criminal network access, fences, safe houses, street intelligence | Jobs that keep the network profitable |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:18: > If you want your character to join "the Zhentarim," you're joining the **Doom Raiders**. A separate Zhentarim cell operates elsewhere in the city, answering to a different and far more dangerous leader, with different contacts, different methods, and a moral code that has nothing in common with the Doom Raiders'. The two organizations do not get along, and your GM will always make clear which one you're dealing with.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:63: - Prevent large-scale violence, assist the City Watch beyond their capacity, or counter Zhentarim Splinter operations.

campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:122: ### Doom Raiders (Zhentarim)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:124: The Doom Raiders reward intelligence on their internal enemies and protection of the Network's commercial fronts. The internal war with a rival Zhentarim cell makes every confirmed piece of intelligence about that cell valuable.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:135: | **Dread Lord** | 50 | Eight Black Network operatives for one major operation. Your contact proposes you for a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council. Doom Raiders safe houses in every ward. campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:163: | Doom Raiders | **Davil Starsong** (Missions 1–2); **Tashlyn Yafeera** (Missions 3–6) | Intelligence on Manshoon's cell; containment of Xanathar's Guild activity; anything that weakens their rivals |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:190: The factions' interests are incompatible by design. The Lords' Alliance wants the gold returned to the city treasury intact. The Doom Raiders want Manshoon destroyed; his cell threatens their entire operation. Force Grey wants the Grand Game resolved before it spills into public view and embarrasses the city. Bregan D'aerthe wants a political favor that extends well beyond Waterdeep's walls.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:192: A party with members in multiple factions will face these tensions as individual character obligations. When a Harper PC and a Doom Raider PC disagree about whether to hand the Stone to Mirt or protect Manshoon's rival operation, that's a character moment. Let it play as one. Treat each faction position as a real constraint on that character, give the players space to negotiate it between themselves, and resist resolving it by erasing one character's obligation. The disagreement is the texture of the campaign's second act.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:200: The seven factions above are the organizations available for player characters to join. The other major powers active in Waterdeep during the Grand Game — **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter**, and the **Cassalanters** — are adversary factions. Players may interact with, negotiate with, or broker deals with these organizations, but formal membership is not available through normal play. See **Notable Figures** for details on their leaders and agents, and **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for how to run them at the table.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:21: **Eye #1** — formerly held by Manshoon's Zhentarim, who stole it from Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. Xanathar killed the Zhentarim envoys who brought it to him and seized the Eye. It now resides in Xanathar's Lair sanctum.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:25: **Eye #3** — held by Jarlaxle Baenre, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. It began in Renaer Neverember's mourning locket. Manshoon's Zhentarim seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse, Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried off the Eye with the rest of the back office without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the same night. It reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout, and Manshoon's cell never held it. Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep. He is active from Act I through his personas — J.B. Nevercott in Trollskull Alley and Zardoz Zord at the Field of Triumph — and his villain escalation keys off the **Jarlaxle Informed** flag set during **Fireball!**, which records only that the party made contact with him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:30: > **Eye #3:** Jarlaxle Baenre — aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Jarlaxle knows exactly what it is. campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:53: **The two Zhentarims.** In Waterdeep, "the Zhentarim" describes two separate organizations with nothing in common except the name. Davil Starsong's Doom Raiders are criminals who present as legitimate businesspeople and will recruit the party during Trollskull Alley; they're a joinable player faction. A rival Zhentarim cell operates elsewhere in the city, answers to a different and far more dangerous leader, and is openly hostile to the Doom Raiders. Establish this at session zero; it's the distinction most likely to confuse players once the city's faction dynamics become visible. Joining the Doom Raiders doesn't mean working for the campaign's primary villain faction.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:5: The campaign contains two distinct Zhentarim factions. Conflating them is a preparation error with real consequences.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:7: **The Doom Raiders** are Davil Starsong's cell — a player-joinable faction that presents a public face of legitimate business and moderate criminal enterprise. They operate openly, hold meetings in taverns, and recruit adventurers. They don't acknowledge Manshoon's cell as legitimate Zhentarim, and they surface during **Trollskull Alley** as the first Zhentarim contact the party encounters.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:9: **Manshoon's Zhentarim** are the villain faction. They operate from Kolat Towers, conduct assassinations, and maintain mind-controlled servants. Manshoon regards the Doom Raiders as deserters who stole his name.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:11: These two cells won't cooperate. If their operations overlap, they actively undermine each other. When a scene involves "the Zhentarim," know which cell is present. The distinction should become apparent to attentive players during **Trollskull Alley** — earlier encounters with the Doom Raiders' professionalism make Manshoon's operatives land harder when they appear.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:14: > Every scene involving a "Zhentarim" NPC requires knowing which cell is present. The Doom Raiders and Manshoon's Zhentarim won't coordinate or share intelligence. If their operations run parallel, they'll try to disrupt each other. Davil Starsong's renown mechanic is entirely separate from Manshoon's faction state. Track them separately and never conflate the two when reading attunement flags.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:26: **Team personalities.** Full rosters are on the Factions guide pages ([Bregan D'aerthe](../factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md), [Xanathar's Guild](../factions/09-xanathars-guild.md), [Manshoon's Zhentarim](../factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md), [Cassalanters](../factions/11-cassalanters.md)). Their tactical characters at the table:
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:29: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim** — professional mercenary tactics; more willing to negotiate mid-fight than any other faction; there are always more members nearby than visible.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:90: Verification lives in the Revelation Lists on the villain Factions guide pages ([Bregan D'aerthe](../factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md), [Xanathar's Guild](../factions/09-xanathars-guild.md), [Manshoon's Zhentarim](../factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md), [Cassalanters](../factions/11-cassalanters.md)).

campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:27: Also in the hidden compartment: the **Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game**. This document is a hand-written summary of the current faction competition for the Stone of Golorr, prepared by Victoro for his own reference. It references Manshoon's operations directly, identifies Kolat Towers by name and location, describes Zhentarim splinter movements in the Castle Ward, and includes the note: *"The wizard who calls himself Manshoon guards his fortress with a force field that admits only those he has keyed."* This is the Three Clue Rule path to Kolat Towers — Kolat Towers.

campaign/locations/gralhund-villa/g15-guest-suite.md:5: *Note: The published Dragon Heist module places a permanent teleportation circle linked to Kolat Towers in this room, along with three flying snakes in a birdcage. campaign/locations/gralhund-villa/g18-childrens-room.md:7: Neither child will fight. Greth is crying quietly; Zartan has pressed himself against the wall between the door and the large wardrobe and hasn't moved. Neither will speak to strangers unless spoken to gently, and neither knows anything about the Stone, the Zhentarim, or Asmodeus.
campaign/locations/gralhund-villa/g19-servants-wing.md:13: **Night state:** Nine commoners are barricaded inside this room with improvised weapons (clubs, a fireplace poker, a heavy brass candlestick). They have been here since Raid Beat 2 and they are not coming out. They are not hostile to the party — if the party identifies itself as not Zhentarim, the commoners will confirm they are alive and ask, very quietly, if it is over. It is not over. They will wait. They cannot help.

campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/z05-offices.md:7: **Paper bird (magic item):** On the reception desk, folded from a torn sheet of vellum — a message that never left. DC 14 Intelligence (Arcana) identifies it as a *paper bird* (WDH Appendix A magic item: when a command word is spoken, a name is written on the bird in ink, and it flies to find that person, delivering a written message of up to twenty-five words). The bird is currently blank. The command word is penciled on the underside of the desk: *Shan* (a Zhentarim courier alias).
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:9: ***The Two Zhentarims in Finding Floon.*** Dragon Heist presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Finding Floon to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist (a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people) is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar's Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Trollskull Alley and then raid Kolat Towers in Kolat Towers will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1: one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. Players feel the distinction before anyone explains it.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:21: ***Renaer's Introduction.*** In the source, finding Renaer in the warehouse is a pleasant surprise in an otherwise thin scene (a friendly NPC who points the party downward). This treatment makes Renaer's introduction structural: the warehouse is the quest's pivot. The party arrives expecting Floon and finds someone who matters more to the campaign's back story. Renaer's knowledge state is asymmetric by design. He knows the Zhentarim took his locket and that the Guild attacked before they finished with him, without knowing what the locket contained or where it went. His ignorance is genuine. This positions him as a trustworthy ally (he doesn't conceal what he knows) while preserving the campaign's central mystery (the nature of the Eyes, the Vault's location). The gap between what they'll eventually learn and what Renaer knows gives the warehouse scene a retrospective weight absent from the source text.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:107: > Doom Raiders: Yagra, flying snake tattoo on her neck, Zhentarim pendant worn openly. Xanathar's Guild: Krentz and four thugs with eye-shaped tattoos shaved into scalps. Bregan D'Aerthe: Ryvarra at the balcony table. Manshoon's Zhentarim: not present at the Yawning Portal. The Dock Ward killing Yagra referenced is their work.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:49: **The Most Trollskull Alley-Specific Faction Content.** Force Grey Missions 1 and 2 are the only Level 2 faction missions designed primarily to feed information threads rather than build renown in isolation. Hlam's warning (Mission 1) gives the first specific reference to Manshoon by implication. Zelifarn's barnacle-covered shipwreck lair at the harbor bottom (Mission 2) contains physical evidence of unusual deep-harbor activity pointing toward Skullport. Force Grey is the only faction where the Level 2–3 missions function as intelligence operations. Parties who join Force Grey in Trollskull Alley arrive at Xanathar's Lair with more operational context than any other faction affiliation provides.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-03-the-neighbors.md:63: Fala's mention of Ziraj (or a subsequent follow-up conversation about the "friend who visits the second floor") establishes the Doom Raiders' personal-connection style before Davil Starsong's recruitment invitation arrives. The distinction between Doom Raiders and Manshoon's Splinter is easier to absorb when one half of it has already been humanized.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-04-the-factions-come-calling.md:79: **Force Grey Mission 1** delivers a key Undermountain thread: Hlam tells the party that "evil's twin hides its face for now — expect that to change before winter's end." campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-04-the-factions-come-calling.md:86: 2. Yagra Stonefist as staff candidate (the **Trollskull Manor** guide) — her Zhentarim pendant and Doom Raiders affiliation visible to any PC who asks
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:21: ***Floxin as Self-Serving Witness.*** His cooperation is not generosity. He wants the Stone tracked so the Zhentarim can follow. His account of the "metal man" is accurate because a false description produces a false lead and he needs the real one found. Parties that pass DC 14 Insight must decide what to do with a useful informant who is also a tail.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:61: ***The First Quest Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Finding Floon establishes the Guild and the Zhentarim as adversaries. Trollskull Alley introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and BD as observers. Fireball is the first quest where all four reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor — they are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time.

A North Ward resident saw Floxin — visibly burned and limping — enter Gralhund Villa on Saerdoun Street within hours of the blast. The City Watch sent two constables who were told everything was fine. No warrant was issued.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:204: **Doom Raiders — Davil Starsong (Renown 1+):** Davil is still free during Fireball. He identifies Floxin immediately as Manshoon's blade. He wants proof of Floxin's direct involvement. He also wants to know if anyone arrived at the blast site early — before the Watch, before the other witnesses — and behaved in a way that suggested foreknowledge. He has a suspicion he is not ready to name.

campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:35: ## Manshoon Stays Invisible
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:37: The party fights Manshoon's operatives throughout this quest and does not learn whose operatives they are. Floxin does not name his master. This is consistent with his Notable Figures profile and with how operational security works in a cell structure where the operator never sees the principal. DC 14 Insight during any interrogation of Floxin confirms this genuinely: he knows a dead-drop chain and a code, not a name or a face.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:39: When the Manshoon reveal comes, it needs to land as a recontextualization of events the party has already lived through. A **Gralhund Villa** reveal produces a name they file away. A later reveal, after they have encountered his agents in multiple quests without connecting them to a single source, produces the retroactive click that defines a well-structured mystery.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:45: If the chase fails and the Stone reaches a faction safe house, the campaign continues from a weaker position. Jarlaxle will deal with the party. Xanathar will not. Manshoon's cell now knows the party is active and failed. These consequences reshape the social landscape of subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is **Faction Outposts**' problem, not this quest's.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:57: Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's North Ward operations become more careful. Jarlaxle leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown (who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn) define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-01-what-the-factions-say.md:27: The most direct voice in the room. Davil places Floxin in Trollskull Alley that night and names him as the reason Dalakhar is dead and the Stone is behind a locked gate in the North Ward. He frames recovery as a matter of clearing the Doom Raiders' name: the Watch has witnesses who placed Zhentarim agents in the alley. What the Watch doesn't have is anyone willing to say those agents weren't working for the Doom Raiders. Floxin's testimony — his own account of who sent him into Trollskull Alley and why — can separate the Doom Raiders from the bombing entirely.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-01-what-the-factions-say.md:79: The source who named the villa does not have a faction brief to deliver. He wants to understand what his father's vault has to do with the Gralhunds, and what the Gralhunds have to do with the Zhentarim who were chasing Dalakhar through Trollskull Alley the night of the explosion. His access to Harper contacts gave the party the address. His family history gives him a stake in what happens once they cross the gate.

He knows the word "Zhentarim" and will use it to construct a misleading story about a rival cell if he judges that story useful.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-05-the-guest-suite.md:35: > Floxin will not name Manshoon. Not under Intimidation, not under magical compulsion, not with a blade at his throat. He does not know the name — he knows a code and a set of dead-drop locations. The party can verify this with DC 14 Insight: he genuinely does not know who employed him. He knows the shape of the operation and the chain of command stopped at a contact he never saw face to face. Manshoon stays invisible.

The point was leverage to recover the Stone from Dalakhar, the gnome who stole it from Lord Neverember's people. "It went sideways. The gnome didn't come for the boy." He says this softly, as if volume changes what it means.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/design-notes.md:47: The draft's favor was "I genuinely do not know yet," which left the GM to improvise. The Event now settles it. The favor is a sealed letter for Rizzeryl, the drow mage of House Auvryndar in the Base de Résistance on the second level. It offers a safe road for an Auvryndar courier in exchange for Xanathar's Guild mooring list. The cost is the secret, which the member keeps from any Zhentarim or Doom Raider ally in the party until Rizzeryl has read it. This is the old draft's "keeping a secret that conflicts with a party ally's interests" category, and it asks nothing of a companion. The host does not describe the favor because telling would change how the member carries it.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:278: > The man Davil asks about is Urstul Floxin, the Black Network assassin he believes leads the other cell. Floxin is a large, heavyset man in his forties who carries himself as though every room belongs to him, and he makes no effort to hide that he is Zhentarim. Davil keeps the name back at this point in the conversation because he wants the candidates' own descriptions, without a name to lead them. The party has not met Floxin yet, because he stays out of sight during **Finding Floon**, but a candidate who saw a man like this around the Dock Ward or the Yawning Portal can answer from memory. Davil wants to know where and when they saw him, who was with him, and whether he carried a black flying snake, because Floxin sends his messages by those snakes. Anything the party reports goes into Davil's book as another sighting of the man he thinks is running the other cell. In truth Floxin is Manshoon's field agent, and nothing in this Event reveals it.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m04-silencing-skeemo/ev-03-the-reckoning.md:194: Skeemo's death, capture or escape changes the cell. A dead Skeemo leaves an empty chair at the table, and Davil is told by Tashlyn when he is released. A captured Skeemo stays in the tack room until Davil decides his fate in **Davil's Return**. An escaped Skeemo sits in the sanctum at Kolat Towers and recognizes the party on sight, and the tower's garrison is briefed on the party's methods.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m04-silencing-skeemo/overview.md:17: At 06:00 a flying snake finds each eligible member and tells them to come to the wagon yard beside the South Gate. campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m04-silencing-skeemo/overview.md:25: > Skeemo's masters in the Splinter want him to keep reporting until he is no longer useful, and the failed poisoning has told him that day is near. He has hired a dray for 10:00 and means to reach the Banite tower called Yellowspire in the Castle Ward, where a teleportation circle takes him to Kolat Towers. The Doom Raiders believe Urstul Floxin leads the Splinter. Manshoon directs Floxin from behind him, and until **Manshoon Named** is marked every Doom Raider says "the other cell" or "the Splinter", and adds "Floxin's cell" only if **Floxin Status** is Alive. If **Floxin Status** is Dead or Captured, the Doom Raiders know Floxin is out of play and believe someone above him gives the orders, and they do not know who.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/design-notes.md:11: The old ten-minute clock started when someone stepped into the circle, and nothing gave a party a reason to do that. The plate now guards the ledger box, so a party that wants the ledger has to deal with it, and Amath's call bowl gives the defenders a way to start the clock on purpose. The clock can be stopped only by wrecking the circle, which costs the party the Yellowspire entry to **Kolat Towers**, and the plate is a small rehearsal of the pressure plate that guards the sanctum foyer there. The plate check is Wisdom (Perception), and every hint has a fallback, because the trap is meant to be found and not to punish.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/design-notes.md:15: **Ziraj's Last Hunt** now runs after **Kolat Towers**, so the gap diagram cannot wait for it. Ziraj hands over the notes at Corellon's Crown in both Events, and Davil's order to him covers a party that comes back with nothing, so the intel never depends on success. The dusk anchor for the gap cycle is an addition of this mission. The **Kolat Towers** quest gives the cycle, the 60-second windows and the 30-minute Alert shift, and it should keep the dusk anchor or replace it.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/design-notes.md:19: Floxin's fate from **Gralhund Villa** changes Doom Raider speech ("the other cell" when he is dead or captured), and **Manshoon Named** gates the name. Agorn Fuoco's one-in-three chance is replaced by a fixed rule, and he never fights and is not statted. The amulet hook now names Avareen Windrivver, Agorn and the Kolat Towers field crews as carriers, matching the three captured-amulet sources in the **Kolat Towers** quest. **Ledger Recovered** is renamed **Relay Ledger Recovered** because the Order of the Gauntlet already uses the old name. Contradictions in other files, including the Trades Ward wording in the **Silencing Skeemo** draft, belong in the Doom Raiders section of the out-of-scope notes and are not fixed here.

A character who makes a successful DC 15 Wisdom (Insight) check notices that she is stalling, and a character who makes a successful DC 14 Wisdom (Perception) check hears a coin ring in the cellar and sees a gray glow beneath the cellar door.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:251: > The door swings inward onto a stone chamber with no furniture except an iron-bound box bolted to the east wall. Runes are cut into the floor in a wide circle, and a brass bowl sits in the ring's edge with three Zhentarim coins lying in the bottom. In the northeast corner, one flagstone looks a little paler than the rest.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:255: > The circle is permanent, and it is always active. Its runes are keyed to a pressure plate in the northeast corner, a flagstone stamped with a Zhentarim coin. While a creature weighing at least 50 pounds presses the plate, the runes stay dormant, and the circle can be crossed or the relay box opened. If anyone opens the box or crosses the ring while the plate is unpressed, the runes flash a dim gray for a heartbeat and send a silent signal to Kolat Towers. That starts the ten-minute clock. A coin dropped in the call bowl sends the same signal whether or not the plates are pressed, because the bowl is how the garrison calls for help on purpose.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:265: > The ledger lists every carrier of a pass-amulet by name, the day each amulet was last refreshed and where each carrier hands it on. It names three carriers: Avareen Windrivver, who works from a house in Brindul Alley; Agorn Fuoco, who visits Yellowspire on some evenings; and the field crews that Vevette Blackwater sends out from Kolat Towers.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:267: > A character who reads the ledger for ten minutes and makes a successful DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation) check understands the refresh marks. That reveals which carrier holds the freshest amulet and the mark that Kolat Towers uses to recalibrate a stale one.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:279: > The letters are written in a simple substitution cipher. A character who reads them for one hour and makes a successful DC 13 Intelligence (Investigation) check decodes them, and Tashlyn decodes them without a roll. They name Vevette Blackwater as the officer who coordinates the strike teams from Kolat Towers and list the operations she has planned for the coming month.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:322: > The party can prevent the reinforcements by destroying the circle before the clock runs out. A successful casting of *Dispel Magic* on the circle, made with a DC 15 spellcasting ability check, ruins it. A party without the spell can pour two flasks of acid along the inner ring and scrape the runes for one minute. Reinforcements then have to walk from Kolat Towers and reach Yellowspire one hour after the clock started.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:324: > A wrecked circle is a permanent loss. It closes the Yellowspire entry method in **Kolat Towers** for the rest of the campaign, and Davil is unhappy about it. Mark **Yellowspire Circle Destroyed**.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:338: Davil asked Ziraj to hand over his surveillance notes on the Kolat Towers force field to whoever went into Yellowspire, whether they came out with the ledger or not. **The Debrief** plays the same scene for a party that went in during **Faction Outposts**, so run only one of the two. campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/ev-01-the-yellowspire-job.md:374: > The notes show three gaps in the force field on the upper walls of the towers. Each gap opens for 60 seconds and closes again, and a different gap opens two hours later. The cycle repeats every six hours: the first gap opens at dusk, the second two hours later, the third two hours after that, and the first again at the next six-hour mark. Each gap is at upper-floor height, and one is level with the bridge between the towers. The notes mark each gap's position on a rough sketch. The party keeps the sketch, which the **Kolat Towers** quest calls the force-field gap diagram.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/overview.md:26: Yellowspire is a narrow tower of yellowish brick that a Banite priestess named Amath Seccent calls the House of Tyranny. She and her acolytes hold a prayer hour there each dusk, and the tower doubles as a relay for Manshoon's Splinter, with a permanent teleportation circle in a chamber beyond the cellar that connects to Kolat Towers. The circle's runes are keyed to a pressure plate in the northeast corner, and a courier who steps onto the ring without pressing it sends a silent signal to the towers. Amath keeps a ledger of pass-amulet carriers in an iron-bound box beside the circle, and she keeps coded letters from Kolat Towers in a locked drawer upstairs.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/overview.md:28: If Skeemo Weirdbottle escaped through the circle, Amath has been warned. The Doom Raiders believe that Urstul Floxin leads the Splinter, but Manshoon directs Floxin from behind him, and no Doom Raider says the name unless **Manshoon Named** is marked. If Floxin is dead or captured, they say "the other cell" and never "Floxin's cell".

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m05-the-yellowspire-job/overview.md:84: In The Yellowspire Job, Amath fights with the 2024 Priest statistics, and her four acolytes use the 2024 Priest Acolyte statistics. Zhentarim guards use the Tough and Warrior Veteran statistics in numbers set by party size. Reinforcements use the Spy statistics and arrive through the circle ten minutes after the alarm. The pressure plate deals no damage, and the party can avoid every fight by leaving before the clock runs out. The Debrief has no enemies.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m06-zirajis-last-hunt/design-notes.md:5: ***The mission used to hand over an intelligence product, and now it asks whether Ziraj survives.*** The draft ended with Ziraj's rooftop diagram of the force field gaps. That intelligence now belongs to **The Yellowspire Job**, where Ziraj hands his notes over at Corellon's Crown, so **Force Field Gap Intel** is no longer set here. Seventh level also requires all four lair heists, which makes this mission a post-**Kolat Towers** event. The kill team is therefore the Splinter's survivors, acting out of revenge and out of Ziraj's own surveillance of the tower. Nothing in the event depends on the party having used or skipped his notes.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m06-zirajis-last-hunt/design-notes.md:13: ***What was cut.*** The forty-three names Davil hands over are gone, because **Kolat Towers** already delivers the list. The "We take care of our own" line is gone as a slogan. The draft's split approach is replaced by the false trail, because the reference rosters assume the whole party fights. The wound rule was rewritten so Fala's healing ends it: magical healing, including her tinctures, removes the Disadvantage, and her two-day rest is advice only.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/m06-zirajis-last-hunt/design-notes.md:15: ***Invented and unverified.*** Ondra Kell is invented, along with her status as a survivor of the Kolat Towers raid. The unprofiled minor characters are Ondra and the unnamed Spies. The inn near the alley's mouth has no name in any source. Corellon's Crown and Trollskull Manor share the alley, so all timings were recomputed around a two-minute walk, and the pressure now comes from the sweep's approach, Ziraj's bleeding on an open roof and the six minutes it takes to get him down and to the shop. Sail Street is used as in the draft and isn't verified against a setting page. The rooftop hide and black-fletched arrows are invented to explain why the Splinter hunts Ziraj.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/design-notes.md:9: ## Tashlyn's Information and the Manshoon Gate
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/design-notes.md:11: Tashlyn's information comes from *Dragon Heist*: Floxin is the rumoured leader of the renegade cell, a warrant is out, scrying has failed, and the Renaer Neverember kidnapping may draw him back. That holds only if **Floxin Status** from **Gralhund Villa** is Alive. If he is Dead or Captured, the Doom Raiders' rumour is wrong: the cell still moves and still filed a tip two days after the villa, so Tashlyn concludes that someone above Floxin gives the orders, and she does not know who. The warrant and scrying facts drop out, and the gate constable remembers a lean hooded man without a bandage. The Renaer line becomes "whoever runs the other cell". The GM block says the tip then came from another cell member on Manshoon's orders. The tip from inside the Network comes from the Alexandrian Remix sidebar for Doom Raiders characters asking about Floxin.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/design-notes.md:13: That sidebar has Tashlyn call Floxin "Manshoon's blade". This Event drops the name from speech and gates it on **Manshoon Named**, which is set at the Faction Outposts Interrogation House. Until then the Doom Raiders believe Floxin leads the renegade cell. The truth about Floxin and the tip is in a GM-only block. Tashlyn gives her reason for trusting the tip's source (the Network title in the note), and the gate constable and the register give the party two more independent routes to the same fact.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/design-notes.md:25: Contradictions outside this folder belong in the Doom Raiders section of `docs/plans/harpers-out-of-scope-notes.md`. The First Meeting draft still has Davil name Manshoon, the Gralhund Villa aftermath (ev-09) presents Tashlyn's "Keep a low profile" as a message rather than the closing line of a meeting, and it applies Istrid's Renown adjustments as reported to Tashlyn, which this Event now hosts.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/ev-01-davils-arrest.md:18: Doom Raiders members are not arrested. The Watch has no evidence against them, only the knowledge that they are friends of a man it holds. Any member who shows a Zhentarim pendant in public for the next tenday is stopped and questioned once by a constable, and then let go.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/ev-01-davils-arrest.md:24: > Urstul Floxin is Manshoon's field agent, and the cell he serves is the Zhentarim splinter that Davil calls "the other cell". Read the **Floxin Status** recorded by **Gralhund Villa** (Alive, Dead or Captured), because it decides who filed the Watch tip and what Tashlyn can tell the party.

Floxin never sent it, and there is no bandaged man to find. The warrant in Tashlyn's information is moot, and she has nothing to scry.

Nobody among the Doom Raiders names Manshoon in this Event, and Tashlyn says "Floxin's cell", "the other cell" or "the renegades". If a member says the name Manshoon, Tashlyn tells them it isn't a name she has any use for and asks what they can prove about the cell instead. If **Manshoon Named** was somehow marked before this Event, she may say "Manshoon's blade" when she talks about Floxin, and her words on the tip stay the same.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s02-davils-return/design-notes.md:15: ## The Manshoon Gate and the Captured Skeemo
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s02-davils-return/design-notes.md:17: Davil says "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked, and he adds "Floxin's cell" only if **Floxin Status** is Alive. He suspects the cell keeps its people in "the towers in the Trades Ward", which are Kolat Towers, and Tashlyn's watcher has logged a tower in the Castle Ward where the letters go. Behind the gate he may say Manshoon and Kolat Towers. The truth is in a GM-only block at the top.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s02-davils-return/design-notes.md:25: Contradictions outside this folder belong in the Doom Raiders section of `docs/plans/harpers-out-of-scope-notes.md`. Guide 07 says Davil suspected Skeemo for months and that Tashlyn confirmed it during the arrest, which conflicts with **The Poisoned Delivery** and **Silencing Skeemo**, where he learns nothing until release. **Xanathar's Lair** Scene 1, **Kolat Towers** and **Vault of Dragons** still read the old **Davil Released: True / False** format.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s02-davils-return/ev-01-davils-return.md:29: > The Doom Raiders believe Urstul Floxin leads the Splinter. The truth is that Floxin serves Manshoon, and every report Skeemo sent went to Manshoon's fortress at Kolat Towers in the Trades Ward. Until **Manshoon Named** is marked, Davil says "the other cell" or "the Splinter". He suspects, without proof, that the cell keeps its people in "the towers in the Trades Ward", which are Kolat Towers. What he knows is Tashlyn's: her watcher has logged a tower in the Castle Ward where the cell's letters are collected. If **Floxin Status** is Alive, he may also say "Floxin's cell". If **Floxin Status** is Dead or Captured, Tashlyn has already told him that the man they took for the leader is gone and the cell still moves, so Davil never says "Floxin's cell" and believes someone above Floxin gives the orders. If **Manshoon Named** is marked, he may say Manshoon's name and may call Kolat Towers Manshoon's home.

WDH also puts it one block south of Kolat Towers. A GM line records the fact, and nobody points that way. Melannor's will-not-discuss list covers the Stone, the vault, the master of the other cell and the house to the north. A written answer covers a player who names Manshoon. The vault gold is Jeryth's line, relayed by Melannor, with no Persuasion check, because it is a public position. The restored Stone request from Jeryth is cut, since the Stone is off limits here. Melannor's alignment is Neutral Good where WDH says Chaotic Good, to match his Notable Figures page. WDH has him assign a first mission at once, and the remix holds it back. **The Undercliff Scarecrows** arrives by its own pigeon at 2nd level.

campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m06-the-writ-and-the-weight/design-notes.md:9: The gate is Renown 13 and 7th level, after **Kolat Towers**, as in the other sixth missions. The villa always precedes it, so the writ now reads the Cassalanter Villa result in a four-row table. Nobody says "contractual counterpart" or "infernal rider", and the family does not send the devil in three of the four rows. The nave is the Halls of Justice, in the Castle Ward, and the hearing gives the night a reason.

campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m06-the-writ-and-the-weight/overview.md:5: > Becomes available when an individual Order of the Gauntlet member reaches Renown 13 and 7th level, after **Kolat Towers**. Seventh level requires all four lair heists, so a party that ran three heists reaches it only after **Vault of Dragons** and plays this mission then. The Vault does not require it. Companions can help with everything except Savra's brief and debrief, which only members attend. A member marked **Quietly Reassigned** gets the note from Brother Hallin Ostrel, and the mission is otherwise unchanged.

campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:39: - **Chosen:** His Chosen become warlords or whispering advisers who tempt rulers to greater cruelty (FRHoF). The wiki names Fzoul Chembryl as his Chosen and the head of his reformed church after 1372 DR (*Forgotten Realms Campaign Setting*, 3e). Chosen in the campaign: none. The campaign history names Fzoul only as the Banite priest who joined Manshoon in 1263 DR.

campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:75: > - **Never reveal:** That Yellowspire is a Banite tower, that its cellar circle leads to Kolat Towers, or that Amath is allied with Manshoon. Until the party marks **Manshoon Named**, even the Doom Raiders say "Floxin's cell" or "the other cell" and call Kolat Towers "the towers in the Trades Ward". Before the party reaches the tower, a player who asks about Bane gets the Overview.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:84: > - **Yellowspire and the Zhentarim split:** Amath is allied with Manshoon's cell, the splinter that peeled off the Doom Raiders' network. The Doom Raiders are the other Zhentarim cell and are at war with it. In **The Yellowspire Job** Davil Starsong sends a Doom Raiders member into the tower to steal a ledger of pass-amulet carriers. Davil tells the member that nobody above him has asked for the job and nobody above him will hear of it.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:85: > - **What the tower is for:** Tashlyn Yafeera tells the Doom Raiders member that the Banites keep the place as a temple and a post office. For Manshoon's cell it is also a fallback position, and the cellar circle gives a route into Kolat Towers that skips the pass-amulet requirement. A pressure plate in the circle chamber alerts Kolat Towers. **Faction Outposts** runs the tower as a mini-heist, **Silencing Skeemo** ends at its door, and **Kolat Towers** can be entered through its circle.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:86: > - **Amath Seccent (Lawful Evil, Human, she/her):** She is a priest of Bane in common clothes, composed and correct. She speaks in courteous, complete sentences, never raises her voice, and treats weakness with controlled contempt. She receives guests as though she were hosting a tea, and the handprints are the only thing in the tower she mentions with warmth. She treats her faith as a settled alignment with Bane, and it predates and outlasts any arrangement with the Zhentarim. If asked, she calls the tower the House of Tyranny and says the neighbors think it a private home, which she prefers.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:89: > - **The turning rite:** In the published adventure the five hold a ritual to break the will of a captive broadsheet publisher, Shan Chien, so that he serves Bane. Amath uses it to turn publishers as part of Manshoon's plan to control the flow of information in the city (`sources/adventure-wdh.json:9749`). **The Yellowspire Job** does not use the rite or Shan Chien.

campaign/setting/deities/cyric.md:34: - **Open worship:** Temples to him are forbidden in most nations and thrive in Amn, where his teaching suits a culture of ambition, self-reliance and "buyer beware" (FRHoF; SCAG, 2014). In the early years of his godhood the Moonsea around Zhentil Keep was the center of his worship, and the wiki says the Zhentarim served then as an extension of his church (2e and 3e sources). No campaign file carries that tie into 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/deities/cyric.md:40: - **Temples the wiki names:** Darkhold, the Zhentarim fortress in the Western Heartlands (*Volo's Guide to the Sword Coast*, 5e); the House of Cyric, a subterranean temple beneath Skullport (*Serpent Kingdoms*, 3e); and the Twin Towers of the Eternal Eclipse, the center of his worship in Amn (*Lands of Intrigue: Amn*, 3e). None is in Waterdeep.

campaign/setting/deities/mystra.md:65: > - **The ordinary Waterdhavian's view:** Voice her as the patron of every spellcaster in the city. A sage, a priest or a regular at the bar says she is the Lady of Mysteries, the god of magic and of the Weave that every spell is drawn from, and that wizards, sages and alchemists favor her. They add that her priests look out for children with a spark and find them a teacher, and that the House of Wonder in the Sea Ward is the finest temple to the gods of magic in the world. Older Waterdhavians tell that she was once the mortal Midnight, raised to godhood on the slopes of Mount Waterdeep at the end of the Time of Troubles, and a sage adds that Cyric is blamed for murdering her in the Spellplague. Anyone who follows city politics knows that the Open Lord, Laeral Silverhand, is one of her daughters. Nobody in this conversation mentions Manshoon.
campaign/setting/deities/mystra.md:77: > - **Campaign ties:** Laeral Silverhand, the House of Wonder in **Fireball!**, and Manshoon's plan. The details are in the GM Notes below. No campaign file ties the dragonward, the Walking Statues or the Blackstaff's office to Mystra, so do not add a link at the table.
campaign/setting/deities/mystra.md:78: > - **Never reveal:** That Manshoon means to use the ancient Weave knowledge in the Stone of Golorr to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra. A player who asks a priest of Mystra about the Stone gets the Overview, and the party's own evidence is the only thing that adds to it.
campaign/setting/deities/mystra.md:89: > - **Manshoon's plan:** Manshoon wants the Stone of Golorr for the ancient Weave knowledge encoded in its aboleth memory. He means to use it to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra, and the vault's gold pays for the preparations. The plan links to **Gralhund Villa** and **Kolat Towers**. In **Gralhund Villa** the party does not learn his name, his goals or the Weave motive, so no priest of Mystra can speak of it before the party's evidence supports it.

campaign/setting/deities/orcus.md:74: > - **Campaign ties:** No campaign file uses this power. No file ties him to the Cassalanters, the Zhentarim or any other faction. The **Kelemvor** page records that his followers "eliminate the scourge of the undead", and the **Myrkul** page lists necromancers and undead as Myrkul's followers, so a table would set Orcus's cults against the first and beside the second (derived). No source says either.
campaign/setting/deities/torm.md:40: - **The Order of the Golden Lion:** This knightly order is based around Tantras, the city with the greatest concentration of Torm worshipers in Faerûn. Its knights each devote themselves to righting one of three Debts. Persecution means aiding good religions, Dereliction means rooting out cults of Bane and routing the Zhentarim, and Destruction means reporting and repairing dead and wild magic (FRHoF). Members wear golden pauldrons carved as lion heads, and a candidate must first complete a holy quest for a temple of Torm. The wiki adds that the pontiff Barriltar Bhandraddon ruled from the Temple of Torm's Coming in Tantras, with no edition tagged. This order is a different body from the Order of the Gauntlet.
campaign/setting/deities/torm.md:72: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Devotion. FRHoF says he is a patron god of many paladins and embodies the tenets of that oath, so the fit is sourced and not derived. A paladin who follows the Order of the Golden Lion would take one of its three Debts, and FRHoF lists cults of Bane and the Zhentarim among the targets of the Debt of Dereliction. The Zhentarim are one of the campaign's four villain factions, but no campaign file uses the Golden Lion.

campaign/setting/deities/tyr.md:82: > - **Lady Gondafrey and Kolat Towers:** Duhlark Kolat trapped a gargoyle imbued with the personality of a human knight of Tyr in a cell at Kolat Towers. In the campaign she was a City Watch captain who tried to breach the towers in 1379 DR, refused to swear Duhlark's oath of silence and has been a prisoner for 113 years. She wants to go on doing her duty as a knight of Tyr from inside the gargoyle's body. Her alignment shifts with the time of day, and the rule is set out in **Kolat Towers**.

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:66: **Jalester Silvermane** (Lawful Good, Cormyrean Human, he/him) is a Lords' Alliance field agent who reports to Laeral and carries a badge of the Watch. campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:72: Several more officers have no Notable Figures page, so they stay as plain text. Hyustus Staget (Lawful Good, Illuskan Human, he/him) is the uptight captain who keeps the peace in the Dock Ward and has been dragging his heels on the Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild, since he reasons that they may as well destroy each other. If he has died, the published adventure replaces him with Cressa Galavarco (Lawful Good, Tethyrian Human, she/her), a veteran. Officers Jonn Bannock (Lawful Neutral, Waterdhavian Human, he/him) and Ysa Terrow (Lawful Neutral, Waterdhavian Human, she/her) work the late shift in **Ten Nights in the City of the Dead**, where they keep the party off the Brandath ground.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:83: > - **Who can be bought:** Corylus Thann, a Masked Lord whom Manshoon's cell blackmails, keeps eight crooked Watch veterans who crack down on anyone who annoys him, and he clears any charge against them. If **Cultists Escaped** is marked in **The Fouled Channel**, the Splinter pays Watch sergeants to ignore reports about the Trades Ward cisterns, and **Vault of Dragons** reads this as reduced Watch cooperation in that ward. In the **Kolat Towers** structure document, Mirt says someone with civic influence has reduced Watch patrol coverage around the towers over eighteen months. Jalester calls the coverage informally diminished and suspects Masked Lord influence that he cannot prove without evidence.

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:65: > - **What anonymity does not protect:** The masks stop a stranger's bribe and do nothing against blackmail by someone who has learned a name. Manshoon keeps a list of five Masked Lords whose identities he knows. campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:66: > - **Who holds which Lord:** Manshoon's cell blackmails Jelenn Urmbrusk and Corylus Thann and forces them to cover up crimes by Manshoon's forces. Jelenn's debt to Manshoon's Zhentarim was borrowed to avoid borrowing from rival nobles, and the Splinter now holds it. Corylus uses crooked Watch veterans against anyone who annoys him and will not accept that he owes the party anything. See **Manshoon's Zhentarim** and **Jelenn Urmbrusk**.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:67: > - **Seats that may open:** Several Masked Lords were assassinated in quick succession some years ago. Yalah Gralhund expected a vacancy, was passed over, and wants a seat. House Gralhund joined Manshoon's Zhentarim partly to learn the Lords' identities and weaknesses. In the published adventure's Xanathar version, Yalah offers to return the Stone to it if it helps her create a vacancy. The Alexandrian files date the killings to 1491 DR and call them the Lord Murders.
