campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:35: - The wiki's account of the first holder: a beholder hired by the Zhentarim to start a cell in Skullport. It cut them off, built its own guild, and by 1357 DR the Guild was a thieves' guild working through freelance agents under four Councilors. Each Councilor supervised one domain: mercenaries, bookkeeping, thievery or blackmail (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar's Thieves' Guild; Waterdeep and the North). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:67: - The Guild has rivals below. A drow mage named Rizzeryl works for House Auvryndar and for the Zhentarim, and he and his wererats harry Guild forces on one of the upper levels (WDMM). Which Zhentarim he serves is not settled for the remix, and Draft Notes lists it.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:72: - The war with Manshoon's Zhentarim is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward. It began when Manshoon's envoys came to Skullport three months before the fireball with Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr, and Xanathar killed them.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:83: > - Nihiloor, the mind flayer, has been psionically dominating Xanathar for the last four months. The Xanathar that the party meets, including the one who killed Manshoon's envoys and sent response teams against the party's allies, has been Nihiloor's instrument. Xanathar does not know.

campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:126: > - **The campaign additions to X33.** A secret door from X6 (DC 17 Wisdom (Perception) check) leads to the room. It holds the remains and effects of every previous holder, evidence that the title is claimed through murder, the original charter signed by the first holder, and a locked coffer with the personal journals of the last three Xanathars. The journals record early dealings with Manshoon's predecessors.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:127: > - **What the party can learn.** The room proves that "Xanathar" is a title and that thirteen holders precede the current one. It implies that the current Xanathar can be replaced, and that someone may already be planning it. The wiki's story of a Zhentarim-hired first holder fits the journals. `[research: wiki only]`

The campaign has Eye #1 arrive with Manshoon's envoys three months before the fireball (`arc-f` line 9), and the vault is unopened. The page follows the campaign.

campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:8: > - **What this is:** The Era page for the Zhentarim, from Manshoon's founding of the Black Network to the two rival cells that now share its name in Waterdeep. It is the fourth of eight eras, after **Bregan D'aerthe's Long Road** and before **A Debt in Blood**.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** The GM reads it before voicing Davil Starsong, Tashlyn Yafeera or any Zhentarim NPC, and before answering a contact's question about which Zhentarim the party has met. Every scene with a "Zhentarim" NPC needs a decision about which cell is present.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:10: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**, **Vault of Dragons**.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:11: > - **Spoiler level:** Everything above the Overview is GM-only. The Overview gives the Black Network as most Waterdhavians know it, through the Doom Raiders, and mentions no clone, no second cell and no Kolat Towers.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:15: **Keywords:** Zhentarim, Black Network, Manshoon, Zhentil Keep, stasis clones, Manshoon Wars, Doom Raiders, Davil Starsong, Kolat Towers, the Splinter
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:17: **Span:** 1261 DR to 1492 DR. The founding date, 1261 DR, comes from the Forgotten Realms Wiki. The Waterdeep chapter runs from 1489 DR, when the Doom Raiders were sent to found a foothold, to the campaign opening on Ches 1, 1492 DR. Manshoon's cell arrived after them, about two years before the campaign. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:19: **Other Names:** The Black Network; the Splinter (the name the factions use for Manshoon's cell); the Two Zhentarims (a GM name from the guides). "The Founding of Chains" is the old era title, and no in-world name for the era is sourced. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:24: > - Manshoon, a wizard-lord of Zhentil Keep, founded the Zhentarim in 1261 DR as a mercantile network and spread it from the Moonsea to the Sword Coast. The Black Network outlived his death in 1370 DR and lost its capital in the century after.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:25: > - Manshoon survived that death through the stasis clone spell and fought his own clones in the Manshoon Wars. The man in Kolat Towers is a clone of a clone who claims to be the true Manshoon, and he keeps a simulacrum in reserve.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:26: > - The Doom Raiders, five retired adventurers, were sent to Waterdeep in 1489 DR to found the Black Network's foothold. They regard themselves as the true Zhentarim of Waterdeep.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:27: > - Manshoon's clone arrived later, took over Kolat Towers and built a cell of his own, the Splinter, in about two years. He peeled off a large part of the Doom Raiders' local network.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:28: > - Manshoon's cell regards the Doom Raiders as deserters who stole his name, and the Doom Raiders are at active internal war with it. The two cells will not cooperate.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:32: - Manshoon was born in Zhentil Keep in 1229 DR and became one of the city's lords. He founded the Zhentarim in 1261 DR, and the Banite priest Fzoul Chembryl joined him in 1263 DR.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:33: - The published adventure calls Manshoon "one of the founders" of the Zhentarim. This page follows the wiki and treats him as the founder.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:34: - Manshoon set his wizards to open trade routes between the Moonsea and the Sword Coast. The Network began as a mercantile concern and grew into a company that trades mercenaries and goods, weapons included.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:35: - Zhentil Keep fell in stages. Its northern half was ruined about 1369 DR after Cyric's fall, and the Shadovar razed the whole city in 1383 DR over the Zhentarim's alliance with the phaerimm.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:38: - The Zhentarim are now reforming a council, and efforts are underway to reclaim and rebuild Zhentil Keep. The Doom Raiders see the vault's gold as the price of a seat on that council.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:39: - Under the leadership of the Pereghost, the wiki says, the Zhentarim changed from a conquering force into a mercenary army that served others. By 1489 DR they were said to have more members than ever and a reputation for service (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Zhentarim; Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide, 5e). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:44: - Manshoon built the stasis clone spell as insurance against his enemies. Over the years he died several times and returned in a new body.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:45: - In 1370 DR Fzoul and Lord Orgauth killed him and found themselves facing a dozen or more clones. Each clone believed he was the real Manshoon and felt driven to destroy the others. These fights are the Manshoon Wars.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:46: - Three clones survived. One rejoined the Zhentarim and conceded leadership to Fzoul, one became the vampire Orbakh in Westgate, and one took refuge in Undermountain with Halaster.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:48: - Simulacra are a separate tool. Manshoon makes copies of himself with the simulacrum spell, and the copy in Kolat Towers has more hit points and less spellcasting than he does.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:49: - The man in Waterdeep is "a clone of a clone". He claims to be the one true Manshoon, as every surviving clone does.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:50: - The Zhentarim outlasted the 1370 DR death. Fzoul led the Network after it, and one Manshoon clone served as a free agent under him.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:51: - The old outline says the Network "retained only his methods" after Manshoon's coherent leadership ended. No source supports this. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:55: - The Doom Raiders were five adventurers who plundered the lairs of liches, which some call "dooms". They gave up adventuring and joined the reformed Zhentarim in the late 1480s.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:57: - They were sent to Waterdeep in 1489 DR to establish a Zhentarim foothold. The published adventure says only that they came "three years ago".
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:62: - Davil wants to end the street violence that Manshoon's cell has caused, because a legitimate criminal enterprise cannot run in a city where an illegitimate one keeps shooting people.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:63: - The Doom Raiders operate openly, meet in taverns and recruit adventurers. They are the first Zhentarim contact the party meets, during **Trollskull Alley**.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:64: - The Doom Raiders believe they are the true Zhentarim of Waterdeep. The old outline says Davil's cell "separates from Manshoon on pragmatic grounds". The sources reverse that direction: the cell came first, and Manshoon's clone peeled off its network.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:66: > *I confess I accepted a drink at the Yawning Portal from Davil Starsong, and that I stayed for the whole of the speech that came with it. His position, delivered with a patience I have rarely met in his trade, was that the Black Network is a trading company first and everything else a long way after (I wrote it down at once, having never heard a Zhentarim explain himself so politely).*
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:69: ### Manshoon's Waterdeep Operation
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:71: - Kolat Towers was built in the late fourteenth century by the wizard brothers Duhlark and Alcedor Kolat. Duhlark added a force field that covers the whole structure and admits only holders of a keyed amulet.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:73: - Manshoon's clone fled Undermountain, found the towers abandoned with the force field intact, and took the amulet-making instructions from Duhlark's study. He spent two years building the Splinter.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:74: - The Splinter is hub and spoke. Kolat Towers is the command center, and the Interrogation House in the North Ward and Yellowspire in the Castle Ward are field offices linked to it by teleportation circles.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:76: - The clone arrived after the Doom Raiders and peeled off a large part of the local network they had built. Through it he learned that Neverember had used the local Zhentarim's resources in the embezzlement.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:78: - The published adventure gives him a different goal: to oust the Open Lord, replace the City Watch with Black Network mercenaries, and then turn on Halaster. The campaign keeps the Weave goal, which is set out on the **Manshoon** villain page.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:79: - Manshoon's supporters among the Zhentarim want Xanathar destroyed and the city's economy in their hands. The Doom Raiders want to expose Manshoon before the authorities drive them out.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:80: - The old outline says the towers were set up out of sight and that Manshoon "read Neverember's ouster as political opportunity". The nearest source for the second claim is the Alexandrian remix, which has him learn of the embezzlement through the Doom Raiders' network. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:84: - Two cells use the name Zhentarim in Waterdeep, and each denies the other's claim. The distinction should become clear to attentive players during **Trollskull Alley**, because the Doom Raiders' professionalism makes Manshoon's operatives land harder when they appear.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:85: - The Doom Raiders call themselves the legitimate Black Network in the city. They are at active internal war with Manshoon's cell, and anyone who joins them is caught in the crossfire.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:86: - Manshoon's cell does not acknowledge the Doom Raiders as Zhentarim. It regards them as deserters who stole his name.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:87: - The cells will not cooperate or share intelligence, and where their operations overlap they undermine each other. Information given to the Doom Raiders does not reach Manshoon's agents.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:88: - The Doom Raiders have a leak of their own. Skeemo Weirdbottle has been feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell since before the party was recruited.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:89: - Factions that speak to players say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:93: The Zhentarim, also called the Black Network, is a mercenary and trading company founded in Zhentil Keep in 1261 DR, a city that has since fallen to ruin. In Waterdeep it is known mainly through the Doom Raiders, a band of retired adventurers who came to the city in 1489 DR and describe themselves as the Zhentarim of Waterdeep. They sell mercenary work and lend money through their businesses, and they welcome adventurers who are looking for contracts. The Black Network has long wanted a place in the city's politics, and the strength of the Masked Lords, the nobility and the guilds has kept it out.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:98: > - Waterdeep holds two Zhentarim organizations, and the Doom Raiders are only one of them. Nobody speaks Manshoon's name to a player until **Manshoon Named** is marked for that listener.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:99: > - The Harpers do not know that the Splinter is Manshoon's cell or that it answers to Kolat Towers. No Harper contact names him.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:100: > - The man in Kolat Towers is a clone who believes he is the true Manshoon, and he keeps a simulacrum in reserve. The old outline asked whether he was the original or a simulacrum, and he is a third thing.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:104: **Tone:** Measured and factual, in the Ember history register. The Doom Raiders are sincere and Manshoon is patient. State each cell's view of the other as that cell's own belief, and give no narrator ruling on which one is the true Zhentarim.

campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:157: > - **Research:** Three claims in the old outline have no source: "a criminal network spanning the Sword Coast", "retained only his methods", and Davil's "explicitly pragmatic" grounds. The Doom Raiders' creed on this page comes from the Doom Raiders organization page. Also unsupported is the claim that "most of Waterdeep" knows them as "the Zhentarim". The campaign says only that they operate openly.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:158: > - **Conflicts:** Direction of the split. `history.md` line 39 has Davil's cell separating from Manshoon. `organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md` line 13, `villains/manshoon.md` line 32, `guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md` line 7, `structural-rules.md` line 9, the Alexandrian Part 1 p.16 and `adventure-wdh.json` lines 31916 and 1445-1447 all say the Doom Raiders came first and Manshoon peeled off their network. The newer pages win.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:159: > - **Conflicts:** Clones or simulacra. `history.md` line 38 says Manshoon "died and was replaced by his own simulacra". `notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md` line 23 and `organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md` line 15 say clones, with simulacra as a separate tool. The clone wording wins.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:160: > - **Conflicts:** Clone survivors. The body keeps the 3e account that three clones survived 1370 DR. The wiki's later account is that the 1383 DR razing killed the Zhentarim clone and Fzoul, the Undermountain clone died in the Spellplague, and only the vampire Orbakh remained (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Zhentarim and Manshoon; Forgotten Realms Campaign Guide, 4e). The published adventure wins: its Waterdeep clone is "long thought dead" (`adventure-wdh.json` line 472), which is the Undermountain clone, and the man in Kolat Towers is that clone. "Three survived" describes the state after 1370 DR only.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:162: > - **Settled by ruling 1:** The ward of Kolat Towers. `adventure-wdh.json` lines 26797, 32215 and 32218 say the Southern Ward, and line 560 says the Trades Ward. The campaign follows the Southern Ward, and `arc-i-kolat-towers.md` and `guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md` line 73 now say so.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:163: > - **Conflicts:** Arrival timing. The wiki and `adventure-wdh.json` line 31916 date the Doom Raiders to 1489 DR, while the Alexandrian Part 1 p.16 needs a local Zhentarim network during Neverember's 1467-1489 tenure. The wiki ties Neverember to the Zhentarim through his advisor Rian Nightshade (The Rise of Tiamat, 5e), which could reconcile the two. This page does not adopt it.

campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:41: - **Eye #1, Xanathar:** Manshoon's Zhentarim stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. About three months before the fireball, Manshoon sent two lieutenants to Skullport with it as a gift. Xanathar ate them and kept the Eye, and Ott Steeltoes put it in the bowl of Sylgar, Xanathar's goldfish, because it was a pretty color. Xanathar does not know what the Eye is.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:43: - **Eye #3, Jarlaxle:** It began in Renaer's locket. Manshoon's cell seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse. Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried the Eye off with the rest of the back office, and nobody in the Guild recognized it. Bregan D'aerthe agents posing as guards lifted it from a strongbox in the hideout before dawn, and it reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout.

campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:58: - **The sunlight:** The *daylight* spell suffices, and the structure doc rules that its light counts as sunlight under the 2024 rules. Otherwise twenty large steel mirrors (400 gp in all) and several hours of alignment will bring sunlight down.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:60: - **Learning the ceremony:** Four independent paths lead to it. The Stone's vision during the Full Awakening gives its shape, in the lines "Three keys ye seek. Strike scale of wyrm in light of day. As the vault bears witness, so shall it ope to mithral's strike." Kalain's notes in **Kalain's Tower** give it in full, in Neverember's hand. The Cassalanters know it from their own investigation and share it if the party's standing with them allows. Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game from **Kolat Towers** (area E9) gives an outline and a partial Elvish command phrase.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:37: - **Vevette Blackwater:** Seffia approached her to open a channel between the Cassalanters and the Zhentarim, and Vevette refused, suspecting a trap. Unresolved: no contact since, and Vevette still carries Seffia's visiting card, which gives the Windmill's address.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:14: **Emotions.** Jelenn most often feels desperate anxiety about debt, wounded pride, bitter regret over her failed investments, suspicious hostility toward strangers, fraying dignity, and private terror of Manshoon's leverage.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:16: **Motivations.** Jelenn wants to escape Manshoon's blackmail, repay her debts without any other noble learning of them, and reclaim the financial independence that her bad investments destroyed.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Jelenn is a proud noblewoman in her fifties who travels with sixteen guards and has no time for strangers. To those she trusts, she is a Masked Lord who borrowed from the Zhentarim because she feared gossip more than gangsters. Deep down, she knows the decision was catastrophic, and Manshoon's blackmail is the price of her pride.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:24: **Morale.** In a confrontation, Jelenn would demand the party leave and threaten dire consequences. Her threat is mostly bluster. If freed from Manshoon, she grants a Special Favor. If blackmailed by the party instead, she pretends to cooperate while secretly working against them.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:26: **Relationships.** Jelenn is a Masked Lord of Waterdeep, a debtor of the Zhentarim (borrowed to avoid borrowing from rival nobles), and a blackmail victim of Manshoon's cell whose name appears on the Kolat Towers blackmail list.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Davil is the Master of Opportunities and Negotiations: a sun elf who makes friends easily, negotiates deals with grace even while drunk, speaks with a lilt he attributes to his elven heritage, and wears a charm bracelet hung with small trophies from monsters the Doom Raiders have killed. He conducts business from a curtained room on the Yawning Portal's second floor, with a permanent suite on the third. To those he trusts, he is a retired adventurer who joined the Zhentarim with his companions and frames the whole enterprise as helping people in need. Deep down, he withholds explanation and lets things sit, showing interest without revealing why, and he was fond of Skeemo in a way he does not say aloud.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:26: **Relationships.** Davil is the cell leader of the Doom Raiders (Waterdeep Zhentarim), the faction contact for Zhentarim-aligned PCs, a permanent resident at the Yawning Portal (second floor business room, third floor suite), a colleague of Istrid, Tashlyn, Ziraj, and the traitor Skeemo Weirdbottle, and the unknowing target of Nihiloor's intellect devourer, with Meloon watching him and waiting for a clean opportunity to kill him without witnesses.

To those she trusts, she arm-wrestles patrons because people relax after losing to her. Deep down, she tells the story of the slaver ship she was on before the Doom Raiders found her like an old weather report, wanting nothing for the telling.

She says "the Splinter" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the party member she is speaking to, and only then says his name.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/01-yalah-gralhund.md:24: **Morale.** In a confrontation, Yalah would feign innocence, redirect blame, and rely on Hrabbaz and her Zhentarim retainers. She does not fight unless cornered, and even then she negotiates.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/03-hrabbaz.md:26: **Relationships.** Hrabbaz is the bodyguard of both Gralhunds (shared, though more loyal to Yalah), and a half-orc whose impeccable manners and wardrobe make him the most unsettling presence in a villa full of Zhentarim.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:16: **Motivations.** Dalakhar wanted to deliver the Stone of Golorr to the party (planning to reclaim it later for Neverember), avoid the Zhentarim and Xanathar agents closing in, and serve the man he believed was Waterdeep's rightful Open Lord.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Dalakhar was a small gnome with dry waste on his boots and sewer-stink on his cloak. To those he trusted, he was Neverember's most reliable agent, the one who succeeded where many others had failed. Deep down, he was afraid that the Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild were close to catching him, and he was right.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/18-lady-gondafrey.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Kolat Towers**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/18-lady-gondafrey.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Lady Gondafrey is a gargoyle with a woman's face and stone wings, locked in a cell in Kolat Towers, lawful good by alignment and humanoid by disposition. To those who speak with her, she retains the protocols and memories of a City Watch captain who was investigating a suspicious wizard when he captured her — and she still considers herself on duty. Deep down, once per day she feels the gargoyle's chaotic evil nature claw at the surface, and she has never told anyone how often that battle is close.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/18-lady-gondafrey.md:24: **Morale.** When lawful good, she fights with a knight's discipline: protecting others, targeting combatants, holding ground. When chaotic evil (before dawn; lawful good for four hours after dawn, then drifting neutral, per the **Kolat Towers** entry-timing rule), she attacks indiscriminately and attempts to orchestrate deaths, including the characters'.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/18-lady-gondafrey.md:26: **Relationships.** Lady Gondafrey is a City Watch captain from 1379 DR who became a prisoner of Duhlark Kolat and subsequently of Manshoon, a potential **Kolat Towers** ally whose alignment is unstable on a daily basis, and a witness to decades of Kolat Towers' history.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/18-lady-gondafrey.md:30: Lady Gondafrey is a gargoyle with a woman's face and stone wings who speaks with the protocols and memories of a City Watch captain, imprisoned in Kolat Towers for over a century.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:1: # Manshoon
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:8: > - **Villain profile:** see **Manshoon** in Villains
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:9: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**, **Vault of Dragons**; **The Talking Mare**, **The Dead Drop**, **The Doppelganger Auditions**, **A Friend's House**, **The Stone's Other Master**, **The Poisoned Delivery**, **Silencing Skeemo**, **The Yellowspire Job**, **Ziraj's Last Hunt**, **The Exile's Return**, **The Red Wizard's Price**, **An Audience with the Open Lord**, **Consulting Hlam**, **Smoke in the Tower**, **The Undercliff Scarecrows**, **The Doppelganger Problem**, **The Fouled Channel**, **Breaking Up the Brawl**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:13: **Resonance.** Manshoon should inspire intellectual dread with his patient precision, frustrated rage from the party's inability to pin him down, and dark fascination with a centuries-old wizard who lost an arm to Halaster Blackcloak and refuses to stop scheming.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:15: **Emotions.** Manshoon most often feels cold patient calculation, contemptuous superiority, precise anger reserved for specific targets, possessive ambition, frustrated pride about his artificial arm, and the absolute certainty that he is the one true Manshoon.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:17: **Motivations.** Manshoon wants to acquire the Stone of Golorr for the ancient Weave knowledge encoded in its aboleth memory, use that knowledge to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra, fund his preparations with the vault's gold, and deepen his hold over Masked Lords Corylus Thann and Jelenn Urmbrusk into a full Council majority.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:19: **Inspirations.** When playing Manshoon, channel Magneto (*X-Men*), Emperor Palpatine (*Star Wars*), and Moriarty (*Sherlock Holmes*).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:23: **Persona.** To the world, Manshoon does not exist; he is never seen entering or leaving Kolat Towers, using a teleportation circle for all movement. To those he trusts, he is a surviving clone of the Zhentarim founder who fled Undermountain after Halaster amputated his left arm, crafted an artificial replacement, and now orchestrates a bid for total control of Waterdeep. Deep down, he claims to be the one true Manshoon, as all surviving clones do. He creates simulacra of himself and takes great pains to conceal his identity.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:25: **Morale.** In combat, Manshoon lets his simulacrum absorb the opening volley while he casts from behind it. He prefers to capture rather than kill (prisoners answer questions; corpses do not), and this habit creates tactical openings the party can exploit. Only those who breach his extradimensional sanctum see his full power.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:27: **Relationships.** Manshoon is the leader of the Zhentarim splinter cell, the employer of Urstul Floxin, Sidra Romeir, and Vevette Blackwater, a blackmailer of Masked Lords Jelenn Urmbrusk and Corylus Thann, and a clone-wizard whose response teams are deliberately the weakest in muscle because drawing attention is the one mistake he will not make twice.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:31: A name the party does not have yet. The hard-edged Zhentarim operatives working Waterdeep's streets answer to someone, and none of them will say who.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**; **Gralhund Villa** (location)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:16: **Motivations.** Urstul wants to recover the Stone of Golorr for Manshoon, maintain operational control of the field team, and survive encounters with the party by any means necessary, including hostage-taking.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Urstul is a large, heavyset man in his forties who treats every room as his own. To those he works with, he is Manshoon's chief field operative who runs both the Dalakhar capture attempt and the Gralhund Villa operation. Deep down, he will not divulge his master's name unless magically compelled, and he keeps a collection of black flying snakes as couriers because he trusts animals more than people.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:26: **Relationships.** Urstul is Manshoon's chief field operative, a temporary occupant of Gralhund Villa (where he treats Yalah as an underling), and the antagonist who drives **Fireball!** and **Gralhund Villa** before the party can identify his master.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:30: A large, heavyset man in his forties who carries himself as though every room belongs to him. He works for the Zhentarim and makes no effort to hide it, though he will not name who gives him orders.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Faction Outposts**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**; **Silencing Skeemo**, **The Yellowspire Job**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:16: **Motivations.** Vevette wants to serve Manshoon's interests while positioning herself for advancement, keep the Stone in Zhentarim hands, and avoid imprisonment by exploiting the law.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:26: **Relationships.** Vevette is Manshoon's most trusted field lieutenant, the coordinator of strike team deployments from Kolat Towers, and a swashbuckler whose operational relationship with the Cassalanters consists of a rebuffed contact and a kept visiting card.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:8: > - **Featured in:** **Faction Outposts**, **Kolat Towers**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:12: **Resonance.** Sidra should inspire wariness with her unhurried calm (she has seen worse than the party and it shows), respect for the way she runs Kolat Towers like a barracks, and frustration with a loyalty to Manshoon that no argument will crack.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:16: **Motivations.** Sidra wants to maintain Kolat Towers' security, protect the teleport ring system, and ensure that Manshoon's operations run without interruption.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Sidra is a Calishite veteran who assumes intruding PCs are guests of Manshoon, since Kolat Towers' force field screens out riffraff. She offers water, asks their business, and watches their hands. To those she trusts, she holds the teleport rings when lieutenants leave the Towers and hands them to replacements without ceremony when the previous holder does not come back. Deep down, she has outlasted every other lieutenant Manshoon has employed. She does not wonder why.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:26: **Relationships.** Sidra is Manshoon's Kolat Towers lieutenant, the keeper of the teleporter ring system, and the only person in the cell whose loyalty Manshoon has never tested, because testing it would mean he does not already know the answer.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:30: A Calishite veteran who staffs the ground floor of Kolat Towers. She treats unexpected visitors with professional courtesy — offering water, asking their business, and watching their hands.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/05-agorn-fuoco.md:6: > - *Human bard (Manshoon lieutenant), neutral evil*. Stat block: **Bard**.

To those who capture him, he offers his pass-amulet, Amath Seccent's address, Manshoon's general operational structure, and whatever else occurs to him — starting immediately and working outward from least to most useful, in the order most likely to buy another minute. Deep down, he would rather be playing music.

To those who investigate her, she has no suspicious contacts, no unusual expenses, and no detectable magical connection to the Splinter's cell. Characters say "the Splinter" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for them, and only then connect her to Manshoon or to Kolat Towers (**Smoke in the Tower**). Deep down, the resonance disruptor is already in place, and she is counting days.

To those who enter the kitchen through the wrong door, he asks whether they have eaten, what they would prefer, and whether he should let Lord Manshoon know they have arrived. Deep down, he wears the teleporter ring because Manshoon trusts him, and the intruders always seem to respond well to the food.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:24: **Morale.** In a fight, Manafret retreats to a defensible position, calls for help through whatever means are available, and uses his halfling size to make himself a difficult target. He does not use the teleporter ring except on Manshoon's orders.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:26: **Relationships.** Manafret is Manshoon's Kolat Towers cook and a teleporter ring holder, a lieutenant whose threat is contextual (his ring bypasses the pass-amulet system), and a man whose hospitality toward intruders has probably prevented more deaths than his employer would prefer.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:16: **Motivations.** Kaevja wants to earn her continued place in Manshoon's organization, protect the sanctum against unauthorized entry, and cast the sending that accelerates reinforcements before she is incapacitated.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:22: **Persona.** To the world — specifically to anyone who has breached E2 and triggered the Mask discharge — Kaevja is the mage at E3, already reaching for her component pouch. To Manshoon, she is the sanctum's most reliable early-warning mechanism, valued more for the sending she can cast than for the spells she throws in combat. Deep down, she serves Manshoon because he is the most capable operator she has encountered, and working for the most capable operator available is, in her estimation, the rational choice until demonstrated otherwise.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:24: **Morale.** Kaevja's first action is always the sending — to Manshoon's current location and to E8 — not combat. If she sends successfully and the party's attention is fully on her, she uses the earth elemental gem and fights behind it. If the party eliminates her silently before she can act, she contributes nothing; if she succeeds, she has made the entire operation significantly harder.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:26: **Relationships.** Kaevja is Sidra Romeir's sanctum counterpart in E3, Manshoon's primary early-warning mechanism, a mage whose communications role matters more than her combat capability, and the party's most time-sensitive priority target in the extradimensional sanctum.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:30: A mage stationed in the inner sanctum of Kolat Towers. She is already reaching for her component pouch the moment anyone enters the room.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/12-havia-quickknife.md:14: **Emotions.** Havia most often feels settled conviction, physical readiness she has to consciously contain, absolute certainty in her service to Manshoon, controlled anticipation in combat, and no ambivalence of any kind about the work.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/12-havia-quickknife.md:30: A small halfling woman seated in the formal audience chamber of Kolat Towers' inner sanctum. Her stance marks her as someone trained far past what her size would suggest.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/13-mookie-plush.md:16: **Motivations.** Mookie wants to serve Manshoon completely. His warmth is not a tactic; it is simply who he is. These two things coexist without contradiction, from his perspective.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/13-mookie-plush.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Mookie Plush is the friendlier of the two halflings in the audience chamber — he smiles easily, seems genuinely interested in who the party is, and gives a distinct impression that he would rather this did not have to happen. To anyone who probes this affect, it does not crack because there is nothing beneath it to crack: he is genuinely warm, genuinely loyal, and genuinely willing to kill everyone in the room. Deep down, he found what he was looking for when Manshoon offered him a place, and the finding resolved him completely.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim

campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:14: **Emotions.** Yorn most often feels steady patience, a physical readiness he carries the way other people carry stillness, satisfaction in his particular utility to Manshoon's operation, and a focused clarity that descends once he understands a situation is no longer going to be avoided.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:16: **Motivations.** Yorn wants to do the work Manshoon brought him in for — enforcement, extraction, the maintenance of internal fear as much as external threat — and to do it well enough that his value is never questioned.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:22: **Persona.** To the world — specifically to anyone brought into the sanctum who is about to be questioned — Yorn the Terror is the fact of what is going to happen. He does not monologue. He does not explain himself. To Manshoon's lieutenants, he is the asset deployed when a situation has moved past the point at which conversation is the primary tool. Deep down, he has been called "the Terror" long enough that he has stopped thinking of it as a name and started thinking of it as a description.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:26: **Relationships.** Yorn is a Manshoon Splinter enforcer stationed in the extradimensional sanctum whose role in the organization is the maintenance of internal fear as much as the exercise of direct violence, and a threat that compounds significantly if encountered late in the operation when the reinforcement timer has already thinned the party's resources.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:30: An enforcer stationed in Kolat Towers' extradimensional sanctum. He does not introduce himself. The epithet "the Terror" was not self-applied, and the professionals who gave it to him knew what they were talking about.

campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:1: # The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:6: > - **Campaign role:** Player faction — the legitimate Black Network cell in Waterdeep, here since 1489 DR and at active war with Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:9: > - **Featured in:** **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Kolat Towers**, **Vault of Dragons**
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:11: > *Contact: Davil Starsong, always at the Yawning Portal. He keeps a room upstairs and does all his business in the taproom, which he has decided is safer than a private office and more honest than a warehouse. He's a sun elf who looks like a man who has decided that charm is both a genuine quality and an excellent professional tool, and has spent enough decades cultivating the combination that he can no longer tell where one ends and the other begins. He uses an elven lute as a spellcasting focus and has it slung across his back during every meeting. He negotiates with grace and aplomb even when he's been drinking, which is most of the time. He is genuinely invested in ending the street violence Manshoon's cell has caused. His investment in ending the violence is the position of someone who cannot run a legitimate criminal enterprise in a city where an illegitimate one keeps shooting people.*
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:15: > *After Mission 2, the City Watch arrests Davil on suspicion of involvement in the Gralhund Villa incident. Contact passes to **Tashlyn Yafeera**, who communicates exclusively by flying snake. She is Master of Arms and Mercenaries and serves as Captain of the City Guard at the South Gate under Vorondar Levelstone, a dwarf magister whose trust she has carefully built over two years. She is precise and carries sharper intelligence than Davil, because she has been spending the past year building sources inside Manshoon's cell rather than business contacts in the Trades Ward. Her briefings use fewer words and expect more inference.*
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:23: The Doom Raiders are a cell of experienced Zhentarim operators who arrived in Waterdeep in 1489 DR, the first Zhentarim cell in the city, and built a network of commercial fronts they genuinely believe in. They are professional criminals who prefer contracts to confrontations and sincerely believe the Black Network works best when it operates like a legitimate enterprise. Their agreements hold. Their violence is purposeful, never gratuitous.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:25: The Doom Raiders and Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter cell are at active war. Manshoon's cell came later and peeled off the Doom Raiders' network, and the two cells despise each other: information shared with the Doom Raiders will not reach Manshoon's agents. However, characters who join the Doom Raiders are joining the older cell and will inevitably be caught in the crossfire as Manshoon moves to eliminate Davil's cell entirely. This is a structural feature of the campaign.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:27: **Primary Contact:** Davil Starsong — a sun elf retired adventurer who leads the Doom Raiders' Waterdeep operation. Charming, direct, and genuinely invested in ending the street violence Manshoon's cell has caused. Tashlyn Yafeera handles the cell's business whenever Davil is unavailable.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:29: **Secondary Contact:** Tashlyn Yafeera — takes over mission briefings after Davil's arrest, communicating by flying snake. She has sharper intelligence on Manshoon's faction than Davil and her assignments reflect it.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:38: - **Skeemo Weirdbottle** — Master of Magic; runs Weirdbottle's Concoctions in the Trades Ward. Traitor feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell. See the **Skeemo Weirdbottle** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:45: Davil's cell sees an enormous opportunity in the half-million dragons — efforts are underway to reclaim and rebuild Zhentil Keep, and that money could buy the Doom Raiders a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council. PCs who recover the gold and hand it to the Doom Raiders are effectively buying their patrons into continental leadership. This is a real choice with real consequences.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:53: The Doom Raiders are a cell of experienced Zhentarim operators running commercial fronts in Waterdeep — professional criminals who prefer contracts to confrontations. They are at active war with a rival Zhentarim cell that peeled off their network, which makes every piece of intelligence the party brings them valuable and every mission a potential move in a larger fight.
campaign/setting/organizations/08-xanathars-guild.md:26: - **Ott Steeltoes** — Dwarf cult fanatic (Zuggtmoy), neutral evil. Guild field agent, currently held prisoner in the Zhentarim Interrogation House. Wears a leather skullcap stitched with fake beholder eyestalks. If rescued and cultivated, he can lead the PCs toward Xanathar's Lair. See the **Ott Steeltoes** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:1: # Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:7: > - **Contacts:** **Avareen Windrivver**, **Agorn Fuoco**, **Vevette Blackwater** (field operatives); **Sidra Romeir** (Kolat Towers)
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:8: > - **Villain profile:** see **Manshoon** in Villains
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:9: > - **Featured in:** **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Kolat Towers**
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:13: **A note on the Zhentarim:** Two cells operate in Waterdeep. Davil Starsong's **Doom Raiders** are the player-joinable faction covered on **The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)** organization page. Manshoon's splinter is a completely separate organization that peeled off from the Doom Raiders' local network when he returned to Waterdeep. The two groups dislike each other. When players ally with the Zhentarim through Davil, they're working with the Doom Raiders, not Manshoon. The villains in this section answer only to Manshoon.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:15: Manshoon is a centuries-old wizard, a clone of a clone of the original Black Network's founder, who claims, as all surviving Manshoon clones do, to be the one true Manshoon. The gold means nothing to him as wealth. He wants the Stone of Golorr for what it knows: the Stone is a transformed aboleth, and encoded in its centuries of absorbed memory is ancient knowledge of the Weave's structure. Manshoon intends to use that knowledge to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra. The vault is an instrument. The Stone is the goal.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:17: The 500,000 gold dragons matter for what they buy: a private army, bribed magisters, time. Two Masked Lords on the Council are already compromised — Corylus Thann through scandal, Jelenn Urmbrusk through debt — and voting Manshoon's way without knowing why their interests have aligned. This leverage is his most carefully guarded intelligence. A character who brings evidence of the blackmail to Laeral Silverhand or the full Lords' Council collapses Manshoon's political position immediately, which is why the documentation lives at Kolat Towers and nowhere else.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:19: He keeps a simulacrum in Kolat Towers while Manshoon watches from safer ground. His approach reflects his temperament: precision, patience, no unnecessary violence. The Zhentarim response teams are the weakest in raw muscle of any faction. This is deliberate. Drawing too much attention is the one mistake Manshoon won't make twice.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:23: - **Manshoon** — Human wizard (clone), lawful evil. See the **Manshoon** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:24: - **Sidra Romeir** — Calishite human veteran, lawful evil. Manshoon's most trusted lieutenant, stationed at Kolat Towers. Holds the teleport rings when lieutenants leave the Towers. If captured lieutenants are killed, Sidra gives their rings to Kaevja. See the **Sidra Romeir** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:25: - **Vevette Blackwater** (DH p. 216) — Human swashbuckler, lawful evil. A swashbuckler with fiery red hair streaked platinum that can glow in the dark on her command. Charming in public, cruel in private. Carries *Seffia Naelryke's Visiting Card*: Seffia approached Vevette to open a Cassalanter–Zhentarim communication channel, but Vevette rebuffed her, suspecting a trap. See the **Vevette Blackwater** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:26: - **Agorn Fuoco** (DH p. 195) — Human bard (Manshoon lieutenant), neutral evil. A bard and hollow coward who sends his thugs into danger while hanging back. Will barter his pass-amulet to Kolat Towers for his own life without hesitation. Carries a *Report to Agorn Fuoco*: intelligence confirming that Korgstrod's duergar operate out of Orb Confectioners on Rising Ride. See the **Agorn Fuoco** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:28: - **Avareen Windrivver** — Human spy (Zhentarim interrogator), lawful evil. Spy maintaining the Interrogation House alongside Zorbog. Carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers. See the **Avareen Windrivver** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:29: - **Zorbog Jyarkoth** — Human thug (Zhentarim), neutral evil. Thug, Avareen's partner at the Interrogation House. See the **Zorbog Jyarkoth** page in Notable Figures.

campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:31: - **Vira Solkan** — Human mage (Manshoon plant), neutral evil. See the **Vira Solkan** page in Notable Figures.

campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:1: # Manshoon (Zhentarim Splinter Cell)
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:7: ### Manshoon (Zhentarim Splinter Cell)
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:9: **Purpose:** Distinguishes Manshoon clearly from the Doom Raiders and gives the GM the tools to play the campaign's most intellectually dangerous villain — the one who is always further ahead than anyone believes.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:11: > **The Two Zhentarims — GM Note:** Manshoon's splinter cell and Davil Starsong's Doom Raiders are entirely separate organizations. They share a name and a history and nothing else. Never conflate them in NPC dialogue. See also: GM Guide Session Zero; the **Organizations** pages.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:17: | 1 | The Shadow | Acts I–II | Absent; only his lieutenants are visible; PCs fight "the Zhentarim" without knowing Manshoon is a distinct player |
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:19: | 3 | The Simulacrum Problem | **Kolat Towers** | Cornered; the performance of calm drops; the simulacrum fights while the real Manshoon exits |
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:22: - Phase 1 → 2: PCs identify Manshoon specifically by name or signature, requiring at least two separate intelligence threads to connect
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:23: - Phase 2 → 3: PCs penetrate Kolat Towers or destroy a significant portion of his operation
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:33: | Xanathar | Rival | Useful; the Guild's visibility keeps the Watch occupied; when the beholder becomes a direct obstacle, Manshoon has a plan |
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:34: | Jarlaxle | Mutual wariness | The only Grand Game player Manshoon takes seriously; non-interference arrangement, fragile; Jarlaxle has better intelligence and both know it |
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:35: | The Cassalanters | Missed opportunity | Their infernal connections could have been leveraged; Manshoon chose independence over shared control |
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:40: 1. **Acquire the Stone of Golorr** — the Stone is a transformed aboleth encoded with ancient Weave knowledge; Manshoon means to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra; the vault's gold funds the process; method: Stone recovery → Weave absorption; holds no Eye — Xanathar killed the envoys who delivered Eye #1 as a gift and kept it; needs the Stone plus any two Eyes; contingency: destroy the vault rather than cede it; connects to **Gralhund Villa** and **Kolat Towers**
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:42: 3. **The Simulacrum Insurance** — the simulacrum is not a backup; it is the primary survival strategy; defeating Manshoon in combat may defeat only his copy; connects to **Kolat Towers** design requirement
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:44: **Key design note:** The simulacrum reveal needs to be timed carefully. Too early, and it deflates the Kolat Towers raid. Too late, and it feels like a cheat. Target: players discover it during the raid itself, as a revelation that changes how they approach the final confrontation.

campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:17: Xanathar is a beholder and the crime lord of **Xanathar's Guild**, a paranoid intelligence that has built a criminal empire around a terror of being vulnerable and wears the trappings of a monster because the trappings are convenient. Its core motive is to prove its supremacy and remove every threat to it, which means taking the Vault of Dragons, destroying Manshoon and keeping Skullport's money flowing.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:51: | Manshoon | Rival | Active enemy; Xanathar has ordered Manshoon killed twice; both failed; this is accelerating the paranoia |
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:58: - The war with Manshoon is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward, grinding on with the kill order that has failed twice.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:63: - **Eliminate Manshoon.** The splinter cell incursion is a personal insult. Method: Guild response teams targeting Zhentarim operations. There is no contingency, and this failure is part of what drives Phase 3.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:95: - **Manshoon** and **Jarlaxle** (Villains): the rival and the unidentified player in the Relationships table
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:109: > - **Conflicts:** The Manshoon relationship is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward. `running-the-villains.md` line 71 still says "Cold war".
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:25: The trail begins in the Dock Ward. Three independent paths converge on a single warehouse on Candle Lane: a deep gnome shopkeeper who witnessed the kidnapping and will talk if given something purple or persuaded; dockworker regulars at the Skewered Dragon who know the warehouse address without prompting; and a trail of Floon's dropped blue pearl beads running from Old Xoblob Shop's doorstep to the mouth of Candle Lane. En route, the party crosses a City Watch crime scene: three arrested Zhentarim thugs kneeling in the street, black winged snake tattoos on their necks, while wagons arrive for the bodies.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:27: Manshoon's Zhentarim hold the warehouse. Five of Urstul Floxin's thugs grabbed Renaer Neverember and Floon outside the Old Xoblob Shop two nights ago, brought them here, and questioned Renaer about his father's embezzled hoard. They took his mourning locket. Renaer believed it held a keepsake from his mother; it hid an Eye of the Stone of Golorr in a sealed compartment. The Eye was delivered to Kolat Towers before the Xanathar Guild raid killed most of the cell. Four kenku now search the wreckage. Renaer hid when the raid hit and is still hiding in a back room. Floon was mistaken for Renaer and dragged through a trapdoor into the sewer. Renaer, once found, tells the party what he saw: Xanathar agents, circle-eye tattoos, Floon unconscious over one man's shoulder, the trapdoor in the cellar floor. Three more clues in the warehouse confirm the route. They follow.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:45: Yagra's line is the party's earliest signal that the Dock Ward killing was personal — and that the faction at that bar is not the same faction that Durnan will eventually introduce in the form of Davil Starsong. Two Zhentarim cells operate under the same symbol; Yagra's Doom Raiders and Urstul Floxin's Splinter are enemies in everything but name. This distinction is introduced through lived conflict, before any NPC explains it.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:77: *Faction Visibility.* Doom Raiders: Yagra, flying snake tattoo on her neck, Zhentarim pendant worn openly. Xanathar Guild: Krentz and four thugs with eye-shaped tattoos on shaved heads. Bregan D'Aerthe: the silent drow observer at the balcony table. Manshoon's Zhentarim: not present at the Yawning Portal; the Dock Ward killing Yagra referenced is their work.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:93: - **Blood in the Streets.** En route from the Yawning Portal into the Dock Ward — before the party reaches the Xoblob Shop or the Skewered Dragon — they cross a City Watch crime scene. A dozen guards have arrested three surviving Zhentarim thugs from a street brawl: black leather armor, hands on heads, flying snake tattoos visible on necks and forearms. The Watch waves the party off, but the tattoos are in plain view. These are **Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim**, not the Doom Raiders; they report to Kolat Towers, not to Davil Starsong. A DC 13 Intelligence (History) check with any criminal, faction, or guild background identifies the tattoo as Zhentarim; distinguishing which cell is not possible from a tattoo alone.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:95: - **Skewered Dragon.** A dim Dock Ward dive owned and operated by **Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim** as an informal ward headquarters. Regulars here remember Volo and Floon from last night. After Volo left, Floon stayed and met Renaer Neverember. They drank, played Three-Dragon Ante, and left together around midnight. Five men followed them out. "Those men haven't come back," says one regular. "Flying snake tattoos. They keep a warehouse on Candle Lane." This clue delivers Revelation #1. The regulars will also name Candle Lane if pressed, giving the party both revelations in a single stop; this is not a formal third path to Revelation #2 per the Three Clue Rule, but it is the fastest route for parties who follow Volo's lead. The current regulars are Splinter men on their own ground; the party is in unfriendly territory if they push too hard without social skill.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:107: *Proactive Failsafes.* If the party fails to find any path to the warehouse (unlikely given four independent routes): (a) A pair of Manshoon's Splinter thugs approaches the party at the Skewered Dragon and warns them to stop asking questions about the Renaer kidnapping — naming the subject they should be investigating. (b) A Splinter squad arrives on Candle Lane while the party is nearby and fights a group of kenku in the street directly in front of the warehouse, effectively delivering the address by combat.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:109: *Faction Visibility.* Xanathar Guild: yellow chalk territory marks (passive). Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim: under arrest at the crime scene, or at the Skewered Dragon, or approaching to intimidate. Doom Raiders: not present in the Dock Ward; the bar brawl at the Yawning Portal established the faction fracture.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:115: ### Scene 3: The Zhentarim Warehouse (Candle Lane)
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:121: *Location Overview.* The warehouse sits at the back of a yard behind a high fence; the gate is unlocked. Three entry points: front door, loading bay, painted-over window — all locked (DC 12 Dexterity with thieves' tools, DC 10 Strength [Athletics] to force). A black winged snake is painted above the front door handle; DC 10 Intelligence identifies it as the Zhentarim symbol. Four kenku occupy the building — left by Xanathar to kill any Zhentarim who return. Renaer Neverember is in area Z2 (storage closet), having slipped his bonds and hidden before the kenku began their search. The building is Manshoon's Zhentarim property. The Doom Raiders have no presence here.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:125: - **Z1 — Main Room.** Tables and chairs overturned. Twelve corpses along the walls: five Zhentarim thugs (flying snake tattoos on neck or forearm) and seven Xanathar Guild thugs (one has a circle with ten spokes on their right palm — Xanathar's mark). Rapiers and daggers lying nearby. Crates of weapons, rations, boots, and black uniforms. Four kenku are here unless they heard the party enter, in which case they hide behind overturned furniture. Kenku speak only in mimicked voices; DC 10 Charisma (Intimidation) to force useful repetition — captured kenku say, in three different voices: "Xanathar sends its regards" (deep, orcish-accented); "Tie up the pretty boy in the back room!" and "Follow the yellow signs in the sewers" (thin, nasally); "No time to loot the place. Just get him to the boss" (scratchy). Characters attempting to enter without announcing themselves make a group DC 13 Dexterity (Stealth) check against the kenku's passive Perception of 13. If the kenku heard noise before entry (forced door, combat in the yard), the DC rises to 16.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:129: - **Z5 — Offices.** A suite of offices used for Zhentarim cell planning. Desks, chairs, bare shelves. Steel alarm bells above each office door — wired to the Z3 secret door, ringing loudly when it is opened. Harmless rats. One paper bird (magic item, WDH Appendix A) on a desk. This is the room where Zhentarim agents discovered the Eye of the Stone of Golorr hidden inside Renaer's mourning locket. The locket still lies here — an elaborate ivory case on a broken chain, its secret compartment pried open and empty. DC 12 Wisdom (Perception) to spot it under a desk.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:131: *Finding Renaer.* Renaer (see Appendix A) speaks freely once the kenku are dealt with, asking only for confirmation that the building is clear before emerging from Z2. He explains: five Zhentarim grabbed him and Floon outside Old Xoblob Shop last night. They brought him here and questioned him about his father's embezzled hoard — money Renaer refuses to help his father hide. They took his mourning locket, which he believed held a keepsake from his mother; he did not know there was a compartment inside. The Xanathar Guild attacked before the interrogation went further. He slipped his bonds in the confusion and hid. From a gap in the Z2 door he watched Xanathar agents drag an unconscious Floon — the man they mistook for Renaer — through the main room, out through the yard, and down the alley toward a sewer grate.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:133: What Renaer freely shares: the Zhentarim wanted information about his father's gold; they took his locket; he watched Xanathar's agents drag Floon through the main room, out through the yard, and down the alley toward a sewer grate.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:193: | 1 → 2 | A | The party clears the Zhentarim warehouse and finds Renaer Neverember alive | 100 |
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:204: **Tone:** Warm, slightly chaotic, and quietly triumphant. The party solved a crime in a city full of crimes. They did not stop anything — the Eye Manshoon's cell extracted from Renaer's locket is already in Kolat Towers. But they saved two people and earned a home. That should feel like something.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:212: ***The Two Zhentarims in Arc A.*** The original adventure presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Arc A to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist — a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people — is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Arc B and then raid Kolat Towers in Arc I will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1 — one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. The distinction should be felt before it is explained.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:220: ***Renaer's Introduction.*** In the original, finding Renaer in the warehouse is a pleasant surprise in an otherwise thin scene — a friendly NPC who points the party downward. This redesign makes Renaer's introduction structural: the warehouse is the arc's pivot, not its anticlimax. The party arrives expecting Floon and finds someone who matters more to the campaign's back story. Renaer's knowledge state is asymmetric by design — he knows what Manshoon's cell wanted and why the locket mattered to his father, without knowing what the locket contained. His ignorance is genuine. This positions him as a trustworthy ally (he does not conceal things he knows) while preserving the campaign's central mystery (the nature of the Eyes, the Vault's location). The gap between what Renaer knows and what the party will eventually learn gives the warehouse scene a retrospective weight it lacks in the original.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:235: 4. **Which Zhentarim cell at the warehouse.** Manshoon's cell — Urstul Floxin's thugs. Not the Doom Raiders. The Eye extracted from Renaer's locket was delivered to Kolat Towers before the Xanathar Guild raid.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:236: 5. **Skewered Dragon ownership.** Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim. The Splinter operates the Skewered Dragon as an informal Dock Ward headquarters. The five men who followed Floon and Renaer out were Splinter thugs who have not returned.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:239: 8. **Renaer's knowledge state.** Freely shares: Zhentarim interrogation about his father's money; locket seizure; Floon dragged through the main room, out through the yard, and down the alley to a sewer grate. Does not know: locket contents; Vault location; Stone of Golorr's role. His ignorance is genuine.

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:145: Force Grey Mission 1 (Consulting Hlam) delivers the arc's Undermountain thread: Hlam tells the party that "evil's twin hides its face for now — expect that to change before winter's end." An oblique reference to Manshoon that will recalibrate on a second hearing. **Thread: Undermountain Is Active.** See also Scene 6.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:147: *The Two Zhentarims Clarify.* When the party joins the Doom Raiders or receives a direct briefing from Davil Starsong, the Doom Raiders / Manshoon's Splinter distinction is made explicit. **Thread: Two Zhentarims.** Second path: Yagra Stonefist is a bouncer candidate (see Appendix F Staff Candidates) — her red jade Zhentarim pendant and explicit Doom Raiders association make the split visible to any PC who asks. Third path: Fala's friendship with Ziraj the Hunter (Scene 3) established the Doom Raiders' personal-connection style of operation before any organizational explanation.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:367: ***The Most Arc-B-Specific Faction Content.*** Force Grey Missions 1 and 2 are the only Level 2 faction missions designed primarily to feed information threads rather than build renown in isolation. Hlam's warning (Mission 1) gives the first specific reference to Manshoon by implication ("evil's twin hides its face"). Zelifarn's barnacle-covered shipwreck lair at the harbor bottom (Mission 2) contains physical evidence of unusual deep-harbor activity pointing toward Skullport. Force Grey is the only faction where the Level 2–3 missions function as intelligence operations. Parties who join Force Grey in Arc B arrive at Arc F with more operational context than any other faction affiliation provides — and that context was earned through missions that felt like their own stories, not like exposition delivery.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:13: Dalakhar, a rock gnome spy working for exiled Lord Dagult Neverember, had been carrying the *Stone of Golorr* toward Trollskull Manor when four separate factions converged on the same alley at the same moment. Urstul Floxin's Zhentarim moved to intercept him from the north. The Gralhund nimblewright — a Lantanese construct sold by Jarlaxle Baenre's Sea Maidens Faire, armed with a *necklace of fireballs* by Lady Yalah Gralhund — watched from a rooftop, under orders to secure the Stone before any other faction could claim it. When Floxin appeared about to take Dalakhar alive, the nimblewright hurled a bead into the crowd. The fireball killed Dalakhar, two Zhentarim sellswords, eight bystanders, and the DM's chosen Arc B victim. Floxin survived — burned and furious — the Stone already gone. The nimblewright dropped from the roof, rifled Dalakhar's body, pocketed the Stone, and fled east across the rooftops toward Gralhund Villa. Floxin followed it back to the estate. Lady Gralhund disarmed him at swordpoint, confined him in a ground-floor room, and refused to yield the Stone. He killed his guards, escaped without it, and is now assembling a strike team for a forced return. That return is Arc D. The investigation that begins in Arc C must reach Gralhund Villa before the night of Ches 24th, when Floxin's team arrives and the situation becomes unrecoverable.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:49: - **DC 15 Wisdom (Perception):** Black Network tattoo on the dead Zhentarim; sewer silt on Dalakhar's boots; a rain barrel at the east end of the blast radius holds something metal — the *necklace of fireballs* (two beads remaining, broken clasp) dropped when the nimblewright's chain caught on a chimney as it fled east. Martem Trec has it.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:55: *Speak with Dead.* Available after the Watch takes custody of the bodies. Three access methods: faction access at Renown 1+ in the Harpers, Lords' Alliance, Order of the Gauntlet, or Force Grey arranges a cleric through the appropriate temple; a temple donation of at least 25 gp per casting buys one casting with questions provided in advance; a character who knows *Speak with Dead* can attempt their own access with a DC 13 Persuasion check with Barnibus or Cromley. Dalakhar can answer up to five questions: he stole the *Stone of Golorr* from the lair of a beholder called Xanathar, deep in the dungeons below the city; he worked for the rightful Open Lord — meaning Neverember, not Silverhand; the Stone is the key to a hidden hoard of gold dragons sealed somewhere in the city; he heard of adventurers who rescued Neverember's son and decided his hands were safer than his own; Renaer Neverember vouched for them. The two dead Zhentarim (Bashekk Ortallis and Wern Malkrave) can each answer up to five questions: they worked for Urstul Floxin; they were assigned to intercept a gnome named Dalakhar; whatever Dalakhar carried would make them "as rich as kings"; they do not know where Floxin billeted the cell.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:86: If the party mentions Fala's account of the burned man fleeing north — Renaer reaches out to Harper contacts at once. Within one day, he returns: the burned man matches Urstul Floxin, Manshoon's chief field agent in Waterdeep; a North Ward resident saw Floxin — visibly burned and limping — enter Gralhund Villa on Saerdoun Street within hours of the blast. The Watch sent two constables who were told everything was fine. No warrant was issued.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:92: - **Speak with Dead — The Two Dead Zhentarim.** Bashekk Ortallis and Wern Malkrave each provide up to five answers. They were assigned to intercept Dalakhar for Urstul Floxin, and confirm that whatever he carried would make the cell "as rich as kings." Their testimony establishes that the Guild was tracking Dalakhar specifically because he carried the Stone. Each body is an independent path; neither depends on the other.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:94: **For Doom Raiders Characters. (New Content)** Davil Starsong is still free during Arc C — his arrest happens in the Watch's post-Gralhund crackdown, which follows Arc D. Davil identifies Floxin immediately as Manshoon's blade and suspects Floxin filed an anonymous tip naming the Doom Raiders as persons of interest in the fireball — damage control meant to draw Watch heat away from the Splinter cell. He wants proof of Floxin's direct involvement. *The Dilemma:* The bombing was Zhentarim — the other cell, the rival operation, a splinter this party never sanctioned. That distinction is real. Explaining it to Trollskull Alley neighbors is going to be very difficult.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:106: *Faction Response Teams Deploy.* All four villain factions move on the crime scene within hours. Xanathar Guild gazers post to watch the alley (DC 16 Perception to spot them); Manshoon's Zhent operatives run a parallel investigation through their own network; Bregan D'Aerthe observers activate conditionally, their level of engagement scaling with whether the party later reaches the Sea Maidens Faire; the Cassalanters send flowers and a warm handwritten condolence note — Ammalia's handwriting. Cross-ref Appendix E (faction response team rosters and behavior by arc).
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:259: - **Mirt (Harpers, Renown 1+):** Identifies the mark without hesitation. Shares a rough map of observed Guild patrol patterns in the Trades Ward. Notes that Harper intelligence may be tainted — Manshoon has placed double agents in the network.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:262: - **Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders, Renown 1+):** Examines the mark with professional detachment. Confirms Guild death marks have been increasing. He is calculating whether the same leak that burned Dalakhar is going to burn him next — and whether Floxin filed the anonymous Watch tip naming the Doom Raiders as persons of interest, drawing heat away from Manshoon's cell.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:307: - Manshoon's Zhentarim (Urstul Floxin's cell) was independently tracking Dalakhar and wants the Stone.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:321: 2. **Urstul Floxin survives and talks.** Real name given voluntarily. Merchant cover story transparent to DC 14 Insight. His account of the "metal man" is accurate — he wants the party to track the Stone so Manshoon's Zhentarim can follow.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:324: 5. **Speak with Dead is included.** Three access methods to the bodies. Dalakhar's five answers. Two dead Zhentarim's five answers each.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:347: - `sources/4. Gralhund Villa.pdf` — Revised continuity (nimblewright and Zhentarim not working together, operating independently); Jarlaxle's observation teams; adversary roster for Arc D cross-reference.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:360: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Faction response team rosters and deployment behavior; Xanathar Guild gazer network; Manshoon parallel investigation; Bregan D'Aerthe conditional activation scaling with party choices at the Faire.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:382: ***Floxin as Self-Serving Witness.*** His willingness to talk is not generosity. He wants the party to track the Stone so the Zhentarim can follow. His account is accurate because lying would be counterproductive — a false description would produce a false lead, and he needs the real one found. Parties that read his motive through Insight will have to decide what to do with a useful informant who is also a tail.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:386: ***The First Arc Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Arc A establishes the Xanathar Guild and Manshoon's Zhentarim as adversaries. Arc B introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and Bregan D'Aerthe as observers. Arc C is the first arc where all four factions reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete rather than atmospheric: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers scaling with party choices at the Faire, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation.
