These are not flavor. They are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time — the moment the party understands they are not the only investigators in Waterdeep, and that the city is full of people who already know more than they do.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:13: The investigation that began in Trollskull Alley ends at the gate of Gralhund Villa on Saerdoun Street, North Ward. Urstul Floxin's Zhentarim strike team is coming on the night of Ches 24th. Xanathar's gazers are already watching the perimeter. Jarlaxle's Bregan D'Aerthe — if the party found their way to him in Arc C — has a team in the house across the street and knows more about what is inside than anyone. Lady Yalah Gralhund has the Stone and is holding it against all of them. The party's window is less than a day.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:25: After the fireball, Urstul Floxin followed the nimblewright's rooftop trail east to Saerdoun Street. He was not expected. Lady Yalah Gralhund met him in the front hall with a drawn blade at his throat. She had the guards disarm him and pry the *Stone of Golorr* from his grip at sword-point; he had been carrying it under his coat since the fireball. The arrangement Floxin had brokered — the Gralhunds as junior informants, the Stone passing to Manshoon's cell in exchange for protection and payment — had been Yalah's own initiative. She had simply revised the terms. The Stone was worth more than Manshoon's protection. A Masked Lord's seat required the kind of leverage that could not be manufactured. The vault's half-million gold dragons could buy it. She was deciding how.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:43: *Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — available if renown 1+).* The most direct voice. Davil ties Floxin to the fireball explicitly, names him as the reason Dalakhar is dead and the Stone is behind a locked gate. He frames recovery as a matter of clearing the Doom Raiders' name: bring him proof that Floxin ordered the fireball — Floxin's own words, ideally — and he can use it to cut the Watch's case against the Doom Raiders loose from Manshoon's cell. "Go in. Get out. Bring proof." He does not explain what kind of proof he needs. He trusts the party to recognize it when they find it.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:51: *Renaer Neverember (available if Renaer Contact: Gralhund Villa Named attunement is set from Arc C ev-01).* The source who named the villa does not have a faction brief to deliver. He wants to understand what his father's vault has to do with the Gralhunds, and what the Gralhunds have to do with the Zhentarim that burned Trollskull Alley. His access to Harper contacts gave the party the address; his family history gives him a stake in what happens once they cross the gate. He does not ask the party to act. He asks to know what they find. He will be at his townhouse, in the study, when they come back.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:65: *The Day/Night Metric.* When the party arrives at Saerdoun Street determines the encounter state. If they arrive before nightfall on Ches 24th, the household is in its day configuration: the Gralhund guards are active and the Stone is upstairs with Lady Yalah. The Zhentarim raid has not yet happened. If they arrive on the night of Ches 24th, Floxin's team has already entered through the pantry and the raid is in progress. The party walks into a battle rather than a household.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:71: **Zhentarim Watchers (DC 12 Perception).** Two Zhent thugs on nearby rooftops, one black flying snake courier between them. They are watching the villa to ensure no one leaves before Floxin's team arrives. They noticed one of Xanathar's gazers several days ago but could not identify it. They are unaware of Jarlaxle's team in Artheyn Manor. If they spot the party approaching: the flying snake carries word to Floxin. His strike team mobilizes and arrives in 10 + 2d6 minutes. The party has that window.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:80: - **DC 14 Investigation or Survival:** Identify the Zhentarim watchers' sightline gaps, revealing a blind approach route along the villa's west wall.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:137: *Peaceful Entry.* If the party approaches openly — the front gate, a visiting card, a polite request — they can access Lord Orond. DC 12 Persuasion to be received as guests; DC 15 if they mention the fireball or the Zhentarim. Lady Yalah never appears for a peaceful visit. She stays upstairs. Orond, if cultivated (DC 10 Charisma, disadvantage if Yalah has been warned), reveals under pressure: the Stone is in Yalah's keeping, Floxin was there of their own invitation (though he now understates the arrangement), and the Stone "has something to do with the Neverember matter." He does not know what the matter is. He does not know Manshoon is Floxin's master. He knows the Zhentarim offered protection and money, and that the arrangement has gone badly wrong.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:148: *Faction Reactive Behaviors (Daytime).* If the party is spotted by the Zhentarim watchers before entering: flying snake to Floxin, 10 + 2d6 minutes before his team mobilizes. The daytime infiltration becomes a race against Floxin's arrival. If the gazers track the party inside: they take up positions outside the walls and wait. They will not enter the estate. Jarlaxle's team (if present) watches; if the party is extracting the Stone before nightfall, Jarlaxle's team may attempt to intercept them on exit — not aggressively, but opportunistically.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:160: Separate from the trapped tome: a folded letter on a reading stand. *A Missive from Castle Ward*, signed "Chirada," dated two weeks prior. The exact text: *"We are now certain that the Second Eye was carried from the warehouse in the Dock Ward to Yellowspire in the Castle Ward. I no longer believe that it remains within Yellowspire, however. After observing the Zhentarim traffic here, I am convinced that they must be using a teleportal within the tower. If we wish to track the Second Eye further, we will need to access that teleportal or otherwise determine where it leads. – Chirada"* The Second Eye is one of three objects required to fully awaken the Stone of Golorr; this letter is a direct intelligence feed into the Grand Game, addressed to the Gralhunds by someone who has been watching Yellowspire on their behalf. It pays off in Arc E. Cross-ref Appendix E, Yellowspire section.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:162: **G15b (Guest Suite, Inner Room).** The published module places a permanent teleportation circle linked to Kolat Towers in this room, along with a birdcage holding three flying snakes. The Alexandrian Remix explicitly removes both elements. G15b is an ordinary guest room with no magical infrastructure. If a party searches it, they find luggage belonging to a Zhentarim operative — Floxin's gear, minus his weapons and the Stone.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:170: ### Scene 4: The Zhentarim Raid
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:222: **G15b (Guest Suite — Orond's Barricade).** The wardrobe that was pushed against the door from inside has been shoved aside. Inside, overturned furniture, Orond's evening coat torn at the shoulder, and Lord Orond Gralhund himself backed against the far wall with a fireplace poker in both hands and the whites of his eyes showing. When he sees the party is not Zhentarim, the grip on the poker goes slack.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:229: - The house funded Zhentarim agents who abducted Renaer Neverember months ago — before the fireball, before Floxin, before the Stone. Orond says this quietly, as if saying it softly makes it less true. Yalah arranged it. The point was to use Renaer as leverage to recover the Stone from Dalakhar, the gnome spy who stole it from Lord Neverember's people in the first place. "It went sideways," he says. "The gnome didn't come for the boy." He does not say what happened to Renaer after that. The party may already know.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:257: *Zhentarim Failure Is Not Foreordained.* If the party draws off or eliminates the Barracks reinforcements (beat 9), Floxin's team may actually win the ground floor and hold it. If Jarlaxle's team enters early — triggered by PC action rather than the natural timeline — the sequence collapses into improvisation. Xanathar's gazers observe throughout and will not intervene inside the estate.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:272: - A surviving Zhentarim agent grabs the Stone during the confusion and runs.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:302: *If the Chase Fails.* The Stone reaches a Bregan D'Aerthe outpost (Fenerus Stormcastle in the Trades Ward), a Zhentarim safe house, or is carried back to Xanathar — depending on who took it. From there it moves to the faction's primary lair within 2d6 days. The party has not lost the campaign; they have lost their current leverage and must find a new approach to the faction now holding it. Cross-ref Appendix E outpost and lair entries for which faction ends up with the Stone and what that implies for the next arc.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:314: *Bad Time to Be a Zhent.* By the morning of Ches 25th, the City Watch has sealed Gralhund Villa and begun processing what they find inside. Twenty-three bodies. Lord Gralhund in shock, saying nothing intelligible. Hrabbaz dead. No Stone, no nimblewright, no Floxin. The lead broadsheet names the event the **Gralhund Villa Bloodbath** within forty-eight hours, and the Watch begins a city-wide crackdown on the Black Network — arresting known and suspected Zhentarim figures regardless of cell affiliation, legitimate business or not.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:318: Party members with visible Zhentarim associations should keep a low profile for at least a tenday. Characters who were seen at the villa and can be described by surviving witnesses may be sought for questioning. A PC who fled the scene visibly may receive a Watch summons within three days.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:331: - **Help her:** She stays hidden for a tenday, then vanishes south. She leaves a handwritten note: the name and location of a Zhentarim courier cache in the South Ward that the party can raid without Manshoon's cell knowing for at least a month. Doom Raiders renown +2 if the party reports the arrangement to Tashlyn.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:338: - **Harpers (Mirt):** A Harper agent was in the crowd outside the villa — watching Zhentarim activity, not the party — and recognized at least one PC. If the party reports in first, Mirt receives the account with measured satisfaction and asks no follow-up questions. If they don't, he sends a note in his own handwriting: *"I heard Saerdoun Street was lively last night. Whenever you have a moment."* He would appreciate the courtesy of being told things that concern him before he learns them from someone else.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:351: - **Manshoon's Name:** The party has not heard it. Floxin refused to name his master throughout. Cross-ref Appendix E — this is preserved for a later arc.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:388: 1. **Day and night are separate scenes.** Scene 3 covers the daytime state (household dynamics, alarm escalation, optional homebrew additions). Scene 4 covers the nighttime Zhentarim raid (Floxin's timeline, Jarlaxle's team in motion, the raid's live outcome). Both scenes reference the same keyed areas (G1–G19); Scene 4 describes the night adversary roster as changes from Scene 3's baseline.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:400: 7. **Manshoon does not appear and is not named in Arc D.** Floxin refuses to name his master throughout — this is established in his Appendix A profile. The party fights Manshoon's agents without knowing whose agents they are. The reveal is preserved for a later arc.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:420: - **Arc I: Kolat Towers** — If the party encounters Floxin and he survives Arc D, he becomes a Manshoon response team leader with a specific directive (prisoners delivered to Avareen in Brindul Alley). Cross-ref Appendix E.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:423: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Manshoon's Weave motivation and blackmailed Masked Lords (Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk); *A Directive from Jarlaxle* document listing; *Gralhund Study of the Grand Game* document listing; Yellowspire section (Chirada, Second Eye, teleportal); Xanathar Guild response teams (Scene 5 gazer intercept option).
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:449: This arc follows that philosophy. Scenes 3 and 4 are not fights with predetermined outcomes. They are situations with multiple possible resolutions, each shaped by the party's decisions, the rival factions' behaviors, and the specific sequence of events on the night. The ten-beat raid timeline is a spine, not a script — the DM can drop the party into it at any point, and the party's actions can derail it entirely. Zhentarim failure is not foreordained. Jarlaxle's team arriving early changes the calculus. The party drawing guards away from the barricade at G15b might let Floxin succeed at his hostage-taking.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:461: ## Manshoon Stays Invisible
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:463: The party fights Urstul Floxin's agents throughout this arc. Floxin refuses to name his master — this is specified in his Appendix A profile and is consistent with his established characterization as a loyalist who understands operational security. The party may learn that Floxin works for a "silent partner" above the Zhentarim cell structure they have seen. They do not learn Manshoon's name, his goals, or the Weave-supplanting motivation in Arc D.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:465: This is the only arc in the campaign where the party directly fights Manshoon's operatives without knowing whose operatives they are. Preserving this invisibility matters because the Manshoon reveal — when it comes — needs to land as a recontextualization of events the party has already lived through. An Arc D reveal reduces it to a name they file away and immediately forget. A later reveal, when they have encountered his agents multiple times without connecting them to a single source, produces the kind of retroactive click that defines a well-structured mystery.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:471: If the chase fails — if the Stone reaches a Bregan D'Aerthe outpost, a Zhentarim safe house, or Xanathar's custody — the campaign continues, but the party enters Arc E negotiating from weakness rather than strength. Jarlaxle will deal with them. Xanathar will not (Xanathar never deals). Manshoon's cell will know the party is active and has failed. These are real consequences that reshape the social landscape of the subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is Arc E's problem, not Arc D's.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:485: The Watch's sweep removes Davil from play, puts Tashlyn in a provisional leadership role she has not asked for, and signals to every faction in the city that the Grand Game has become visible to law enforcement. Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's operations in the North Ward become more careful. Jarlaxle's team leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown — who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn — begin to define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:15: Ten outposts are distributed across Waterdeep's wards, maintained by four factions, each one a data point in the web of the Grand Game. The party cannot storm the lairs blind — Xanathar's sanctum, Cassalanter Villa's temple, the Sea Maidens Faire, and Kolat Towers are not targets until the party knows enough to attempt them. The outposts are the research: entering a Xanatharian front to find how the Guild accesses its own lair, or breaking into a cultist's apartment to find a map the Cassalanters spent months assembling.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:35: That calculation is no longer accurate. Neverember is in Neverwinter. The parties holding his Eyes have been in Waterdeep long enough to develop their own plans for whatever the Stone might reveal. Jarlaxle Baenre holds his Eye aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, where it serves as leverage and curiosity in equal measure. The Cassalanter family, working through a network of cult agents and infernal diviners, located a second Eye buried in the crypt of a noblewoman who died without heirs, and extracted it. Xanathar holds the last in Sylgar's fishbowl in the lair beneath Skullport. Zhentarim envoys stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter and carried it to Xanathar as a gift. Xanathar killed them, kept the stone, and has never learned what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:37: Manshoon holds no Eye. His envoys carried one to Xanathar and never came back. He wants the Stone itself — specifically, the compressed aboleth memory within it, which encodes structural knowledge of the Weave that Mystra's servants have never catalogued. The vault is an instrument he would use to fund his actual goal. The Stone is the goal. He needs the Stone and any two Eyes, but he never intended to open the vault the way Neverember did — only to hold the Stone long enough to extract what he needs.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:39: The Stone now sits with the party. The Watch crackdown following the Gralhund Villa incident has disrupted Manshoon's Brindul Alley operation, scattered the Zhentarim response teams, and landed Davil Starsong in Watch custody on circumstantial evidence. Xanathar's Guild is consolidating — pulling agents tighter, increasing surveillance. Jarlaxle watches the party with quiet interest and no urgency; he holds something they need, and he is in no hurry to discuss terms. The Cassalanters, undisturbed, continue their research. Below the city's peaceful surface, four factions know the Stone has surfaced. Three of them want it back.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:65: *The Fourth Faction.* The Stone does not produce a fourth vision, because Manshoon holds no Eye. If pressed — if the attuning character asks whether there are others — the Stone responds differently — an awareness rather than a vision. Something watches from a tower. It watches the Stone, not the vault. The impression ends abruptly; the Stone does not elaborate.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:83: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* The Alliance has Watch patrol schedules for every ward, available to the party with LA credentials at renown 3 or higher. Jalester's advice is careful and nonspecific: reconnaissance before entry, Watch contact windows, the legal calculus of unauthorized access to private premises. He is building an intelligence file on all four factions and makes no secret of wanting the party to share what they find from all outposts, not just whichever they target. He does not volunteer that the Lords' Alliance has independent surveillance on Yellowspire; the party can earn that information by demonstrating commitment to the Manshoon thread.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:87: *Savra Belabranta (Order of the Gauntlet — renown 1+).* The Gauntlet's intelligence on Manshoon comes from prior mission fallout — the Splinter's intermediary network, the checkpoint strike near the Trades Ward, circumstantial connections to Brindul Alley. Savra has not verified the address, but she knows the Splinter keeps a Trades Ward safe house and that prisoners taken from previous encounters were being directed somewhere. Her concern is justice: she wants the torture operation exposed, and she wants it done properly — meaning with survivors willing to testify. She does not mention that the Gauntlet has its own reasons for wanting Manshoon's legal exposure rather than his elimination.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:91: *Tashlyn Yafeera (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* Tashlyn has replaced Davil Starsong, who is in Watch custody on Gralhund-related charges. She is colder and more operational, and she establishes this in the first exchange: "Davil talked too much and trusted too easily. I won't make that mistake." She has been watching Yellowspire for two months and has schematics — structural layout, entry points, guard rotation estimates — which she will share when the party is ready to move, not before. Her immediate lead is tactical: every Manshoon agent carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers. Capturing one means one fewer entry point they can use. The subtext of everything Tashlyn says: she needs Manshoon neutralized because Manshoon neutralized is the precondition for Davil's release.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:117: **Manshoon's Zhentarim:** Parties who searched Urstul Floxin's effects at Gralhund Villa have documents pointing to Brindul Alley. Parties that did not can find the Interrogation House through Trade Ward canvassing — the safe house's traffic is unusual enough to notice if someone is looking (DC 14 Investigation over a day).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:127: *Cross-Faction Spillover.* Xanathar and Manshoon are actively at war; each monitors the other's losses. Hitting a Xanathar outpost raises Manshoon's alert tier by 1, and vice versa — both factions assume the other is responsible before they conclude it was the party. Cassalanter imp surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire creates a reciprocal intelligence channel; Cassalanter losses alert Jarlaxle that something is moving against infernal-adjacent operations in the city. See **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for full inter-faction dynamics.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:165: *Approach.* Grinda operates openly as a black-market armorer. Anyone asking about Xanathar weapons, Guild suppliers, or black-market arms in the Dock Ward gets pointed to Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma in Dock Ward taverns or street contacts; no faction membership required). Three additional paths: the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Terasse Estate names her; a captured Xanathar response team member's *Directive from Nar'l* names her as a supplier; Agorn Fuoco's *Report to Agorn Fuoco* (from any Zhentarim response team) confirms her location. Parties can approach as customers, investigators, or raiders. Grinda does not fight for the Guild — she fights for her business. She will negotiate if the party offers more immediate value than Xanathar does, and she will report the encounter afterward.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:191: ### Scene 5: Manshoon's Zhentarim Outposts
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:193: **Purpose:** Two outposts that define Manshoon's character through contrast. The Interrogation House is ugly and deliberate — a place where precision curdles into cruelty. Yellowspire is quieter and stranger — a Banite tower hiding in plain sight in the Castle Ward. Together they deliver the campaign's first named reference to Manshoon and the only bypass route to Kolat Towers that avoids the force field.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:195: **Tone:** Espionage thriller. Manshoon's operations are surgical. The Interrogation House is the exception — deliberately ugly, reminding the party that his patience has limits. Yellowspire is quieter, stranger, a Banite tower in plain sight.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:201: *Trade Ward, Brindul Alley.* — **Street-level entry point for the Manshoon chain** (for parties with **Gralhund Villa** documents).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:203: *Approach.* Four paths to the Interrogation House: the *Directive to Urstul Floxin* from **Gralhund Villa** names Avareen Windrivver and Brindul Alley explicitly; Yellowspire's crumpled note from Avareen (for parties who hit Yellowspire first); questioning a captured Zhentarim response team member (prisoners taken by Zhent teams are brought here — they'll say so); or Trade Ward canvassing for parties lacking **Gralhund Villa** documents (DC 14 Investigation over a day; no faction membership required). The house appears unremarkable from the street. Use house L1 layout (DH p. 62).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:205: *Key Areas.* Front Room: Avareen Windrivver (spy) and Zorbog Jyarkoth (thug) with three black flying snakes. Avareen carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers; her first instinct if captured is to destroy it. Backroom: a torture rack, an arcane circle, and Ott Steeltoes strapped to a chair.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:207: *The Directive to Zorbog.* A flying snake arrives during or shortly after the party enters, bearing a sealed letter addressed to Zorbog. The letter is a *Directive to Zorbog* signed **Manshoon** — the first document in the campaign bearing his name as a signature. It orders the kidnap of Fenerus Stormcastle of Quill Alley, specifying delivery to the Interrogation House within four days. The party now knows: there is a named Manshoon. He is the Splinter's leader. He is targeting a specific civilian. And his directive's phrasing suggests awareness of the party's recent activities.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:211: *Intel Yield.* Manshoon's name (first use in the campaign). The pass-amulet from Avareen (route to Kolat Towers). Ott Steeltoes as a potential **Xanathar's Lair** contact. The *Directive to Zorbog* naming Fenerus Stormcastle — a cross-faction thread pointing toward Bregan D'aerthe (Fenerus is a BD lamplighter; Manshoon has identified him as a soft target).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:213: **Escalation: Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3).** Avareen's flying snakes carry the alarm faster than the party can move; if even one escapes, Manshoon knows within hours. Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 1d4 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves directly to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:227: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Primary objective: locate and document the permanent teleportation circle to Kolat Towers. The circle is active. Stepping through it immediately means arriving at Kolat Towers unannounced and unprepared — a one-way trip that bypasses the force field, which is Yellowspire's entire value to Manshoon. Secondary: a crumpled note from Avareen, cross-referencing the Interrogation House. Tertiary: Agorn's pass-amulet to Kolat Towers, which he surrenders without hesitation in exchange for his life.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:231: *Step 5 — Getaway.* Amath fights with conviction; acolytes are less committed. Agorn flees. **Escalation: Manshoon moves to Suspicious (Tier 2) if the Interrogation House has not been hit; moves to Lockdown (Tier 4) if it has.** Agorn carries word to surviving Zhentarim contacts if he escapes. Response teams redeploy within 1d4 days.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:275: *Step 1 — Gather Information.* Five paths to the Converted Windmill: Seffia Naelryke's visiting card found on Vevette Blackwater from a Zhentarim response team (Seffia approached Vevette to open a communication channel; Vevette rebuffed her, suspecting a trap, but kept the card); Arn's Book of Worship at the Asmodean Shrine; the *Injury Reports* logbook at Terasse Estate ("the windmill on Coachlamp Lane"); a Cassalanter Villa report if **Cassalanter Villa** is completed first; or the infernal presence detected by Divine Sense within 60 feet of the building (DC 12 Wisdom for any character with Divine Sense; no faction required). Parties without Divine Sense can reach the windmill through Coachlamp Lane neighborhood inquiry — neighbors report strange smells, unusual visitors after midnight, a couple who never quite fit in (DC 13 Charisma; no faction required).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:303: *Approach.* Three paths to Fenerus: the Seven Masks Theater intelligence archive (a letter from "K" to "J" referencing Fenerus as a BD field contact and his address at Quill Alley); the *Directive to Zorbog* from the Interrogation House (Manshoon is targeting Fenerus — his address is in the directive); or a captured BD response team member's *Sealed Letter from Krebbyg*, naming Fenerus as a message recipient.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:309: If the party did not warn him and arrives before the kidnap team: Zorbog's team may be in the alley or entering the building. A three-way encounter develops — party, Zhentarim kidnappers, and Fenerus in the middle.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:351: *Response Team Compositions.* See the relevant Factions guide pages for full compositions and carried documents. Every team leader carries faction-specific documents that function as intelligence opportunities: Zhentarim teams carry directives naming Brindul Alley or Kolat Towers; Xanathar teams carry operational correspondence from Nar'l or Ahmaergo; BD teams carry playbills or coded messages in Drow; Cassalanter teams carry instructions from Lord Cassalanter. The party should treat every response team encounter as a potential document recovery, not just a combat encounter.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:353: *Cross-Faction Response Team Encounters.* Xanathar and Manshoon teams may encounter each other during this quest — both factions are moving through the same wards. Witnessing these confrontations without engaging either side generates intelligence about both factions simultaneously. BD teams prefer misdirection over lethal force; they are more likely to shadow the party than confront them directly. Cassalanter teams vary by the party's relationship with the family.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:371: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance).* Wants all outpost intelligence shared with the Watch, framed as actionable intelligence about criminal operations rather than unauthorized entry. Offers legal cover for all this quest's operations retroactively in exchange for Manshoon intelligence that will hold up in a Magisters' proceeding. He is building a legal case, not an arrest warrant; the distinction matters to him.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:377: *Vajra Safahr (Force Grey).* Asks about arcane intelligence: teleportation circles, magical defenses, ward schematics. If the Yellowspire teleportation circle was documented, she immediately requests the sigil sequence. Vajra can verify the circle's destination (Kolat Towers) through divination and will confirm it within a day of receiving the sequence. She shares that the Blackstaff has been tracking Manshoon's arcane signature and can use the sigil sequence to triangulate his operational base. She does not share what she intends to do with this information.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:379: *Tashlyn Yafeera (Doom Raiders).* Asks about Manshoon's operational capacity. If pass-amulets were captured, she wants to examine them — specifically to determine whether they contain identification components that could be used to track the holder's movements. If the Interrogation House was hit, she wants to know whether Davil's name appears in any recovered documents. Her concern is documented and unstated in equal measure: she is building a legal defense for Davil and needs evidence that Doom Raiders involvement in the Gralhund operation was circumstantial rather than directed.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:393: *Completing a chain:* Hit at least two outposts of the same faction and recover at least one piece of actionable lair intelligence. For Xanathar (three outposts), two of three is sufficient. For all other factions (two outposts), both must be cleared. A party that hits one Xanathar outpost and one Zhentarim outpost has started two chains but completed neither.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:395: Total **Faction Outposts**: 1 Milestone Point, for the first completed chain; a second chain earns intelligence, not points. The party remains 4th level through all of **Faction Outposts**, at 10 cumulative points. A party can attempt any lair heist (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, or **Kolat Towers**) after completing just one chain, or even none; this quest's outposts are the recommended on-ramp, not a hard gate. See the Milestone section in **Xanathar's Lair** (or any lair heist's) for how the heist phase carries the party from 4th to 7th level.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds enough intelligence to attempt at least one lair heist. The GM reviews each faction's escalation tier and communicates it through environmental detail: Dock Ward patrols are heavier than last tenday; a Castle Ward merchant notes that the old tower on the hill has gone dark; the Sea Maidens Faire hasn't performed in a week. Party chooses which lair first. **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** are available in any order.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:413: 5. **Player faction hooks gap-filled.** Enclave: infernal resonance confirmed as infernal but source unknown (→ Cassalanters). Gauntlet: Splinter intelligence trail (→ Manshoon). Force Grey: BD operational infrastructure (→ theater and message architecture).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:417: 7. **Manshoon reveal sequence.** Name only at Interrogation House (*Directive to Zorbog*). Identity at **Kolat Towers**. Motivation at **Vault of Dragons**.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:421: 9. **Chain completion defined.** Two outposts of the same faction plus one piece of actionable lair intelligence. The first completed chain awards 1 Milestone Point (cumulative 10), not enough to reach level 5. Level 5 is reached on the first completed lair heist (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, or **Kolat Towers**).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:452: - **Kolat Towers** — The Yellowspire teleportation circle and captured pass-amulets are primary entry paths to Kolat Towers, bypassing the force field.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:469: ***Cross-Faction Spillover and the Xanathar–Manshoon War.*** The spillover rules — hitting Xanathar raises Manshoon's tier and vice versa — have a specific purpose beyond realism. They ensure that a party pursuing only one faction's chain still generates meaningful pressure across multiple fronts. A party that focuses entirely on Xanathar outposts will encounter Manshoon response teams without having engaged Manshoon directly, surfacing the inter-faction war as lived experience rather than exposition.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:477: ## The Manshoon Reveal Sequence
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:479: ***Name, Identity, Motivation.*** Manshoon's revelation has been staged across three quests: name at the Interrogation House, identity at **Kolat Towers**, motivation at **Vault of Dragons**. The decision to surface his name on a document that arrives live during the party's operation — rather than in a cutscene or NPC speech — is deliberate. The *Directive to Zorbog* bearing his signature is the party's first tangible evidence that the Splinter has a named leader. It lands differently there, in a house they just broke into, while a prisoner they may or may not rescue is strapped to a chair nearby, than it would in a faction briefing. The emotional environment matters.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:491: ***Lair Entrance Verification.*** Four lair entrances, all reachable from this quest's intelligence: Xanathar's Lair — Nar'l's guildsign trail, gladiatorial transport via Terasse, Ott Steeltoes as guide (three paths); Kolat Towers — Yellowspire teleportation circle, captured pass-amulet from Avareen, Agorn's amulet traded under duress (three paths); Cassalanter Villa — Ammalia's **Fireball!** dinner invitation, Cassalanter cultist testimony from this quest's outposts, Esvele Rosznar in **Cassalanter Villa** (three paths, the last requiring no completion of this quest at all); Sea Maidens Faire — Fenerus's decoded messages, Malcolm Brizzenbright's testimony, Xanathar's Lair report from **Xanathar's Lair** (three paths).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:495: **Harpers.** This quest's Harper hook is an ongoing operative benefit rather than a single scene beat. Once the party reaches Renown 30+, Mirt can direct embedded Harper informants to provide advance intelligence against Xanathar and Manshoon outposts — shift rotations, guard counts, unusual personnel — delivered before each operation rather than discovered during it. A separate informant inside the Sea Maidens Faire reports that Jarlaxle has been asking about the party by description; Mirt passes this to Harper PCs as a quiet heads-up in the days before **Sea Maidens Faire** begins. These benefits are not surfaced in Scene 2's consultation; they activate through maintained renown and are delivered as one informant report at the start of each outpost operation the party undertakes in Scenes 4–7, once a Harper PC has Renown 30+. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Harpers** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:9: Three months before the Trollskull fireball, Manshoon dispatched two Zhentarim lieutenants to Skullport carrying a gift: Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr, stolen from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. The Eye was offered as a peace overture — proof that Manshoon's splinter faction could be a valuable partner in the Grand Game. Xanathar received the envoys in its throne room, listened to their proposal, and ate them. It kept the Eye and has never learned what it is. The Eye passed to Ott Steeltoes, who placed it in Sylgar's fishbowl because it had a pretty color and Xanathar liked Sylgar's bowl to look interesting.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:13: Nihiloor has used this paranoia as cover. The illithid's three unauthorized projects — running in parallel for years — are now approaching critical phases. The Infiltration: intellect devourers inserted into four positions in Waterdeep's Watch and civic administration, with two additional placements pending. The Experiments: twelve captives from the gladiatorial arena modified into psionic relay organisms, intended to form the backbone of a city-wide intelligence network that would answer only to Nihiloor. The Puppet: four months ago, Nihiloor successfully applied Dominate Monster to Xanathar through a sustained psionic relay device in X26. The Xanathar who has been running the Guild since then — the Xanathar who killed Manshoon's envoys, who sent response teams against the party's allies in **Faction Outposts**, who holds the Eye in a fishbowl because it trusts Ott's aesthetic judgment — has been Nihiloor's instrument. The beholder does not know this. The domination does not feel like domination; it feels like its own decisions.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:33: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* Watch patrol schedules for the sewer approaches to Skullport: three viable windows per day in the Dock Ward drainage network. If Manshoon outposts were also hit in **Faction Outposts** (cross-faction spillover raising the Watch's general alert posture), the gaps narrow. Jalester can identify a 90-minute window on the third watch that still exists even at elevated patrol density. At renown 10+, Jalester provides a letter of introduction to the Dock Ward senior Watch officer, who will formally log any disturbance report the party files — giving their sewer approach a legal cover story and keeping Watch patrols clear of the guildsign entrance for two hours.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:147: **X7 — Cell Block.** Eight cells cut into rock, three occupied. Samara (gladiatorial combatant, six weeks in captivity) knows: Xanathar holds "a magic stone and a marble with a colored center"; the marble is in "the fish tank in the big room with the purple carpet"; and Manshoon's organization is based in a tower in the North Ward (overheard from another prisoner since sent to the arena). The tower information is one of three clue paths to **Kolat Towers**. Two Guild Soldiers watch the cell gate; a third is asleep in a chair. Cell locks are DC 13 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools). If all three prisoners are freed, the two Guild members in custody offer to start a fight at X2 — buying the party 5 minutes before being subdued.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:167: **X33 — Crypt of Xanathar's Past.** Through the secret door from X6 (DC 17 Perception), a narrow passage leads to a chamber containing the preserved remains and personal effects of every previous beholder who held the title "Xanathar" — predecessors stretching back centuries. Nar'l Xibrindas is the only current Guild member who knows this room exists. The room contains: evidence that "Xanathar" is a title claimed through murder, not inherited; the original Guild charter signed by the first holder; and locked coffer containing the personal journals of the last three Xanathars, including early dealings with Manshoon's predecessors. The revelation that Xanathar is a title implies the current one could be replaced, and that someone may already be planning to do so.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:175: - **X23 — Competitive Intelligence Files.** Nihiloor's research on all four villain factions, assembled as preparation for an eventual play for true control. The Manshoon section is the most detailed: intercepted Zhentarim communications, field reports on Kolat Towers' external security, a partial lieutenant roster, and a ground-level map of Kolat Towers. This is one of three clue paths to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:286: **Manshoon's Zhentarim Splinter.** Monitoring via agents watching the lair's entrances. If Xanathar is weakened or eliminated, Manshoon moves within days to absorb abandoned Guild territory. His alert tier increases if the party was observed.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:304: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr and Eye #1. The Stone can talk. It wants to be whole. Two Eyes remain; the sharpened impressions point toward a Sea Ward villa and a ship in the harbor. **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** are available in any order. The party's next choice is informed by the Stone's impressions, the faction debrief intelligence, and whatever documents they recovered from the lair.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:318: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:330: This quest's transition is open. The three remaining lair heists (**Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers**) are available in any order, and the Stone's sharpened impressions plus faction debrief intelligence provides organic guidance toward each. The Founders' Day deadline creates pressure without prescribing order.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:344: - **Kolat Towers** — Samara's testimony (X7), Nihiloor's Manshoon intelligence (X23), and a third clue seeded in **Cassalanter Villa** form the Three Clue Rule paths to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:410: The Manshoon intelligence in X23 is a designed cross-quest clue. Three independent clue paths lead to **Kolat Towers**: Samara's testimony in X7 (a tower in the North Ward), Nihiloor's intelligence files in X23 (partial Kolat Towers layout and lieutenant roster), and a third path seeded in **Cassalanter Villa**. The Three Clue Rule is satisfied across quests, not within a single location. This is the correct application of the rule in a campaign where quests run in variable order.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:197: **C6 — Victoro's Office.** Second floor, locked (DC 17 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools) or Victoro's key). A helmed horror stands in the corner — motionless until a creature other than Victoro or Ammalia touches the desk, at which point it attacks without warning. The desk contains: thirteen loan contracts (Cassalanter banking leverage over Waterdeep's guilds), a hidden compartment (DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation)) holding a gold Asmodeus holy symbol worth 250 GP, a report from Seffia Naelryke on outpost operations (cross-reference to **Faction Outposts** Converted Windmill), and the Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game. The Report on the Grand Game is the Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers** — it references Manshoon's operations, identifies Kolat Towers by name and location, describes Zhentarim splinter movements, and notes that "the wizard who calls himself Manshoon guards his fortress with a force field that admits only those he has keyed."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:203: **C22 — Ammalia's Study.** Third floor, locked (DC 18 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools) or Ammalia's key). A red leather grimoire sits on a lectern — the commune ritual to contact Asmodeus (who responds through a pit fiend proxy), the contract's terms transcribed in Ammalia's handwriting, and marginal notes in increasing desperation. The desk holds Ammalia's spellbook and ten vials of midnight tears poison. A trapdoor in the floor opens to C23 (a closet leading to C24, the attic). The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is also here per the Alexandrian overlay — a second placement of the **Kolat Towers** clue document, ensuring that parties who reach C22 but not C6 still find the path to Kolat Towers.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:357: **Manshoon's Zhentarim.** Monitors through double agents. If the Cassalanters are weakened, Manshoon considers one fewer rival and may accelerate his own timeline.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr and 1–2 Eyes. The children's fate is resolved or ticking. The remaining lair heists — whichever of **Xanathar's Lair**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** have not been completed — are available in any order. The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (C6/C22) points toward **Kolat Towers**. Esvele's Viper Den (if discovered) points toward the Brandath Crypts (**Vault of Dragons**). The Stone's impressions guide the party toward remaining targets.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:411: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:437: - **Kolat Towers** — The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (C6/C22) is the third of three independent clue paths to **Kolat Towers**, alongside Samara's testimony (**Xanathar's Lair** X7) and Nihiloor's files (**Xanathar's Lair** X23). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied across quests.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:447: This quest must feel like social infiltration followed by dungeon horror, and the distinction from **Xanathar's Lair** (dungeon infiltration), **Sea Maidens Faire** (caper heist), and **Kolat Towers** (fortress raid) is structural, not cosmetic. The social exposure system — Accepted, Noticed, Suspected, Exposed — is the defining mechanic above ground. It creates a different kind of tension than the panopticus: the panopticus watches through magic; the villa watches through people. A failed Stealth check in **Xanathar's Lair** triggers an alarm cascade. A failed Deception check in this quest triggers a social escalation that the party can still recover from — but each recovery narrows their options.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:497: ## The Three Clue Rule Path to Kolat Towers
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:499: The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is placed in both C6 (Victoro's desk) and C22 (Ammalia's study). This dual placement ensures that a party reaching either room — not necessarily both — discovers the path to Kolat Towers. The report references Manshoon by operational name, identifies the Towers' location, describes Zhentarim splinter activity, and notes the force-field entry restriction. It is the third of three independent paths to **Kolat Towers**, alongside Samara's testimony from **Xanathar's Lair** X7 and Nihiloor's intelligence files from **Xanathar's Lair** X23.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:501: The Three Clue Rule is now satisfied across quests. A party that skipped **Xanathar's Lair** entirely can still reach **Kolat Towers** through this quest. A party that completed **Xanathar's Lair** but missed both the Samara and Nihiloor paths can reach **Kolat Towers** through the Cassalanter report. No single failed check, missed room, or skipped quest blocks all three paths. This is the correct application of the Three Clue Rule in a campaign where lair heists run in variable order.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:160: **J30 (Zardoz Zord's Cabin).** Jarlaxle's private quarters aboard the Eyecatcher. 1 nimblewright valet. Contains: the Nimblewright Sales Ledger (complete list of nine buyers — cross-reference **Fireball!** Scene 4 for the partial ledger the party may already hold); the Report on the Cultists of Asmodeus (identifies the Asmodean Shrine on Aveen Street and the ancient worship site below the Sea Ward — Three Clue Rule path to **Cassalanter Villa**, if not yet completed); Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (references all four factions' positions including Manshoon's Kolat Towers — Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**). The trapdoor to the Scarlet Marpenoth is beneath a rug in the aft corner — DC 16 Perception to locate, DC 18 Thieves' Tools to unlock or Jarlaxle's key.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:198: **Purpose.** What goes wrong, what goes sideways, and how the party gets out — managed alongside the Manshoon parallel strike and the Jarlaxle confrontation.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:202: *The Manshoon Parallel Strike.* Manshoon knows Eye #3 is aboard the Eyecatcher. His intelligence sources — scrying via simulacrum, double agents in multiple factions, Zhentarim informants in the Dock Ward — have confirmed the target. He dispatches a strike team regardless of what path the party has chosen.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:204: **The Strike Team.** Urstul Floxin (if alive from **Gralhund Villa**; if dead, replaced by Vevette Blackwater) + 1 lieutenant + 4 thugs. They approach by rowboat from the harbor's south side, boarding the Eyecatcher via grappling hooks on the port hull. They carry pass-amulets to Kolat Towers for emergency extraction.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:206: **Trigger Conditions.** The Manshoon strike always fires. On the heist path, the strike begins 30 minutes after the party boards the Eyecatcher. On the alliance or patron path, the strike hits while the party is aboard negotiating or receiving the Eye — Jarlaxle's crew repels the Zhentarim, but the attack gives Jarlaxle a reason to ask the party for payback: "They came to my ship. In my harbor. I want to know where they came from, and I want it to stop." This becomes a direct hook into **Kolat Towers** — Jarlaxle sends the party after Manshoon at Kolat Towers, providing whatever BD intelligence the network has on the location. The Zhent attack transforms the alliance and patron paths from clean resolutions into obligations with teeth.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:210: | PC State | Zhentarim Action | Result |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:212: | PCs undetected, moving through Eyecatcher | Zhentarim board port side, trigger alarm on J1 deck watch | Alarm draws crew AWAY from PCs toward the Zhentarim; pressure relief for the party but chaos increases |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:213: | PCs detected, improvising | Zhentarim arrive into existing chaos | Three-way: PCs vs. BD crew vs. Zhentarim. BD crew splits response. Party can exploit the confusion |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:214: | PCs in Scarlet Marpenoth | Zhentarim fight through Eyecatcher, reach J30, discover trapdoor | Race to U4 — Zhentarim are 5–10 minutes behind the party in the submarine |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:215: | PCs have Eye, escaping | Zhentarim encounter PCs during extraction | Zhentarim attempt to take the Eye from the party; BD crew may treat both groups as hostile or focus on the more visible threat |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:216: | PCs encounter Zhentarim directly | Combat or negotiation | Zhentarim will trade information (Kolat Towers intelligence, Manshoon's operations) for the Eye. They will not accept anything less. If defeated, their pass-amulets to Kolat Towers are recoverable — Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers** |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:217: | PCs allied or patron path (not heisting) | Zhentarim board and attack BD crew | Jarlaxle's crew fights back; party can assist or stand clear. Jarlaxle uses the attack as leverage: "Now you owe me — find where they came from." Pass-amulets recovered from defeated Zhentarim + Jarlaxle's BD intelligence on Manshoon = strongest **Kolat Towers** entry path available |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:219: *Facing Jarlaxle.* If Jarlaxle confronts the party — because the alarm reached him, because the Zhentarim strike drew him out, or because he was in J30 or J32 when the party arrived:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:239: **Tone.** The complications should feel like a heist going sideways — not catastrophic, but requiring rapid adaptation. Jarlaxle's confrontation should feel like a chess match, not a battle. The Manshoon strike adds genuine danger — the Zhentarim are not charming; they are violent, and their presence transforms the caper into something more dangerous. The contrast between Jarlaxle's wit and the Zhentarim's brutality is the scene's defining dynamic.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:273: - *Impressed (heist succeeded, Jarlaxle confronted and negotiated):* Jarlaxle lost the Eye but gained respect for the party. He is not hostile — he is intrigued. BD continues to operate but treats the party as worthy opponents rather than targets. Jarlaxle may approach them for **Kolat Towers** or **Vault of Dragons** cooperation.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:278: **Manshoon's Zhentarim.** If the Zhentarim strike team was defeated, Manshoon knows — the pass-amulets went dark. He adjusts **Kolat Towers** preparations. If the strike team caused chaos but failed to get the Eye, Manshoon redirects to Kolat Towers defense. Manshoon's cell holds no Eye unless the party's failure puts one there: if the strike team recovered the Eye, **Kolat Towers** becomes a recovery operation as well as an assault.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:293: - *Tashlyn or Davil (Doom Raiders):* "What happened to the Zhentarim team?" Names and faces from the strike team help them map Manshoon's remaining force structure. The pass-amulets to Kolat Towers are of particular interest: "Those get you inside his base. That is extremely valuable intelligence."
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:302: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone with 1–3 Eyes. The remaining lair heists are available in any order. The Report on Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) points toward **Cassalanter Villa** if not yet completed. The Letter from Nar'l (U3) confirms the BD/Xanathar connection, providing context for **Xanathar's Lair**. Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (if recovered from Scene 5) both point toward **Kolat Towers**. If this was Eye #3 — the Full Awakening — **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:304: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. The caper's resolution carries a different emotional register than **Xanathar's Lair**'s revelatory awakening or **Cassalanter Villa**'s moral reckoning. This quest's aftermath is about relationships — with Jarlaxle (enemy, rival, or ally?), with Zelifarn (the crystal ball decision), with Manshoon's Zhentarim (confirmed threat, now owed payback). The party has engaged with the most charming villain in the Grand Game. What that engagement produced depends entirely on how they played it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:316: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:342: - **Kolat Towers** — Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (Scene 5) both provide independent Three Clue Rule paths to **Kolat Towers**. Combined with Jarlaxle's BD intelligence on Manshoon (alliance/patron path), this is the strongest entry path to **Kolat Towers** in the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:360: This quest is the only lair heist that offers three fundamentally different approaches to obtaining the Eye. This is because Jarlaxle is the only villain who would willingly trade it for something he values more. Xanathar does not negotiate — it eats envoys. The Cassalanters are too desperate — the children's deadline consumes their attention. Manshoon holds no Eye. Jarlaxle is the one Grand Game villain who treats every interaction as a transaction, and the Eye is currency he is willing to spend if the price is right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:384: ## Manshoon Parallel Strike as Rival Actor
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:386: Every lair heist needs a parallel heist actor: **Xanathar's Lair** has Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist, **Cassalanter Villa** has Esvele Rosznar, this quest has Manshoon's Zhentarim strike team. The parallel actor creates three-way pressure, provides a pressure-relief valve, and connects the current quest to future quests.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:388: Manshoon is the correct choice for this quest because: he has the intelligence resources (scrying, double agents) to know the Eye's location; his Zhentarim are the most violent faction, creating genuine danger contrast against Jarlaxle's charm; the strike team's pass-amulets to Kolat Towers provide a Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**; and on the alliance or patron path, the Zhent attack gives Jarlaxle a personal reason to send the party after Manshoon — transforming a clean diplomatic resolution into one that carries a new obligation and provides the strongest possible **Kolat Towers** entry.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:390: The strike always fires regardless of which resolution path the party chose. Manshoon's intelligence is independent of the party's relationship with Jarlaxle. This is a deliberate departure from **Cassalanter Villa**'s Esvele matrix, where the parallel actor could be avoided entirely — Manshoon is more aggressive and more consequential.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:426: **To Kolat Towers.** Two independent paths from this quest: Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) references Manshoon's operations and Kolat Towers; and the Zhentarim pass-amulets recovered from the Manshoon strike team (Scene 5) provide physical access to Kolat Towers. These join the existing paths from **Xanathar's Lair** (Samara's testimony from X7, Nihiloor's files from X23) and **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game from C6 or C22). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied to **Kolat Towers** through any combination of prior quests.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:1: # Kolat Towers
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:13: The Manshoon who now occupies Kolat Towers is a clone of a clone — a body activated in Undermountain when the Manshoon before it died, as the clone network was designed to do. Halaster Blackcloak, who tolerated the clone for a time out of professional curiosity, eventually severed Manshoon's left arm at the shoulder with a blade that prevented magical regrowth. The clone fled Undermountain carrying the staff of power he had smuggled in and arrived in Waterdeep to find the Kolat Towers abandoned, the force field still running on its original enchantment, and the amulet manufacture instructions in Duhlark's study. Manshoon needed a base. The towers were available.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:15: He spent two years building the Splinter cell — the faction that bears his name but operates independently of the Zhentarim's surviving leadership. The structure is hub-and-spoke: Kolat Towers as the command center, with the Interrogation House in the North Ward and Yellowspire in the Castle Ward serving as field offices connected by teleportation circles. A pass-amulet system replaced the need for a trusted courier network; amulets could be revoked remotely, confiscating access without confrontation. The towers' extradimensional sanctum — a space behind the towers that is not physically behind the towers, accessible only via a teleportation ring keyed to a specific circle in K22 — houses Manshoon's closest lieutenants and his most sensitive operations.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:17: The simulacrum in E7 is Manshoon's contingency against assassination. It was created seven years ago and has been ice-preserved since — a duplicate with half his hit points, none of his 6th-level or higher spell slots, and a different quality to its decision-making that those who know Manshoon well can identify after the fact. The simulacrum has never been activated outside of tests. Manshoon has never needed it. He is considering, for the first time in years, whether he might.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:19: The blackmail operation is what funds the Splinter's political reach. Corylus Thann, a Masked Lord, was photographed in a situation that would end his career; he supplies Guild intelligence in exchange for the ledger's continued privacy. Jelenn Urmbrusk accumulated significant debt to a Splinter front organization; his votes in the Council of Guildmasters follow Manshoon's preferences without his ever being asked directly. The ledger documenting these arrangements, and three others, lives in an arcane-locked drawer in Manshoon's study in E13. The identity list in the same drawer names Mirt, Thardouk Starbuckler, and Dorgar Adarbrent as Masked Lords — intelligence Manshoon has been accumulating for reasons that are not yet clear.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:25: **Purpose.** Convert prior-arc intelligence — the name "Manshoon," the Kolat Towers location, the pass-amulet system, the Splinter network — into an operational picture of a fortified position. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (a dungeon whose defenses the party can infiltrate around) or **Cassalanter Villa** (a villa with social cover) or **Sea Maidens Faire** (a carnival that looks like something other than a military installation), this briefing reveals a target that is overtly what it is: a wizard's fortress behind an impenetrable force field. The party cannot sneak up on Kolat Towers. They can only prepare better or worse for the assault.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:33: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* Harper agents have been watching the Kolat Towers block since **Faction Outposts**, tracking lieutenant movement. Mirt provides: exterior description and confirmed ward location (Southern Ward, not the North Ward — the misdirection in Samara's **Xanathar's Lair** testimony was fragmentary field intelligence); pass-amulet delivery patterns (Manshoon's couriers approach from two directions, suggesting two off-site entry points); the observation that the force field extends twelve feet above the roof and six feet below street level, making aerial approaches impossible and undermining impossible without specialized tools; and a warning that Watch patrol coverage in the surrounding streets has been quietly reduced over the past eighteen months — someone with civic influence arranged it. At renown 10+, Mirt activates a 48-hour Harper surveillance rotation: a field agent (spy stat block) delivers a written report covering patrol patterns by time of day, delivery schedules, and one verified force-field gap cycle including the gap's precise location, duration, and rotation frequency.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:43: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* The richest briefing. The Doom Raiders have been building toward Kolat Towers for longer than any other faction — six missions of accumulated intelligence over **Trollskull Alley** through **Faction Outposts**. What they provide depends on which missions the party completed: the pass-amulet distribution ledger (Mission 5), a diagram showing the three force-field gap locations and their 6-hour rotation cycle drawn by Ziraj during his own surveillance (Mission 6), a list of 43 identified Splinter operatives by name and role, and a garrison estimate. If Missions 5 and 6 are not complete, Tashlyn still provides the 43 names and garrison estimate — that intelligence has been accumulating since **Trollskull Alley**. At renown 10+, Davil commits to full joint-operation planning: synchronized entry timing, objective assignment between the party and the DR team, designated rendezvous point at K22, and a contingency signal protocol for communication mid-raid. Below renown 10+, the Doom Raiders' operation launches regardless — it always does — but coordination is improvised rather than planned.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:45: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle's interest in Kolat Towers is personal. If **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Manshoon parallel strike fired — and it always fires — Manshoon sent agents to attack Jarlaxle's ships in his own harbor. The experience left Jarlaxle more annoyed than alarmed, which for him is the more dangerous emotional state. "They came to my ship. In my harbor. I want to know where they came from, and I want it to stop." BD intelligence on Kolat Towers is ground-floor-only — the Bregan D'aerthe never penetrated past the second floor of the towers and have no information on the sanctum. What Jarlaxle provides: partial interior layout (K1 through K9), confirmation of the pass-amulet system by name and mechanism, confirmation that Sidra Romeir commands internal security, and the observation that Manshoon's agents carry amulets minted with a specific design that distinguishes them from amulets issued to the towers' service staff. If the party recovered pass-amulets from the defeated Zhentarim strike team during **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Scene 5, Jarlaxle confirms they work — those amulets bypass the force field and unlock the front door. At renown 10+, Jarlaxle provides one additional piece: "The wizard in those towers lost an arm in Undermountain. Whatever he is — and I have suspicions — he came up from below." The Halaster connection is stated without elaboration. It is the first hint that Manshoon's identity is stranger than a Zhentarim faction leader.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:53: **Purpose.** Steps 1–3 of the heist framework adapted for the raid genre. Kolat Towers does not offer the same casing texture as **Xanathar's Lair** (a dungeon with accessible entry points to scout), **Cassalanter Villa** (a villa with a public face), or **Sea Maidens Faire** (a carnival in which the party can wander freely). The force field makes exterior casing the only option. The party cannot see inside the towers before the operation begins. What they can learn is: entry timing, approach windows, and force-field gap mechanics. The Identifying the Score step is unusual in this quest — no Eye, no Stone impression — which means the party's motivation must come from somewhere else entirely.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:57: *Identifying the Score.* Kolat Towers holds no Eye. The Stone of Golorr offers no impression pointing to this location. The party's motivation is strategic: neutralize Manshoon before he ambushes them at the Vault, recover the blackmail ledger, seize intelligence that makes **Vault of Dragons** viable. This is the only lair heist where the party might legitimately ask "why are we doing this?" The answer comes from three convergent directions. First, every faction that provides intelligence about Manshoon eventually ends the same way — "and he will not stop." The ledger, the Vault approach intelligence, the political reach — leaving Manshoon operational means arriving at **Vault of Dragons** against an adversary who has had weeks to prepare. Second, Lady Gondafrey. Savra's briefing makes her existence and her location known; the party knows there is a prisoner in K13 who has been there for 113 years. Third, the intelligence haul itself has specific quest consequences that every contact will name if pressed. "The Blackstaff wants that spellbook. The Open Lord wants that ledger. Whatever Manshoon knows about the Vault, you want to know it first." The party should arrive at Scene 4 having decided what they are there to get.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:59: *Three Clue Rule — Paths to Kolat Towers.* All of the following were seeded in prior quests:
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:61: - Nihiloor's intelligence files (**Xanathar's Lair**, X23): partial Kolat Towers layout and Splinter lieutenant roster.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:62: - Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (**Cassalanter Villa**): references Manshoon and identifies the towers' location in the Southern Ward, correcting Samara's misdirection.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:63: - Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (**Sea Maidens Faire**, J30): references Manshoon's Splinter operations.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:64: - Zhentarim pass-amulets (**Sea Maidens Faire**, Scene 5): recovered from the defeated strike team — physical entry tokens.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:67: This quest is the convergence point for every faction's Manshoon intelligence. A party that spread across multiple quests arrives here having assembled the picture from multiple angles. A party that skipped most of those quests arrives having assembled less but still enough — Samara's testimony alone names the target.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:73: **Captured Pass-Amulets (via Avareen Windrivver, Agorn Fuoco, or the **Sea Maidens Faire** Zhentarim strike team).** Amulets recovered from any of these sources allow the party to walk through the force field to K1 (the ground-floor entrance) without triggering it. The amulets also prevent the force field's passive alarm from registering unauthorized entry. Advantage: social window — Sidra will not attack immediately on sight at Unaware or Suspicious tier, creating 1–3 minutes of ambiguous contact before the operation's nature becomes clear. Disadvantage: the approach is on the street, visible from the towers' windows. At Alert tier, captured amulets require a DC 14 Intelligence (Arcana) check to recalibrate before use — a failure produces an audible rejection tone that the garrison hears. At Lockdown tier, captured amulets are invalid; only freshly issued amulets function.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:79: **Tone.** Compressed and decisive. The raid genre imposes its own time pressure before the operation even begins. Every hour the party spends observing the towers is an hour Manshoon has to prepare. The Founders' Day clock, the Vault approach intelligence sitting in E9 and E13, and Manshoon's active **Vault of Dragons** preparations all push against extended casing. The party should feel that the optimal casing window is shorter than they would like, and that waiting longer costs something.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:91: Lady Gondafrey is a secondary objective, not a recruited ally — the party will not know her current alignment state before entering K13. Savra's briefing establishes her existence and the moral case for her extraction. What she provides if freed depends on her alignment: a lawful-good Gondafrey is the most valuable ally in the towers, with 113 years of architectural knowledge and a deeply personal motivation to see Manshoon's operation destroyed; a chaotic-evil Gondafrey is an active threat. The DC 14 Wisdom (Insight) check that detects her current state is available only after K13 is reached. The party should decide, before entering the towers, whether extracting Lady Gondafrey is a primary objective, a secondary objective, or a risk they are not willing to take.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:99: | Tier | Force Field | Garrison State | Manshoon & Simulacrum | Timer |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:101: | Unaware | Standard; gaps on normal rotation | Standard roster; Sidra assumes visitors are guests until credentials fail | Manshoon studying in E13; simulacrum dormant in E7 | 30 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:102: | Suspicious | Gaps shifted 15 min from diagram; amulets work | +2 thugs at K2; Sidra checks credentials before allowing passage | Manshoon studying in E13; simulacrum dormant in E7 | 25 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:103: | Alert | Gaps shifted 30 min; amulets require DC 14 Arcana to recalibrate | Posts doubled; K22 adds 2 thugs to standard spectator/snake garrison; Manafret poisons all kitchen food | Manshoon in E12, staff in hand; simulacrum activated in E1 as decoy | 20 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:104: | Lockdown | Gaps sealed; only fresh amulets work; Yellowspire circle still functions | Full garrison at battle stations; Sidra does not ask questions | Manshoon in E12, defensive spells prepared; simulacrum activated in E1, briefed on party capabilities via Skeemo's intelligence if he survived **Faction Outposts** | 15 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:112: **Purpose.** The raid itself. Step 5 of the heist framework. Full operational content for both locations: Kolat Towers (K-series areas) and the extradimensional sanctum (E-series areas). Unlike the prior lair heists — which offer genuine stealth windows throughout the operation — Kolat Towers compresses the stealthy phase to the approach and entry. Once combat begins in the towers, the reinforcement timer starts, and the primary strategic variable becomes speed.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:116: *Running This Scene.* The critical path runs K1/K22 (entry) → K2/K3 (Sidra, Manafret, teleporter ring) → K22 (circle to sanctum) → E1/E2/E3 (sanctum foyer) → deeper sanctum → E12 or E13 (Manshoon). Everything else is optional. A party that clears every room fights a garrison that gets larger with every minute they spend. A party that moves with purpose reaches Manshoon before the timer reaches its hardest entries on the schedule. The GM communicates timer progression through environmental cues: flying snake couriers crossing between towers, shouted orders echoing from below, boots on stairs that were silent a minute ago.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:118: Jarlaxle's position and the simulacrum's state are predecided by escalation tier. At Unaware and Suspicious, Manshoon is studying in E13 and the simulacrum is dormant in E7. At Alert and Lockdown, Manshoon is in E12 with the staff of power in hand and the simulacrum is activated and standing in E1. These positions do not change during Scene 4 unless the party's actions force Manshoon to move — and Manshoon moves toward a confrontation, not away from one.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:120: *Kolat Towers — Critical Path Areas.*
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:122: **K1 — Ground-Floor Entrance.** The front door. Only relevant for pass-amulet entry. A Zhentarim thug behind a viewing port opens it if the amulet check passes, admits the visitors to the narrow entry hall, and moves to alert Sidra in K2. At Unaware tier, this happens without alarm. At Suspicious and Alert, the thug calls for Sidra before the door is fully open.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:132: **K13 — Holding Cell.** Lady Gondafrey. She has been in this room for 113 years. The alignment shift mechanic: her alignment resets at dawn each day, shifting on a d10 — but per standing campaign design rules, the GM does not roll. Her alignment for the operation is determined by when the party arrives relative to dawn: if the party enters the towers before dawn, she is at whatever state she was at last night's reset, which is chaotic-evil (Manshoon uses the alignment oscillation as a psychological tool — she is sometimes herself and sometimes not, and she cannot be certain which she is). If the party enters after dawn and within 4 hours of it, she is lawful-good. If the party enters more than 4 hours after dawn, she is drifting — treat as true neutral, cooperative but not fully capable of tactical coordination. The party's entry timing therefore determines which version of Lady Gondafrey they find.
