If she is lawful-good: she provides a complete verbal floor plan of both towers and the sanctum (she memorized the layout over decades), identifies the anchor in K18 by name ("the rune room — destroy the stone in the wall, the field falls"), and offers to fight alongside the party with whatever she can find. She is a skilled combatant at CR 5 equivalent (veteran stat block, with innate arcane disruption abilities from her Order training). If she is chaotic-evil: she attacks the party on sight.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:134: **K15 — Summoning Chamber.** 3 apprentice wizard minions (22 HP each) in active ritual, binding a barlgura in the summoning circle. The apprentices are the minimum complement required to maintain the binding — disrupting any one of them (dropping them below half HP or breaking their concentration) releases the demon. Released, the barlgura attacks the nearest creature, friend or enemy, for 3 rounds, then rampages through the towers following noise. The trade-off: a released barlgura draws 2–4 Zhentarim defenders from other positions to contain it, creating a tactical window for the party — but it also triggers the reinforcement timer if not already running, and poses genuine injury risk. The barlgura ignores pass-amulets.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:136: **K17 — Flesh Golem Access Corridor.** A pressure plate 10 feet before the K18 door releases a cloud of contact poison gas (DC 13 Constitution saving throw, 14 (4d6) poison damage on a failed save; the poisoned condition lasts 1 minute on failure). DC 14 Perception to notice the plate before stepping on it; DC 13 Thieves' Tools to disable it. The flesh golem in the corridor guards the approach to K18 and does not have pass-amulet discrimination — it attacks anything moving toward K18 that does not carry a specific command stone (in Manshoon's possession).
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:138: **K18 — Arcane Rune (Force Field Anchor).** A stone rune set into the east wall of a bare room — the physical anchor for the force field. The rune radiates strong abjuration. The moment it takes any damage (AC 10, 22 HP), a red slaad teleports into the room. The slaad is Manshoon's failsafe — it attacks the party until destroyed or dismissed. The slaad does not discriminate; it will attack Zhentarim defenders as readily as the party. Destroying the rune drops the force field permanently — which may not be desirable if the party still has defenders to avoid on the way out.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:140: **K19 — Duhlark's Bedroom.** A flameskull patrols this room. A secret compartment beneath the floorboards (DC 14 Investigation) contains a wand of binding with 3 charges remaining — Duhlark's contingency item, missed by Manshoon's initial search because the flameskull's combat-mode activation damaged the floor when it first triggered on Manshoon's arrival.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:146: **E1 — Arrival Point.** The sanctum's receiving room. Glass walls show purple mist beyond. At Unaware and Suspicious tiers, E1 is empty — the sanctum is at normal operating posture. At Alert and Lockdown tiers, the simulacrum stands here. It looks exactly like Manshoon — same height, same robes, same silver hair, the same artificial left arm. It does not speak first. It evaluates.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:148: **E2 — Foyer.** 12 copper Masks of Manshoon mounted on the walls discharge 5 (1d10) lightning damage each when triggered by a Zhentarim coin pressure plate in the floor (DC 14 Perception to notice; DC 12 Thieves' Tools or DC 13 Arcana to disarm). The masks discharge simultaneously, targeting every creature in the room. This is the sanctum's entry alarm — the discharge also sends a magical pulse to E3, alerting Kaevja.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:150: **E3 — Guard Station.** Kaevja Cynavern (mage), Sidra's counterpart in the sanctum. Kaevja carries a yellow elemental gem (earth elemental) and has the sending spell prepared at all times. If she is not eliminated silently before she acts, her first action is to cast sending to Manshoon — regardless of his location — and to Agorn Fuoco or Vevette Blackwater in E8. A successful sending accelerates the reinforcement timer by 5 minutes. Kaevja is the sanctum's force multiplier: her sending is more dangerous than her combat capability. The party should treat her as a priority target.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:154: **E7 — Simulacrum Chamber.** Extremely cold; breath fogs at the door. An ice sarcophagus occupies the center of the room. At Unaware and Suspicious tiers, the simulacrum is here, dormant inside the sarcophagus — it activates automatically within 1 minute of Manshoon being incapacitated or killed. A party that reaches and defeats Manshoon in E13 without checking E7 may face a second Manshoon arriving behind them from this room without warning. At Alert and Lockdown tiers, the sarcophagus is empty — the simulacrum is already activated and standing in E1. The empty sarcophagus is readable: frost patterns inside show recent occupancy. A DC 13 Intelligence (Arcana) check identifies the sarcophagus as a simulacrum preservation vessel and concludes that the simulacrum has been recently removed, which means a duplicate is active somewhere in the sanctum.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:156: **E8 — Reading Room.** Vevette Blackwater + Agorn Fuoco (if survived **Faction Outposts**). Vevette fights; Agorn does not if the fight is going badly — he offers his ring (he carries one), Manshoon's daily routine, and the ledger's location in exchange for being allowed to leave. A green flame brazier in the center of the room: any creature saying "green flame" within 10 feet of the brazier while aware of its function triggers a fireball (8d6, DC 15 Dexterity saving throw). A DC 14 Investigation check on the brazier reveals the inscription. Manshoon told the lieutenants; Agorn will mention it if he is negotiating.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:158: **E9 — Library and Astral Vault.** The library is accessible normally. The vault at the back of the room is a bloodlocked mechanism: DC 20 Thieves' Tools to bypass with disadvantage, or DC 17 Arcana to bypass via dispel magic or disguise self (making the magical bloodsensor read the user as Manshoon). Inside: 3 spare pass-amulets, 2 spare teleporter rings, and Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game. The Report is the critical **Vault of Dragons** intelligence — it describes the Brandath Crypts approach, identifies Aurinax as "a guardian of considerable patience and considerable power," includes a partially decoded Elvish command phrase, and names Manshoon's analysis of all four faction positions in the Grand Game. This document goes directly to **Vault of Dragons** preparation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:160: **E10 — Audience Chamber.** Havia Quickknife + Mookie Plush, two halfling adepts who serve as Manshoon's household fanatics. Both fight to death. They are the sanctum's most physically capable non-spellcasting combatants — trained in martial arts, faster than their size suggests, and entirely loyal. They are also the least tactically sophisticated opponents in the sanctum; they attack in a straight line and do not retreat.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:162: **E12 — Manshoon's Quarters.** At Alert and Lockdown tiers, Manshoon is here when the party arrives — staff of power in hand, moving around the room with the controlled energy of someone who has been waiting for a fight. At Lockdown, he has cast mage armor, mind blank, and globe of invulnerability in the past hour. At Unaware and Suspicious, E12 is empty — Manshoon is in E13. A hidden staff compartment in the north wall (DC 16 Investigation) holds a spare arcane staff of ordinary make — not a staff of power, but a plausible decoy for a party who finds an empty room and decides to wait.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:164: Manshoon talks first. This is not a courtesy — he is assessing. He wants to know who sent the party, whether they have the Stone, whether the Vault approach has been compromised, and whether any of them represent a faction he might negotiate with. If the party has something he wants, he offers a trade. If they do not, he offers mercy: "Leave. Tell no one. This is the only time I will make this offer." If they refuse: he uses the simulacrum as a shield if it is present and activated, wall of force to isolate the most dangerous party member, dominate person on the second most dangerous. He does not commit to lethal combat until cornered. The Retributive Strike Gambit: at 30 HP or below, Manshoon grips the staff of power and states, clearly, that he will break it. This is not a bluff — a broken staff of power in E12 releases a 30-foot-radius force explosion dealing 8 × charges remaining force damage with a DC 17 Dexterity saving throw for half. The party must find a way to disarm him (DC 16 Sleight of Hand, a targeted attack to knock the staff from his grip, or a sufficiently compelling negotiation that takes him below the 30 HP threshold of despair) or accept the consequences.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:166: **E13 — Manshoon's Study.** At Unaware and Suspicious tiers, Manshoon is here — books open, the Stone of Golorr's history spread across the desk, notes in cramped Elvish describing the Vault's defenders and their known weaknesses. The party finds him at work. He reacts the same way he would in E12 — he talks first — but the setting is different: he is at a desk, not armed and waiting. He requires one action to reach the staff (on a weapon stand beside the desk) before the Retributive Strike Gambit becomes available. At Alert and Lockdown, E13 is empty. A helmed horror guards the spellbook shelf regardless of tier — it attacks any creature other than Manshoon who approaches the shelves. The blackmail ledger is in an arcane-locked drawer in the desk (DC 17 Thieves' Tools to bypass the lock; DC 15 Arcana to bypass via dispel magic or knock; knock works).
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:168: *Adversary Roster — Standard Configuration.* The full garrison is present in both towers and the sanctum. If Skeemo Weirdbottle survived **Gralhund Villa** or **Faction Outposts**, he is in the sanctum (the GM should note whether Skeemo escaped during those arcs). Skeemo recognizes the party and immediately alerts the garrison to their capabilities — he knows which party members are the primary threats, which spell slots were visible during their prior encounter, and which tactics worked against them before. His presence at Alert or Lockdown tiers means Manshoon has been briefed.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:175: | 5 min | 2 thugs arrive at K22 from the Interrogation House via the Yellowspire circle. Kaevja's sending reaches Manshoon and field agents if not silenced. |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:177: | 15 min | Manshoon (at Unaware/Suspicious, if not yet reached) moves from E13 to E12, retrieving the staff of power. The simulacrum (if dormant in E7) activates and moves to defend the sanctum. |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:179: | 25 min | If Manshoon was absent from the sanctum: his sending reply arrives. Simulacrum, if still operational, begins preparing final defensive position. |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:186: **Tone.** Violent and compressed. This is the campaign's most combat-intensive lair arc by design — the raid genre tests combat capability and speed, not social navigation or spatial puzzle-solving. The party that moves with purpose encounters a manageable garrison. The party that clears every room first, or that pauses to assess after each engagement, arrives at Manshoon facing a garrison that has had time to reinforce.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:192: **Purpose.** What goes wrong, the Doom Raiders parallel operation, the Manshoon confrontation and identity reveal, and extraction. The complications in **Kolat Towers** are not the rolling improvisation of **Sea Maidens Faire**'s caper structure — they are consequences of the raid's violence accumulating on both sides.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:196: *The Doom Raiders Parallel Operation.* Always fires. Davil Starsong and Tashlyn Yafeera have been building toward Kolat Towers for six missions — more deliberate preparation than any other parallel actor in the campaign. They are not waiting for the party's permission. Their operation launches on the same night the party raids, timed to maximize pressure on the garrison rather than to coordinate with the party's specific movements.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:198: If a PC is a Doom Raiders member, Tashlyn operates as a field ally. She briefs the party before the raid (Scene 3 coordination), her team enters via a different entry method than the party, and during Scene 4 she responds to the party's tactical situation with genuine cooperation — relaying garrison positions via hand signals if they encounter each other, pulling opposition off the party's flank, and prioritizing objectives that overlap with the party's (Manshoon's operational records, pass-amulet system destruction, Vevette Blackwater captured alive). If no PC is a DR member, the party discovers the Doom Raiders mid-raid — an independent force hitting the same target on the same night, with no coordination and no standing obligation to cooperate.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:204: | PCs in the sanctum | DR fight through towers toward K22 | DR may follow into the sanctum 10–15 minutes after party, arriving as reinforcements during Manshoon confrontation |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:205: | PCs defeated Manshoon, securing objectives | DR arrive, claim Manshoon's operational records | Negotiation over spoils; DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion) for full share |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:209: *Facing Manshoon.* Regardless of which room the party reaches him in, Manshoon talks first. The conversation is an assessment — he wants to know what the party knows about the Vault, whether they have the Stone, and whether there is anything they want badly enough to trade for. His opening is never a threat; it is a question. "What do you actually want?" If the answer reveals that they have come for the ledger, he does not offer to trade it — that leverage is too important. If the answer reveals they have come for the Report on the Grand Game, he may be willing to discuss an arrangement, because the party having accurate Vault intelligence does not hurt him if he reaches the Vault first. If the answer reveals they have come to neutralize him entirely: "Then at least you're honest." Combat.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:211: Manshoon's combat priorities in order: the simulacrum as a shield (if present), wall of force to isolate the most dangerous party member, dominate person, and sustained concentration-based control rather than nova damage output. He is not trying to kill the party quickly — he is trying to exhaust them, because a fresh opponent makes better decisions than a desperate one. The Retributive Strike Gambit is real and available at 30 HP.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:213: *The Manshoon Identity Reveal.* This is **Kolat Towers**'s dramatic centerpiece. Three independent evidence trails converge:
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:215: Documents in E9 (Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game, which refers to "my predecessor" and "the original network" in a way that implies historical continuity without explaining it) + a sigil on documents in E13 (DC 14 Intelligence (History) to identify as the historical Zhentarim founder's personal mark, in use 200 years before the current Manshoon was born) + Lady Gondafrey's testimony, if she was freed ("He told me he founded the Zhentarim centuries ago. I thought he was mad. Then I saw what was left of his arm — the joint where it ends, the way it ends — and I stopped thinking he was mad.") combine to establish:
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:217: - Manshoon is a clone of a clone of the original Zhentarim founder, one of multiple surviving clone bodies.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:220: - The simulacrum mechanic: Manshoon can create ice-preserved duplicates with limited capabilities.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:221: - Manshoon's political reach extends to Masked Lords — his blackmail operation is decades in the making.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:223: No single piece is sufficient on its own. The party assembles the reveal from the documents they recover and the witnesses they find. A party that found Lady Gondafrey, read E13's documents, and identified the sigil has the full picture. A party that skipped K13 or didn't attempt E9's vault has a partial picture — enough to understand what Manshoon is, not enough to fully document it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:235: **Tone.** The aftermath of violence. **Kolat Towers**'s escape is not slipping away — it is emerging from a warzone. The party exits carrying wounded, carrying recovered documents, carrying a functional understanding of something that should not exist. The caper genre's clean escape is not available here. They got out.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:241: **Purpose.** Resolve the raid. No Eye to insert, no Stone upgrade. Instead: deliver the intelligence haul, record the Manshoon faction state, stage the identity reveal through faction debriefs, and position the party for **Vault of Dragons**. Scene 6 is shorter than in prior quests — the dramatic question of **Kolat Towers** resolves on a strategic rather than an emotional register. The party has done something violent and decisive; the aftermath reflects that.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:247: **The Blackmail Ledger (E13 desk).** Delivering it to Laeral Silverhand through Mirt or Vajra collapses Manshoon's political position — Corylus Thann and Jelenn Urmbrusk are exposed, their influence over Watch patrol assignments and Guildmaster votes evaporates, and two of Manshoon's most valuable civic tools are neutralized regardless of whether Manshoon himself survived. The identity list on the back pages names five Masked Lords total: Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk, Mirt, Thardouk Starbuckler, and Dorgar Adarbrent. Mirt will want a word with the party privately before the ledger goes to Laeral, not to suppress the names but to explain them: "I volunteered. The others didn't."
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:249: **Manshoon's Spellbook (E13 shelves, guarded by helmed horror).** A 9th-level wizard's working spellbook — 42 spells across all schools, with marginalia indicating active research into planar travel and gate mechanics. Vajra offers 15,000 gp and a favor from the Blackstaff's office in exchange for the spellbook, primarily for what its marginalia reveals about Manshoon's research trajectory. The Doom Raiders offer 8,000 gp and priority intelligence access. Jarlaxle offers, characteristically, "a conversation about the future" rather than a price — he is interested in the planar travel research for reasons he does not specify.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:251: **Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (E9 astral vault).** The most directly actionable **Vault of Dragons** item. Describes the Brandath Crypts approach in architectural detail, identifies Aurinax by name and species, includes a partially decoded Elvish command phrase (the party has the partial — the complete phrase will require additional research or the Stone's Full Awakening), and analyzes all four Grand Game factions' positions as of the report's date. The analysis is accurate. Manshoon's intelligence on the vault is as complete as anyone's outside the original Masked Lords who created it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:253: **Lady Gondafrey's Freedom (K13, if freed).** Gondafrey is not merely a rescued prisoner — she is a living witness who can testify before the Masked Lords Council about Manshoon's political infiltration, the blackmail operation's scope, and the simulacrum's nature. Her 113-year captivity is itself evidence. Savra will take Gondafrey to the Order's care immediately and arrange the testimony. Gondafrey herself has one request: to be present when the ledger is delivered to Laeral.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:255: *Faction State: "Manshoon Operational?"*
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:259: - **Destroyed.** Both the original Manshoon and the simulacrum were eliminated during the raid. The Splinter cell collapses within a tenday — Vevette (if alive) attempts to hold the surviving operatives together but lacks Manshoon's authority and his clone-network insurance. No Manshoon at the Vault. The party arrives at **Vault of Dragons** facing Aurinax without a third-party obstacle.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:260: - **Simulacrum Only.** The original Manshoon was destroyed but the simulacrum survived and escaped (or was not found). The simulacrum has no 6th-level or higher spell slots and no staff of power. It arrives at the Vault with reduced capabilities and diminished tactical creativity — it can execute plans Manshoon taught it but cannot adapt to novel situations effectively.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:261: - **Alive.** Manshoon survived (escaped, negotiated, or was never reached). He arrives at the Vault at full strength with surviving lieutenants and the staff of power. The hardest **Vault of Dragons** configuration. He will have had time to prepare.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:265: **Mirt (Harpers).** Wants the ledger, and specifically wants to review the identity list before Laeral receives it. "I volunteered" is the context he provides — he became a Masked Lord knowing Manshoon's network existed and chose to be legible to it rather than to fight it from cover. He is not apologizing. If the Manshoon Identity Reveal is fully assembled, Mirt is quiet for a moment before responding: "Clone of the Founder. Of course. That's why he could never be bargained with — he has nowhere to retreat to. He's already been everywhere."
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:267: **Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance).** "Is Manshoon neutralized?" Direct and operational. If alive: "Then he will be at the Vault. We plan accordingly." If destroyed: brief satisfaction, then immediately to the next question — the identity list and its implications for Watch patrol integrity. He will want to know which Watch officers Manshoon's Masked Lord contacts influenced.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:273: **Vajra Safahr (Force Grey).** "The spellbook. Tell me about the spellbook." Vajra's interest is less in the combat applications and more in the marginalia — Manshoon's planar travel research represents an intelligence gap Force Grey has been aware of for years. She also wants the construction specifications for the extradimensional sanctum: "A personal extradimensional space of that scale, anchored to a physical structure, is not a known construction technique. He developed it. I want to know how." If the party destroyed the sanctum without recovering the specifications, she is professionally disappointed.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:275: **Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders).** "Is it done?" If the DR participated in the raid, Tashlyn's debrief is businesslike — which objectives were achieved, which gaps remain, what the operational records recovered tell them about surviving Splinter personnel. If Vevette Blackwater was captured: "Good. She has things to answer for. So does the rest of what's left of that network." The Doom Raiders' internal war with the Manshoon Splinter ends here, one way or another. If the Splinter was only partially dismantled, Davil begins planning follow-up operations without asking the party's involvement.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:277: **Jarlaxle (if contacted in Scene 1).** If the Manshoon parallel strike fired in **Sea Maidens Faire** and the party helped or acknowledged it: "Now we're even. Or close enough." Jarlaxle wants to read the Report on the Grand Game — specifically Manshoon's analysis of BD's position in the Grand Game, which he suspects is more accurate than anyone outside BD has any right to be. If the party assembled the full Manshoon Identity Reveal: Jarlaxle confirms the one piece he withheld from his Scene 1 briefing. "I said I had suspicions. The arm was one. There is another thing about him — the way he holds the staff. No one born to a wand holds a staff like that. You hold it like something that has always been yours." He suspected. He wanted to see if the party could find the rest.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:279: *Campaign Calendar.* **Kolat Towers** consumes 1–3 tendays total: several days of intelligence gathering and preparation (Scenes 1–3), 1 day for the operation and immediate escape (Scenes 4–5), and 2–4 days of recovery and debriefs (Scene 6). The operation itself — Scenes 4 and 5 — is the shortest single-session window of any lair heist. Update the campaign calendar accordingly. The Founders' Day clock is relevant here only as context: **Kolat Towers** consumes roughly the same calendar window as the Founders' Day events, which provides cover for Watch attention being elsewhere.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:281: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr with between 0 and 3 Eyes. If 3 Eyes are restored, **Vault of Dragons** launches — the Full Awakening has already occurred and the Vault's location is known; **Kolat Towers**'s intelligence confirms the approach route. If Eyes remain unrestored, other lair heists are still available; the intelligence haul from **Kolat Towers** (specifically Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game) enriches whichever quests follow. The Report's partial command phrase is a direct **Vault of Dragons** hook regardless of Eye count — the party knows the phrase is partially decoded and that completing it requires either the Stone's Full Awakening or additional research.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:283: **Tone.** Tactical decompression. **Kolat Towers**'s aftermath has a different texture than the prior lair heists' endings: no revelatory awakening (**Xanathar's Lair**), no moral reckoning (**Cassalanter Villa**), no relationship reassessment (**Sea Maidens Faire**). This is strategic positioning — the party has done a violent thing that was necessary, has recovered intelligence that changes the **Vault of Dragons** situation, and has set a faction state flag that will determine what they face at the Vault. Professional satisfaction or grim assessment, depending on how the operation went.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:293: | 4–7 | Kolat Towers | The party completes the Kolat Towers raid | 4 |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:295: **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** each award the same 4 Milestone Points, regardless of order. When a heist is converted to a quest journal, the 4 points sit on four milestone events (1 point each). All four heists can be run in any order at any point after **Faction Outposts**. Track cumulative points against the Milestone Points progression table (L5 at 13, L6 at 18, L7 at 23). The pace is order-agnostic: the *first* completed heist reaches level 5, the *second* reaches level 6, and level 7 requires *all four*, because three heists leave the total one point short. One of the four heists is always Kolat Towers, so level 7 is unreachable without it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:307: **Kolat Towers**'s transition is not open-ended in the same way as prior lair heists. The remaining task is **Vault of Dragons**. The intelligence haul — Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game, the Blackmail Ledger, the partial command phrase — feeds directly into **Vault of Dragons** preparation. If the party completes **Kolat Towers** with all three Eyes restored, **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:313: - **Faction Outposts**, Scene 4 (Manshoon Outposts) — entry methods seeded here (Yellowspire circle, pass-amulets). Doom Raiders Missions 5–6 produce the ledger and gap diagram.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:315: - **Xanathar's Lair**, X23 (Nihiloor's Intelligence Files) — partial Kolat Towers layout and lieutenant roster. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:316: - **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game) — identifies Kolat Towers' Southern Ward location. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:317: - **Sea Maidens Faire**, J30 (Jarlaxle's Report) — references Manshoon's operations. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:318: - **Sea Maidens Faire**, Scene 5 (Zhentarim Strike Team) — pass-amulets recoverable from defeated Zhentarim. Entry method for **Kolat Towers**. Jarlaxle's "They came to my ship" motivation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:319: - **Vault of Dragons** — "Manshoon operational?" flag + intelligence haul (Report on the Grand Game, partial command phrase) directly shape the Vault approach.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:320: - **Notable Figures** — Existing profiles: Manshoon, Sidra Romeir, Manafret Cherryport, Vevette Blackwater, Agorn Fuoco, Urstul Floxin, Lady Gondafrey, Skeemo Weirdbottle. New profiles needed: Kaevja Cynavern, Havia Quickknife, Mookie Plush, Yorn the Terror.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:321: - **the Bestiary (not yet drafted)** — Boss stat block needed: Manshoon (with simulacrum mechanic distinction). Standard stat blocks needed: Havia Quickknife, Mookie Plush.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:323: - **Doom Raiders** Factions guide page — Quest Hooks section; full operation write-up for the Kolat Towers parallel action.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:324: - **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) — Manshoon faction overview, escalation framework, Kolat Towers lair entry (Southern Ward).
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:327: - **Undermountain (future)** — Manshoon's Halaster connection seeds Level 8 content. The Kolat Brothers' extradimensional sanctum construction technique seeds Dweomercore on Level 9.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:331: # Design Notes: Kolat Towers
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:335: **Kolat Towers** is a raid — the fourth and final genre in the lair heist sequence. **Xanathar's Lair** was dungeon infiltration (stealth and navigation). **Cassalanter Villa** was social infiltration (identity and exposure management). **Sea Maidens Faire** was a caper (improvisation and rolling adaptation). **Kolat Towers** is a raid: the party assaults a fortified position with overwhelming violence, and the primary strategic variable is speed. The reinforcement timer is not a complication added to the raid — it is the genre's defining mechanic. Every quest in the sequence tests different party capabilities. **Kolat Towers** tests combat effectiveness and decision-making under time pressure.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:339: Four quests, four genres, because the party has four different capability sets that a single-genre sequence cannot test. The raid placement at the end of the sequence is deliberate: by **Kolat Towers**, the party has demonstrated stealth capability (**Xanathar's Lair**), social intelligence (**Cassalanter Villa**), and improvisational flexibility (**Sea Maidens Faire**). The raid asks whether they can leverage all of those in a context where they cannot afford to use any of them thoughtfully — speed collapses the time for planning, and the reinforcement timer punishes hesitation. A party that treats the raid like a dungeon crawl (clearing every room before advancing) loses to the timer. A party that treats it like a caper (improvising room by room without a plan) arrives at Manshoon underpowered. The raid rewards parties who have internalized the prior quests' lessons and can execute without pausing to plan.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:343: **Kolat Towers** is the only lair heist without an Eye recovery. The structural risk is that the scene feels lower-stakes than **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, or **Sea Maidens Faire** — that without the Stone's awakening, the climax lacks weight. The intelligence haul addresses this by giving each recovered item a specific, named **Vault of Dragons** consequence that the party understands before they enter the towers. The blackmail ledger is not "valuable treasure" — it is the political tool that neutralizes Manshoon's Masked Lord influence. The Report on the Grand Game is not "lore" — it contains the partial command phrase that opens the Vault. Every item in the haul has a downstream consequence that the party can predict, which makes recovering it feel as meaningful as restoring an Eye.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:345: The Manshoon Identity Reveal replaces the Stone's awakening as the quest's dramatic centerpiece. Where the Stone awakening in prior quests revealed something about the campaign's hidden architecture (Nihiloor's domination in **Xanathar's Lair**, the Cassalanters' debt in **Cassalanter Villa**, the aboleth consciousness in **Sea Maidens Faire**), the Identity Reveal reveals something about the antagonist's nature that recontextualizes every Manshoon interaction the party has had since **Faction Outposts**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:349: The simulacrum's predecided positioning creates two structurally different encounter sequences depending on escalation tier. At Alert and Lockdown, the party encounters the simulacrum first in E1 — they may believe they have defeated Manshoon before they reach E12. At Unaware and Suspicious, the party reaches Manshoon first in E13 and may not discover the simulacrum in E7 until it activates behind them.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:351: Three discoverable clues let the party identify which version they fought: the simulacrum cannot cast 6th-level or higher spells (DC 15 Arcana to notice the pattern after observing its spell selection); the simulacrum has roughly half the hit points of the original (a party that tracked damage dealt will notice the fight ended faster than expected); and Lady Gondafrey, if freed, knows that Manshoon "sometimes sends a quieter version of himself" and can describe the behavioral difference after the fact.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:353: The ambiguity is a puzzle, not a gotcha. The clues are present and discoverable. A party that notices and investigates walks out of the sanctum knowing exactly what they fought. A party that does not notice and investigate may discover the answer in the **Vault of Dragons** confrontation instead — when they arrive at the Vault expecting a diminished Manshoon and encounter a full-strength one.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:357: The Doom Raiders have more setup investment than any other parallel actor in the campaign — six missions across **Trollskull Alley** through **Faction Outposts**, each contributing a piece of the intelligence picture that culminates in the Kolat Towers operation. This investment gives their parallel action credibility that a sudden appearance would not have. They were not deployed for **Kolat Towers** because **Kolat Towers** is dramatic — they were deployed for **Kolat Towers** because they have been building toward it since the beginning of the campaign and they have finally run out of reasons to wait.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:361: ## The Manshoon Identity Reveal
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:363: The three-quest reveal structure — Name in **Faction Outposts**, Identity in **Kolat Towers**, Motivation in **Vault of Dragons** — treats Manshoon differently than the other three villain faction leaders. Xanathar is immediately legible; Jarlaxle makes himself legible on his own terms; the Cassalanters are legible once the party reaches their temple. Manshoon is the one faction leader whose true nature requires assembly from distributed evidence, because his true nature is genuinely strange — a clone of a 200-year-old wizard, not just a Zhentarim leader — and because understanding it changes the party's strategic relationship with him in a way that understanding the other faction leaders does not.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:365: A party that assembles the full reveal understands why Manshoon cannot negotiate the ledger away (he cannot expose the operation without exposing himself), why he fled Undermountain (Halaster is one of the few beings who could destroy him permanently), and why his Vault ambitions are less about the gold than about the political legitimacy 500,000 dragons would purchase. This is the Motivation that **Vault of Dragons** will confirm.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:369: Each lair heist has a moment that asks the party to weigh a consequence against a tactical advantage: smokepowder demolition in **Xanathar's Lair**, the children's sacrifice in **Cassalanter Villa**, Zelifarn's crystal ball in **Sea Maidens Faire**. **Kolat Towers**'s equivalent is the Retributive Strike Gambit. If the party is winning the fight against Manshoon, they must decide how to prevent him from breaking the staff — which means either disarming him while he's still dangerous enough to threaten the gambit, or accepting the explosion, or finding a third path (negotiation that takes him past his desperation threshold without combat reaching 30 HP). The gambit compresses **Kolat Towers**'s moral dimension into a single decision point because the raid genre does not leave space for extended moral deliberation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:373: The escalation tier system reaches its harshest expression in **Kolat Towers**. Lockdown tier halves the timer (30 to 15 minutes), seals the force-field gaps, invalidates captured amulets, and pre-activates the simulacrum. A party that hit every Manshoon outpost in **Faction Outposts** arrives at Kolat Towers with Lockdown already active — which means the fastest timer, the fewest entry options, and the best-prepared garrison. This is the correct consequence of aggressive **Faction Outposts** play. The difficulty is not a punishment; it is a calibration. The party that did the most to expose and destabilize the Splinter network is also the party most capable of running the raid under the hardest conditions.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:19: As the Stone's third Eye clicks into its socket — in whichever lair delivered it — every surviving player in the Grand Game receives the same piece of information at the same time: the vault is real, it is beneath the Brandath Crypts, and it can now be opened. Xanathar, if it survives, prefers the gold stay buried and the Stone stay lost — a vault it cannot reach is still leverage it can threaten to use. The Cassalanters, depending on how the party left them, either wait on a promise or plan to take by force what they were once willing to earn. Manshoon, who holds no Eye, has been counting on exactly this moment — he intends to let someone else do the work of opening the vault and then take it from whoever succeeds. And in Neverwinter, word of a Waterdeep in open faction war over a half-million gold pieces has already reached a man who has been waiting years for a reason to come home. Dagult Neverember is packing.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:34: | Manshoon operational? | **Kolat Towers** Scene 6 (if run) | Destroyed / Simulacrum Only / Alive. If **Kolat Towers** was never run, treat as **Alive**, undiminished, and unidentified — the party has not yet assembled the Identity Reveal. |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:45: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* If Lords' Alliance Mission 6 succeeded, Jalester delivers the Masked Lord's Writ's practical terms one final time: the Vault district gate opens to it, Watch officers defer to it within four rounds of presentation, and Laeral has one legal intervention available during **Vault of Dragons** — usable once, for a rival faction complication, a legal challenge, or an NPC arrest. If the party argued Laeral out of the Manshoon leverage offer, Jalester confirms it: "He has no card to play with her. Whatever he tries at the vault, it won't be that." If the offer was accepted instead, Jalester warns that Manshoon can still delay Watch cooperation for up to two hours, or threaten to expose a Masked Lord publicly, if he reaches the vault able to make good on it. At renown 10+, Jalester personally escorts the party to the Vault district gate and stands with them through the Watch's initial questioning, cutting what would otherwise be a lengthy procedural delay down to minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:53: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* If Doom Raiders Mission 6 ("Ziraj's Last Hunt") succeeded, Davil's final note to the party — the one listing every identified Splinter operative "for **Kolat Towers**" — has already done its work if **Kolat Towers** was run. Here, the contact's business is the gold itself. Davil states the Doom Raiders' position without apology: "We're not asking you to give us anything. We're telling you what we intend to ask for, once the vault is open." At Dread Lord renown, Davil proposes a 10% split of the recovered gold in exchange for concrete operational support: a distraction crew, a locksmith backup, and a wagon extraction team for the gold itself. If Skeemo Weirdbottle survived and Manshoon is Simulacrum Only, Skeemo is not with the Doom Raiders today — Tashlyn confirms, grimly, that he is at the vault working for the simulacrum, and will recognize the party on sight. At renown 10+, Tashlyn commits a four-person Doom Raiders team to the City of the Dead operation, coordinated on the same terms as **Kolat Towers** if that quest was run, or offered fresh if it wasn't.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:75: - The Converted Windmill map (**Faction Outposts**, Manshoon outpost) — the primary clue, a physical document showing the Brandath crypt-complex layout.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:78: - Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (**Kolat Towers**, area E9) — if the party secured the contents of the bloodlocked vault during the Kolat Towers raid, this document describes the Brandath Crypts approach and identifies the mausoleum by name. A party that completed **Kolat Towers** and extracted this intelligence enters **Vault of Dragons** already knowing the route.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:118: - **Sunlight.** The *daylight* spell suffices — its light counts as sunlight under 2024 rules, and at 3rd level it's well within reach of a 7th-level party. So does Manshoon's sunglobe, if he is Destroyed and the item was recovered from Kolat Towers. Absent either, twenty large steel mirrors (400 gp total) and several hours of careful alignment work will do.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:126: - Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (**Kolat Towers**, area E9) — the same document that names the Brandath Crypts includes a partial account of the vault-opening ceremony alongside a partially decoded Elvish command phrase. A party that extracted this intelligence in **Kolat Towers** enters Scene 3 with the ceremony's outline already in hand; the command phrase is usable, if incomplete, and reduces the work of sourcing the remaining details.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:194: | Manshoon's Splinter | Not Destroyed | See Manshoon's Three States below | Seize the gold and the Stone; recover the dragonstaff if possible |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:206: **Manshoon's Three States.** Reused directly from **Kolat Towers** Scene 6, and the single most consequential row in this table.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:209: - *Simulacrum Only.* Present but diminished — no spell slots above 5th level, no staff of power, capable of executing plans Manshoon taught it but not of adapting to a situation he never anticipated.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:210: - *Alive* (or **Kolat Towers** not run). Full strength: staff of power in hand, surviving lieutenants at his side, and — if he reached the vault first through his own intelligence network — the advantage of having had time to prepare a position rather than react to the party's arrival. This is the hardest **Vault of Dragons** configuration, and the default one if **Kolat Towers** was skipped.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:212: If more than one hostile faction is present simultaneously, they do not coordinate with each other — Manshoon, Xanathar's remnants, and an Active-Adversarial Cassalanter team are all here for the same gold and are as much a threat to one another as to the party. A party that recognizes this can let its enemies do some of the fighting for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:214: *Facing Manshoon (If Alive or Simulacrum).* He talks first, exactly as he did in **Kolat Towers** if that confrontation occurred. His question here is not "what do you want" — he already knows. It is closer to: "You've done all the work. Why not let someone experienced handle what comes next?" He is not bluffing about being willing to fight for the gold, but he would still rather not — a fight this public, this close to the city's attention, is exactly the kind of exposure he has spent years avoiding. A party that can make continued exposure feel more costly to him than walking away may end this without combat at all.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:218: *The Dragonstaff's Stakes.* Whoever holds the dragonstaff of Ahghairon after Aurinax's oath is discharged decides which dragons the ward admits, one touched creature at a time. This is not a minor detail to gloss past in Scene 6's gold logistics — it is the reason Jarlaxle wants it, the reason Manshoon would take it if he could, and the reason Dagult, whatever else he's lost, still believes he has some claim on it. If the party is deciding what to do with the staff, this is the moment that decision's stakes should be made explicit to them.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:272: - **Halaster notices.** Within days of the vault's opening, a cryptic message reaches the party — a flameskull messenger, a fragment of dream, a brief and unsettling projection. Halaster Blackcloak, who has taken a professional interest in Manshoon before, has now taken one in the party as well.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:292: | 3 (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire** — Kolat Towers skipped) | 22 | 6 |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:295: A party can only reach **Vault of Dragons** with at least three lair heists done, since all three Eyes (from **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**) are required to open the Vault — Kolat Towers carries no Eye and is never required for entry, only for the fourth heist slot that unlocks level 7 early. This is why the quest is written for a 6th-to-7th-level party rather than a fixed 7th — Scene 5's Faction Confrontation in particular should be run with the entering level in mind (see that scene's roster-assembly table), since a level 6 party that skipped Kolat Towers also faces a full-strength, unidentified Manshoon by default (**Kolat Towers** is what typically resolves that threat).
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:308: | 3 (Kolat Towers skipped) | 22 points (level 6) | 24 points | 7 |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:311: A party that skipped Kolat Towers still gains a level from completing **Vault of Dragons** — it just started one level lower, and stays one level lower, ending the campaign at 7th instead of 8th. Level 8 remains exclusive to the party that ran all four heists, but **Vault of Dragons**'s own climax is never worth zero.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:315: *Transition.* There is no further quest within Dragon Heist to transition into. What follows, if the table continues, is Undermountain. 8th level is this campaign's intended handoff point into it — the outcome for a party that did all four heists — but a party that skipped Kolat Towers descends at 7th instead, having traded the deeper start for whatever they gained by leaving Manshoon's fortress alone. Either is a valid place to begin Dungeon of the Mad Mage; the difference is a consequence of the party's own choices, not a gap to patch. The five Mad Mage bridge hooks in Scene 6 are the GM's starting toolkit for that transition — use as many or as few as the table wants, but do not feel obligated to use all five. A campaign can end cleanly here, with nothing left unresolved that the party didn't choose to leave open.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:322: - **Faction Outposts**, Scene 4 (Manshoon Outposts) — the Converted Windmill map, the primary clue to the Brandath Crypts' location.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:326: - **Kolat Towers** Scene 6 — "Manshoon operational?" flag and the full Manshoon Identity Reveal, both read directly in Scene 5.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:327: - **Notable Figures** — Existing profiles used directly: Aurinax, Renaer Neverember, Hlam, Sir Ambrose Everdawn, Laeral Silverhand, Mirt, Jalester Silvermane, Vajra Safahr, Savra Belabranta, Jarlaxle, Manshoon. New profile added: Dagult Neverember.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:339: **Xanathar's Lair** through **Kolat Towers** each tested a different genre and a different set of party capabilities — dungeon infiltration, social infiltration, caper improvisation, raid violence. **Vault of Dragons** is not a fifth genre in that sequence. It is the point where all four genres' consequences arrive at once. The party does not case the vault, because there is nothing left to case — they already know where it is and what guards it. What replaces the heist framework's dramatic engine (plan, execute, adapt) is accumulated consequence: every faction relationship, every renown level, every choice about who to trust and who to betray, all resolving simultaneously. A GM running **Vault of Dragons** should not look for a new structural trick. The trick was already played, nine times, across the quests that came before it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:347: Every prior lair heist ends its Scene 6 by setting one or two flags for later use. **Vault of Dragons** is the quest where all of them get read at once. The Faction-State Summary Table in Scene 1 exists so a GM does not have to flip back through four other documents to reconstruct what's true — it is meant to be filled in once, before the session, and referenced throughout. The table's most important function is making Scene 5's roster assembly mechanical rather than improvised: a GM who knows Manshoon is Alive, Xanathar is Wounded, and the Cassalanters are Active-Adversarial can build the vault confrontation directly from the Roster-Assembly Table without guessing at what "feels right."
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:365: **Xanathar's Lair** through **Kolat Towers** each calibrate their difficulty against a single lair's escalation tier — how aggressively the party moved through **Faction Outposts**'s outposts, essentially. **Vault of Dragons** has no equivalent fresh escalation clock, because it doesn't need one: the Faction-State Summary Table already carries every difficulty variable the campaign has generated across its entire length. A party that dismantled Manshoon's Splinter in **Kolat Towers**, redeemed the Cassalanters in **Cassalanter Villa**, and allied with Jarlaxle in **Sea Maidens Faire** arrives at Scene 5 facing, essentially, nobody. A party that skipped **Kolat Towers**, left the Cassalanters Active-Adversarial, and made an enemy of Bregan D'aerthe arrives facing all three at once. This is not a punishment mechanic bolted onto the finale — it is the entire campaign's accumulated weight, made visible in a single afternoon, which is exactly what a convergence quest should do.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:58: - The two Zhentarims: establish that in Waterdeep, "the Zhentarim" refers to two unrelated organizations. The Doom Raiders (Davil Starsong's cell) are criminals who present as legitimate businesspeople; they are a joinable player faction. Manshoon's splinter cell is an enemy faction. Characters who join the Doom Raiders are NOT working for the campaign's main antagonist. This distinction causes confusion if not established at session zero.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:106: | Scribe | Good | Information-network connections; Zhentarim employs scribes |
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:121: | Zhentarim Mercenary | Excellent | Direct tie to the Doom Raiders; the Two Zhentarims distinction makes this character's loyalty a live question |
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:184: **The Holder: Urstul Floxin (Manshoon's Zhentarim)**
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:185: One of Manshoon's most effective field lieutenants: methodical, personally dangerous, and not given to visible anger. His first approach to the character will be dressed as a business transaction — a reasonable offer for the package's return, no questions asked, the whole matter tidied away. He means this, up to a point. The offer becomes less reasonable each time they decline. Characters who return the package and survive the meeting will discover they've demonstrated exactly the competence Floxin prefers in reluctant assets. The relationship doesn't end with the exchange — it reorganizes around a new one.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:187: > **Warning: Two Zhentarims.** Floxin works for Manshoon, not the Doom Raiders. A character whose debt is held by Floxin is already in the cross-hairs of the campaign's villain faction, whether they know it or not. Davil Starsong's cell will not automatically help this character — they are a separate organization. Let the character navigate the distinction without the DM explaining it for them.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:241: A slender, elegantly dressed sun elf who serves as the public face of the Doom Raiders' Waterdeep operation. He is charming, cultured, and entirely transparent about being charming and cultured — which is its own form of deflection. Beneath the social performance, he's a genuine professional who believes the Doom Raiders' moderate approach to crime is both more profitable and more ethical than Manshoon's methods. His first formal ask of the character is small and explicitly acknowledged as such — he's calibrating them, not exploiting them, and he makes this clear. Characters who engage honestly will find the relationship evolving into something closer to a genuine working partnership. Characters who try to leverage the asset-running against him will discover that Davil has been planning for that move since the first note was dropped.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:316: - The Two Zhentarims note must appear here (not just in the contract section above): a player who wants to join "the Zhentarim" needs to understand they are choosing the Doom Raiders, not Manshoon's cell. These are distinct organizations with different contacts, methods, and moral postures.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:327: | Doom Raiders (Zhentarim) | Davil Starsong | Criminal network access, fences, safe houses, street intelligence | Jobs that keep the network profitable |

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:105: *Era 4 — The Founding of Chains (the Zhentarim's origins and fracture)*
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:106: - Manshoon of Zhentil Keep as the Zhentarim's founder: a mercantile conspiracy built into a criminal network spanning the Sword Coast; ambition on an organizational scale
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:107: - The simulacrum problem as the organization's defining condition: Manshoon has died and been replaced by his own simulacra multiple times; the Zhentarim that now operates across the Sword Coast is an organization that has outlasted its founder's coherent leadership and retained only his methods
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:108: - The fracture: Davil Starsong's cell separates from Manshoon's organization on explicitly pragmatic grounds — visible violence and criminal overreach shorten lifespans; a moderate operation presenting a legitimate face is more profitable and survivable; this is the Doom Raiders, now operating in Waterdeep as "the Zhentarim" that most of Waterdeep knows
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:109: - Manshoon's separate Waterdeep operation: the original philosophy, arrived later, read Neverember's ouster as political opportunity; Kolat Towers established quietly; whether the man in the towers is the original Manshoon or a simulacrum is a question the text leaves open until Arc I
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:133: - The convergence: all four factions move on Waterdeep in the same window — Manshoon's cell establishing Kolat Towers, Jarlaxle arriving with the Sea Maidens Faire, the Cassalanters accelerating their timeline, the Xanathar Guild responding to new pressure from competitors it hasn't yet fully identified
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:153: - **Front businesses**: Sea Maidens Faire (Bregan D'aerthe), Istrid Horn's moneylending (Doom Raiders), a Trades Ward importing company (Manshoon), a charitable hospital (Cassalanters)

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:218: ### Manshoon (Zhentarim Splinter Cell)
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:220: **Purpose:** Distinguishes Manshoon clearly from the Doom Raiders and gives the DM the tools to play the campaign's most intellectually dangerous villain — the one who is always further ahead than anyone believes.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:222: > **The Two Zhentarims — DM Note:** Manshoon's splinter cell and Davil Starsong's Doom Raiders are entirely separate organizations. They share a name and a history and nothing else. Never conflate them in NPC dialogue. See also: Ch. 1 Campaign Contract; Appendix C.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:228: | 1 | The Shadow | Acts I–II | Absent; only his lieutenants are visible; PCs fight "the Zhentarim" without knowing Manshoon is a distinct player |

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:251: 1. **Acquire the Stone of Golorr** — the Stone is a transformed aboleth encoded with ancient Weave knowledge; Manshoon means to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra; the vault's gold funds the process; method: Stone recovery → Weave absorption; has one Eye; contingency: destroy the vault rather than cede it; connects to Arcs D and I
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:253: 3. **The Simulacrum Insurance** — the simulacrum is not a backup; it is the primary survival strategy; defeating Manshoon in combat may defeat only his copy; connects to Arc I design requirement
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:255: **Key design note:** The simulacrum reveal needs to be timed carefully. Too early, and it deflates the Kolat Towers raid. Too late, and it feels like a cheat. Target: players discover it during the raid itself, as a revelation that changes how they approach the final confrontation.

Pre-reveal deflates the confrontation; post-reveal feels like a cheat. The discovery should happen during the fight itself.

campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:60: The campaign contains two distinct Zhentarim factions that must never be conflated:
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:62: - **The Doom Raiders** (Davil Starsong's cell): a player-joinable faction; presents a public face of legitimate business and moderate criminal enterprise; will not cooperate with Manshoon and does not acknowledge his cell as legitimate Zhentarim; found in Trollskull Alley and across the city as apparent professionals
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:63: - **Manshoon's Splinter Cell**: the villain faction; operates from Kolat Towers; the "Zhentarim" that conducts assassinations and uses mind-controlled servants; Manshoon views the Doom Raiders as deserters who stole his name
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:65: When PCs encounter "the Zhentarim" in any given scene, the DM must know which cell is present. These factions will not cooperate. They will actively undermine each other if their operations overlap. The distinction should become apparent to attentive PCs during Arc B.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:77: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim**: professional mercenary tactics; more willing to negotiate mid-fight; always more members waiting nearby than visible
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:144: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the PCs spend three sessions on Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon have not been idle. The DM should have a rough answer for what each faction did during that time if the PCs investigate.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:161: - **Manshoon's Splinter Cell:** Knows the Stone exists and what it unlocks; Urstul Floxin's team has been tracking Dalakhar; does not yet know the Stone reached the Gralhunds; Kolat Towers is established and operational
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:183: - Manshoon and Xanathar's Guild: active street-level conflict in the Dock Ward; Manshoon's operatives are probing Guild territory; Xanathar has issued a kill order on Manshoon (failed twice so far)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:185: - Jarlaxle and Manshoon: fragile non-interference; each considers the other the only genuine peer in the game; neither wants direct conflict because both would rather the other faction absorb the other factions' attention
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:194: - What a faction wants in exchange for standing down (Doom Raiders: information; Manshoon: an asset or a demonstration of usefulness; Cassalanters: discretion; Bregan D'aerthe: the political favor they've always wanted)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:218: | Zhentarim (Doom Raiders) | Davil Starsong's cell; criminal enterprise with a palatable face | Intelligence on Manshoon's operation; containment of Xanathar Guild activity; anything that weakens their rivals |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:228: PCs in different factions will have conflicting faction interests by design. The Lords' Alliance wants the gold returned; the Doom Raiders want Manshoon destroyed; Force Grey wants everything kept quiet; Bregan D'aerthe wants a political favor. This is the campaign's social texture. The DM should let faction loyalties create real pressure on individual PCs without forcing party fragmentation.
